# TrustyFile — Audit & Research Findings (raw)

**Date:** 2026-07-04  
**Source:** 19-agent workflow (9 codebase auditors + 9 local-technique researchers + 1 completeness critic).  
Synthesized into `../specs/2026-07-04-detection-improvements-design.md`. Raw JSON per agent in `raw/`.


---

## Part 1 — Codebase audits

### CODE AUDIT — app.py (Streamlit UI layer, 2487 lines) + tests/ directory (8 files, 3449 lines) of TrustyFile at /Users/edouard/Projects/trustyfile-main

**Checks:**
- **VirusTotal hash lookup (UI-only)** — app.py:23-71 sends the file's SHA256 (never the file) to VT API v3. status=malicious if (malicious+suspicious)>0, clean if 0, unknown on 404. Rendered as a red/green/gray pill (app.py:1104-1135). No Flag object, no severity, does not affect the trust score. Silently disabled when no VIRUSTOTAL_API_KEY secret.
  - _quality:_ Naive: counts a single engine's 'suspicious' verdict as malicious; `except Exception: return None` (app.py:70-71) hides auth/rate-limit errors; re-queried on every Streamlit rerun (page navigation, language toggle) with a 10s blocking timeout because the result is not cached in session_state.
- **File size gate** — app.py:822-844: reject uploads > 10MB with a friendly error page and st.stop(). Hardcoded threshold, no severity (hard reject).
  - _quality:_ Sound but arbitrary; silently prevents analysis of large legitimate documents (scanned multi-page statements).
- **Page count gate** — app.py:846-870: fitz.open on bytes, reject > 20 pages. Hard reject.
  - _quality:_ fitz.open at line 848 has no try/except — a corrupted or renamed non-PDF file raises before any friendly error path, showing a raw traceback.
- **Passport detection to skip 2D-Doc scan** — app.py:899-907: reads identity module details['mrz_lines']; if first line startswith('P') → passport → skip scan_pdf_for_2d_doc. Otherwise runs the DataMatrix scan on every document (if pylibdmtx available).
  - _quality:_ Brittle: depends on identity module exposing raw mrz_lines and on OCR getting the first character right; a single OCR error flips the decision.
- **2D-Doc decode + 'Verified' panel** — app.py:906-909 + 2061-2213: decoded 2D-Doc barcodes rendered in a green always-open panel with a 'Verified' pill, listing identity/tax/invoice fields for manual comparison.
  - _quality:_ Misleading: twod_doc.py parses the signature bytes (twod_doc.py:611-640) but never cryptographically verifies them against ANTS certificates, so 'Verified' really means 'decoded'. An attacker can regenerate a DataMatrix with altered data and get the green panel.
- **Modification-date red pill** — app.py:1020-1029: if |ModDate - CreationDate| > 86400s (1 day, hardcoded), a red '{days} days' pill is shown next to the modification date. UI-only detection, produces no Flag, bypasses scoring.
  - _quality:_ Duplicates and contradicts module logic: metadata/structure modules use a 2s tolerance and scoring.py:280-303 deliberately downgrades <30min scanner-pipeline criticals to 'low' for identity docs — but the UI pill still screams red using its own threshold.
- **Stated-date vs creation-date red pill** — app.py:984-990 + 1031-1039: extracts dates from page 1 only via content.extract_dates_from_text, takes the FIRST date typed 'document'/'invoice' as the stated date; if |stated - CreationDate| > 7 days (hardcoded) shows a red pill.
  - _quality:_ Analysis logic hiding in the UI; picks the first extracted date (extraction order, not layout order); 7-day threshold flags the extremely common legitimate case of re-downloading a document weeks after its stated date.
- **Editor detection from modification history** — app.py:1008-1014: parses the literal substring '→ modified with ' out of get_modification_history()'s last diff to_tool string to display an 'Editor' row.
  - _quality:_ Magic-string coupling to structure.py:356 formatting; any wording change silently removes the Editor row. Should be a structured field.
- **Signature display** — app.py:973-979: scans structure-module flags for a details dict containing 'signer'; displays 'Signed by: <name>' or 'None'.
  - _quality:_ Fragile convention (key presence in an arbitrary flag's details); takes the first match only; unsigned docs show 'None' with no distinction between 'no signature' and 'signature check unavailable'.
- **Grouped score bars (4 categories)** — app.py:1196-1263: recomputes group scores — Modifications=[structure], Consistency=[content], Tampering=[images,fonts,visual,metadata], Authenticity=[external] — as sum(score*weight*confidence)/sum(weight*confidence) using MODULE_WEIGHTS, colored green>=80 / yellow>=50 / red<50. N/A when no member module ran.
  - _quality:_ Two defects: (1) ignores scoring.py CONFIDENCE_MULTIPLIERS (document-type adjustment), so bars contradict the headline score for identity/technical/scientific docs; (2) the identity (weight 1.5), forensics and twod_doc modules belong to NO group — for an ID card the most important module is invisible in the bars.
- **Trust gauge + risk label** — app.py:1149-1194: SVG donut, color thresholds 80/50/20 hardcoded (duplicating scoring.get_risk_level), label 'Trusted' if score >= 95 else risk level.
  - _quality:_ Threshold duplication risks drift; the 20-49 and 0-19 bands render the same red so HIGH vs CRITICAL is indistinguishable by color.
- **Flag severity pills + summary + Detected Issues list** — app.py:1265-1346 and 1450-1519: counts flags by severity, shows verdict from generate_rich_summary, lists every flag twice (summary block and expandable Detected Issues with code + details key/values). Secondary-type banner for scientific/legal_notice/technical/unknown (app.py:1324-1332).
  - _quality:_ Rendering is fine and flag content is html-escaped here (unlike file-properties rows); duplication of the full flag list is by design but wastes vertical space.
- **Modification history timeline** — app.py:1349-1448 (compact) and 1526-1638 (detailed): shown when structure.get_modification_history reports version_count > 1; per-version text diffs (+/- lines, 5-line cap in compact view), object-change badges, timing only for the final version computed from CreationDate/ModDate.
  - _quality:_ Timing for intermediate versions is 'unknown'; entire feature depends on incremental updates surviving in the file — a full-rewrite edit shows nothing; per-version times rely on attacker-writable metadata.
- **Entity extraction and grouping (UI-layer analysis)** — app.py:1747-1951: re-extracts SIRET/SIREN/VAT/RCS from full text, groups unverified identifiers by SIREN (VAT→SIREN via vat[4:] when len==13; RCS→first 9 digits), displays verified companies (INSEE, Active/Closed pill) and 'Not verified' cards. Company name found via regex requiring ALL-CAPS name + legal form (SAS|SARL|EURL|SASU|SCI|SNC|SCOP|SELARL|SEL|SELAFA); address via street/postal-code regex.
  - _quality:_ Substantial detection logic living in the UI; the single extracted name/address is applied to EVERY unverified SIREN card (app.py:1907-1912) — on a two-party invoice the buyer's SIREN is labeled with the seller's name, which looks like fraud; name regex misses SA, SASU lowercase, mixed-case names, non-French forms.
- **IBAN panel** — app.py:1953-2059: shows API-verified IBANs (valid/invalid pill, BIC, bank, country) plus IBANs found by content.extract_iban with a checksum-valid/invalid note and 'Not verified' pill. IBAN API only enabled when IBAN_API_KEY secret exists (app.py:143-149).
  - _quality:_ Informational only — an invalid checksum in the text-extracted path is displayed but produces no visible severity escalation in this panel; feature silently absent without the key.
- **MRZ panel (identity docs)** — app.py:2215-2468: shows MRZ fields, age computed as days//365, expiry VALID/EXPIRED badge vs today, header badge suspect(red)/warning(orange)/valid(green) derived from identity-module flag severities, coherence checks (doc type prefix, issuer format, validity period 5-10y TD3 / 10-15y TD1), and scan-authenticity rows (ELA suspicious-ratio %, noise CV uniformity).
  - _quality:_ Pure display of module output — reasonable; age via //365 drifts on leap years (cosmetic); the panel is the only surface where identity results are visible since the module is excluded from group bars.
- **Analysis caching & temp-file lifecycle** — app.py:872-927: analysis runs once per file (md5 of bytes as session key); results, summary, 2D-Doc scan and tmp path cached in session_state; old tmp file deleted when a new file is uploaded.
  - _quality:_ extract_pdf_data + get_modification_history (app.py:968-970) and check_virustotal are NOT cached and re-run on every rerun — if the OCR fallback triggers this is multi-second per UI interaction; tmp files leak at session end (only deleted on next upload); md5 used for cache key (fine for caching, but inconsistent with the SHA256 shown).
- **Tests: content module (test_content.py, 796 lines)** — Broad unit coverage of pure functions: French/numeric/abbreviated date parsing, date-type identification, impossible dates (CONTENT_FAR_FUTURE_DATE critical >1y future; CONTENT_VERY_OLD_DATE medium <2000), anachronisms (service/order after invoice, due before invoice = high, 1-day tolerance), future invoice date (critical), amounts (>=1.0 filter), reference date patterns, SIRET/SIREN Luhn, FR VAT check digits, SIREN-VAT mismatch (critical), missing company ID (medium), analyze_content scoring integration.
  - _quality:_ Best test file, but drifting: test_service_after_invoice_flagged and test_missing_company_id_flagged now FAIL against the implementation; extract_invoice_reference, extract_all_invoice_references, check_reference_date_match, check_reference_consistency are imported (lines 33-37) and never tested; TestCheckDuplicateAmounts only asserts a disabled check returns [] (documents dead code).
- **Tests: metadata module (test_metadata.py, 457 lines)** — check_producer three-tier detection (AI tools → critical META_AI_GENERATED; iLovePDF-class converters → high META_ONLINE_CONVERTER; Nitro/LibreOffice/Google Docs → medium META_SUSPICIOUS_PRODUCER; substring matching, case-insensitive, single flag priority), check_dates (future creation critical with 1-day tolerance, mod>creation+2s critical META_DOCUMENT_MODIFIED, mod<creation high META_IMPOSSIBLE_DATES), check_missing_metadata (all empty → medium META_NO_METADATA; no producer/creator → low META_NO_PRODUCER), confidence tiers 1.0/0.7/0.3.
  - _quality:_ Good coverage of pure logic; one FAILING test (canva now classified META_IMAGE_EDITOR, tests expect META_ONLINE_CONVERTER) shows the producer taxonomy drifted; no test loads producer strings from a real PDF.
- **Tests: fonts module (test_fonts.py, 289 lines)** — extract_base_font_name subset parsing (6 uppercase letters + '+'), diversity thresholds (>7 families medium FONTS_HIGH_DIVERSITY, >10 high FONTS_EXCESSIVE_DIVERSITY, bold/italic collapse to one family), system fonts low flag (Arial/Calibri/etc.), non-embedded non-standard fonts low flag, mixed subset/non-subset same base name low FONTS_MIXED_SUBSETS.
  - _quality:_ Pure-function only; nothing tests font extraction from an actual PDF, and the flagship CLAUDE.md check 'font change mid-line/mid-word' has no test (and possibly no implementation exercised anywhere).
- **Tests: images module (test_images.py, 497 lines)** — check_screenshot_dimensions (known screen resolutions ± tolerance, rotated, high IMAGES_SCREENSHOT_DETECTED), resolution mismatch (>2.5x DPI ratio medium), heavy JPEG compression ratio, excessive image count per page, no-images low flag, image-only/mostly-image PDF (>=80% page coverage + <200 chars/page medium), find_amount_regions on synthetic PDFs, check_paste_artifacts negative paths only (digital PDF, no amounts, missing file, image-only).
  - _quality:_ Two FAILING tests (screenshot tolerance and compression threshold changed in implementation); the paste-artifact detector — the project's marquee tampering check — has ZERO positive tests, so it could detect nothing and the suite would stay green.
- **Tests: visual module (test_visual.py, 271 lines)** — Pure QR analysis: URL shorteners high VISUAL_QR_URL_SHORTENER, suspicious TLDs medium, domain mismatch vs expected_domains critical, non-URL ignored; watermarks (SPECIMEN/VOID/CANCELLED high, COPY/DRAFT/DUPLICATE/SAMPLE medium, TEST/CONFIDENTIAL low, French variants mapped); converter watermarks ('created with', 'ilovepdf' high, trial/evaluation); sender-domain extraction from emails/URLs with social-media filtering.
  - _quality:_ All tests exercise code that never runs in production for QR: visual.py:33 hardcodes PYZBAR_AVAILABLE=False, so QR extraction is dead and app.py's enable_qr=True (line 138) is a no-op. Watermark checks are text-substring based — no test for the word COPY appearing innocently ('copy of your contract' would flag).
- **Tests: structure module (test_structure.py, 242 lines)** — count_incremental_updates (1 EOF for clean PDF; missing file → 1 'assume clean'), check_incremental_updates/javascript/hidden_annotations/acroform/object_streams on clean PDFs, embedded file high STRUCT_EMBEDDED_FILES positive test, visible annotation not flagged, trusted-signature raises deleted-object threshold.
  - _quality:_ Weakest coverage relative to module size (63KB): almost exclusively negative/happy paths — no fixture with an actual incremental update (fitz saveIncr would do it), no JS-bearing PDF, no AcroForm, no hidden annotation, no ghost objects; get_modification_history (used directly by app.py) untested; signature verification untested.
- **Tests: scoring (test_scoring.py, 402 lines)** — Risk thresholds 80/50/20, confidence-weighted average math, empty→100, zero-confidence ignored, clamping, flag collection/sorting/counting, critical-flag caps (1 critical → score<=49 + at least HIGH; 2 criticals → <=19 CRITICAL), summary keyword checks.
  - _quality:_ Math tests are solid but cap assertions drifted: implementation caps at 40 minus 5/high and 2/medium with floor 5 (scoring.py:311-318) while tests assert <=49 (too loose to catch changes); no test at all for CONFIDENCE_MULTIPLIERS, the _skip_caps_types cap bypass, or the identity scanner-pipeline critical downgrade (scoring.py:270-303) — the most intricate and risk-bearing logic in scoring.
- **Tests: identity module (test_identity_us.py, 495 lines)** — US-only MRZ coherence: TD1 'IP'+USA passport card (subtype, no flags), IP prefix with non-USA issuer → high MRZ_US_PASSPORT_CARD_ISSUER_MISMATCH with expected/found details, TD3 US passport validity (adult 10y / minor 5y ±1.5y, low MRZ_US_VALIDITY_PERIOD_UNEXPECTED, skipped without issuance_date), constants.
  - _quality:_ Well-built helpers with real ICAO check digits, but covers only the US corner of a 60KB module: no French CNI/TD1, no TD3 checksum-failure tests, no OCR extraction, no photo/ELA/noise analysis tests, no full analyze() integration.
- **Modules and layers with ZERO test coverage** — external.py (30KB: INSEE SIRET, VAT, IBAN API logic), twod_doc.py (70KB — the largest module: DataMatrix decode, C40 parsing, field mapping, cross-check against PDF text), forensics.py (17KB ELA/noise), summary.py (39KB verdict/bullet generation), document_type.py (classifier that selects confidence multipliers AND decides whether critical-flag caps apply), analyzer.py (orchestrator), extractors/pdf_extractor.py + ocr.py, i18n, and app.py itself.
  - _quality:_ The highest-leverage untested code path is document_type.py → scoring caps: a classifier regression that labels invoices 'technical' silently disables the critical-flag score caps with no failing test.

**Weaknesses:**
- app.py:929 + 2474-2477 — the giant results block is wrapped in `try: ... finally: pass` with NO except clause; the comment at app.py:918-921 says a failed analysis 're-raises so the outer handler catches it' but no outer handler exists, so any module/extraction exception renders a raw Streamlit traceback to the end user.
- app.py:1002, 1057, 1062-1070, 1076-1082, 1089, 1096 — attacker-controlled PDF metadata (producer, creator, editor_name, signer) and the uploaded filename are interpolated into st.markdown(..., unsafe_allow_html=True) WITHOUT html.escape, while flag messages elsewhere are carefully escaped. A hostile PDF with Producer='<img src=x onerror=...>' injects HTML/JS into the page (stored-in-metadata XSS).
- app.py:848 — `_fitz.open(stream=file_bytes, filetype='pdf')` has no error handling; a corrupted or renamed non-PDF upload crashes before the friendly size/page error paths.
- app.py:70-71 — check_virustotal swallows every exception (network, auth, JSON shape) and returns None, indistinguishable from 'no API key'; the VT row silently disappears with no diagnostics.
- app.py:1105 — check_virustotal is called on every Streamlit rerun (page selector change, language toggle) with a 10s timeout; result is not cached in session_state despite the analysis being cached.
- app.py:968-970 — extract_pdf_data and get_modification_history re-run full text extraction on every rerun; if the PDF has no text layer the OCR fallback (2-5s/page) fires on every UI interaction.
- app.py:904 — passport detection is `mrz_lines[0].startswith('P')` on OCR output stored in another module's details dict; a one-character OCR error flips whether the 2D-Doc scan runs.
- app.py:1008-1014 — editor detection string-parses '→ modified with ' out of structure.py:356's human-readable formatting; renaming that string silently kills the Editor row (magic-string coupling across layers).
- app.py:1020-1039 — two red-pill detections live only in the UI with their own hardcoded thresholds (1 day for ModDate gap, 7 days for stated-date gap) that contradict module logic (metadata uses 2s tolerance; scoring.py:280-303 downgrades <30min scanner-pipeline criticals for identity docs, yet the pill still shows red).
- app.py:1231-1239 — group score bars use MODULE_WEIGHTS × confidence but ignore scoring.py CONFIDENCE_MULTIPLIERS (scoring.py:60-100), so per-group bars can contradict the headline trust score for identity/technical/scientific documents.
- app.py:1203-1208 — the group definitions omit the identity (weight 1.5), forensics, and twod_doc modules entirely; for an ID document the most heavily weighted module is invisible in the score bars.
- app.py:1907-1912 — one globally extracted entity_name/entity_address is stamped onto EVERY unverified SIREN card; a normal two-party invoice shows the same company name/address for both parties (looks like an identity mismatch).
- app.py:1868-1869 — entity-name regex requires ALL-CAPS names followed by a hardcoded list of French legal forms; misses 'SA', mixed-case names, and all non-French forms; app.py:1846 drops any VAT whose length != 13.
- app.py:2200-2213 — green always-open '2D-Doc Verified' panel is shown for any successfully DECODED barcode; twod_doc.py parses but never cryptographically verifies the signature (twod_doc.py:611-640, no certificate-chain code anywhere in the module), so 'Verified' overstates what was checked.
- app.py:137-138 — enable_external=True and enable_qr=True are hardcoded ('sidebar removed'); enable_qr is dead configuration because visual.py:33 hardcodes PYZBAR_AVAILABLE=False, so the entire tested QR pipeline never executes in production.
- app.py:62 — VT 'suspicious' verdicts are summed into 'malicious', so one paranoid engine turns the pill red on a clean file.
- app.py:880-882 + 2474-2477 — temp files persist for the whole session and are only deleted when a NEW file is uploaded; abandoned sessions leak temp files on the small cloud host.
- app.py:990 — 'stated date' is the FIRST extracted invoice/document-type date on page 1 (extraction order, not layout order); mis-picking feeds a red pill directly.
- Test suite drift: 5 tests currently FAIL against the implementation (test_content.py::test_service_after_invoice_flagged, test_content.py::test_missing_company_id_flagged, test_images.py::test_within_tolerance, test_images.py::test_heavy_compression_flagged, test_metadata.py canva classification) — the encoded detection spec no longer matches shipped behavior and there is evidently no CI gate.
- tests/test_scoring.py:287-311 — docstring/assertions describe a cap at 49 while scoring.py:311-318 caps at 40 with -5/high and -2/medium down to floor 5; the loose `<= 49` assertion cannot catch regressions in the actual cap math.
- tests/test_content.py:33-37 — extract_invoice_reference, extract_all_invoice_references, check_reference_date_match, check_reference_consistency are imported and never tested; tests/test_content.py:546-554 (TestCheckDuplicateAmounts) is a vacuous test asserting a disabled check returns [].
- tests/test_structure.py — only clean-PDF negative paths plus one embedded-file positive; no incremental-update fixture (trivially creatable with fitz saveIncr), no JavaScript, no AcroForm, no hidden-annotation, no ghost-object fixture; get_modification_history (consumed directly by app.py) is untested.
- tests/test_images.py::TestCheckPasteArtifacts — the flagship paste/tamper detector has ONLY negative-path tests; if its positive detection silently broke, no test would notice.
- No tests exist for external.py, twod_doc.py (70KB), forensics.py, summary.py, document_type.py, analyzer.py, pdf_extractor.py, ocr.py, or app.py — including document_type.py whose output selects confidence multipliers and decides whether critical-flag score caps apply (scoring.py:263-305).
- app.py:1155-1162 and 1241-1248 — score color thresholds (80/50/20) are duplicated in the UI instead of reusing scoring.get_risk_level; HIGH and CRITICAL bands both render the same red.

**False Positive Risks:**
- Re-downloaded duplicates: a customer fetches a duplicate invoice or bank statement from a portal weeks after the stated document date — the PDF's CreationDate is the download time, so the |stated - creation| > 7 days rule (app.py:1031-1039) puts an alarming red 'N days' pill on a perfectly legitimate document. This is the normal case for bank statements and utility-bill duplicates.
- Viewer re-saves: a legitimate recipient opens a real invoice in Adobe/Preview and saves it (or a scanner writes the file and a viewer touches it) days later — ModDate gap > 1 day triggers the red modification pill (app.py:1020-1029) even when scoring.py:280-303 deliberately treats the equivalent module flag as a scanner-pipeline false positive; UI and engine contradict each other.
- Two-party invoices: because one extracted company name/address is applied to every unverified SIREN card (app.py:1907-1912), a legitimate invoice listing both seller and buyer SIRENs shows the buyer's SIREN labeled with the seller's name — users read this as an identity mismatch/fraud signal.
- Scanned identity documents: the 'Tampering' group bar averages fonts/images/visual/metadata WITHOUT the document-type confidence multipliers (app.py:1231-1239), so an authentic scanned CNI can show a red Tampering bar while the headline score (which applies the 0.3 fonts multiplier) says the document is fine.
- VirusTotal 'suspicious' verdicts: one aggressive engine marking a clean corporate PDF 'suspicious' renders the red 'threats detected' pill (app.py:62,1115-1121), implying malware on a legitimate document.
- SME documents produced with consumer tools: per the tested taxonomy (test_metadata.py, test_fonts.py), Google Docs/LibreOffice producers get medium flags and Calibri/Arial gets a system-font flag — a small business's genuine Word invoice accumulates penalties from tooling alone; a Microsoft-ecosystem producer string containing 'Copilot' would even hit the critical AI list.
- Innocent watermark words: check_watermarks is substring-based (test_visual.py:158-200), so a legitimate letter containing 'please keep a copy for your records' or a 'TEST' environment reference can be flagged, and 'copie' appears routinely in French administrative text.
- Legitimate long documents: hard gates reject > 10MB or > 20 pages (app.py:822-870) — real annual bank statements and notarial files simply cannot be analyzed, which users may misread as rejection for cause.
- Documents with genuine 2D-Doc on passports: the UI skips 2D-Doc scanning whenever MRZ line 1 starts with 'P' (app.py:904) — if a legitimate French document is misread as a passport, its authentic 2D-Doc positive signal is never collected, depriving the doc of a trust boost.

**False Negative Gaps:**
- QR phishing is entirely undetected in production: visual.py:33 hardcodes PYZBAR_AVAILABLE=False, so QR extraction never runs — every tested check (URL shorteners, suspicious TLDs, domain mismatch vs sender) is dead code in deployment despite app.py:138 setting enable_qr=True. An invoice with a phishing payment QR passes clean.
- Forged 2D-Doc: since the barcode's cryptographic signature is never verified, an attacker can regenerate a DataMatrix encoding altered income/tax/identity values matching their edited text — the UI then shows an authoritative green 'Verified' panel that actively legitimizes the forgery (worse than no check).
- Full-rewrite tampering: editing a PDF with any tool that rewrites the file (LibreOffice re-export, print-to-PDF, qpdf --linearize, many online editors) leaves a single %%EOF and lets the attacker set ModDate=CreationDate — the modification-history panel, both red date pills, and the entire structure group show nothing. Only producer-string heuristics remain, and those are trivially spoofed with exiftool by setting Producer='Adobe PDF Library 15.0' (explicitly whitelisted in test_metadata.py:157-165).
- Metadata forgery generally: all date-based UI detections (pills, history timing, version timeline) read attacker-writable Info-dictionary fields; a fraudster who sets coherent CreationDate/ModDate/stated date suppresses every date signal surfaced in app.py.
- Passport-labelled evasion of 2D-Doc cross-check: crafting MRZ line 1 to start with 'P' (app.py:904) skips the 2D-Doc scan, so a doctored CNI/tax document presented with a passport-style MRZ never has its barcode decoded and cross-checked against visible text.
- Second-page tampering: the stated-date extraction reads page 1 only (app.py:987), so manipulated dates or amounts on later pages never feed the pill logic; similarly the amount-region paste detector's effectiveness is unproven — it has zero positive-path tests, so a regression that makes it detect nothing would ship green.
- Classifier-driven cap bypass: document_type.py is untested; if a fraudulent invoice is classified 'technical'/'scientific'/'legal_notice', scoring.py:265 skips the critical-flag score caps AND applies confidence multipliers that mute structure/content findings — an attacker adding academic-looking boilerplate could materially raise their score, and no test guards this path.
- Untested verification modules: external.py (INSEE/VAT/IBAN) and twod_doc.py cross-checking have no tests — silent regressions there (e.g., an API schema change making every SIRET 'unverifiable') would quietly remove the strongest positive/negative signals with the suite still green.
- VirusTotal scope confusion: the VT pill only detects known malware hashes; a fraudulent-but-not-malicious invoice reliably shows the neutral/green states, which users can misread as document authenticity.
- Anachronism regression already shipped: test_content.py::test_service_after_invoice_flagged currently FAILS — service-date-after-invoice-date (a classic fabricated-invoice tell that the tests document as 'the most valuable date check') is apparently no longer flagged by the implementation, and likewise CONTENT_MISSING_COMPANY_ID no longer fires for French invoices with no SIRET/SIREN/VAT.

**Improvement Ideas:**
- Move every UI-layer detection into modules that emit Flag objects — the two red date pills, editor extraction, entity name/address extraction, and VT lookup — so severity, i18n, scoring, and display stay consistent and the engine/UI contradictions (scanner-pipeline downgrade vs red pill) disappear. app.py should only render flags.
- Extract a shared `effective_weight(module, confidence, document_type)` helper in scoring.py and use it for both calculate_final_score and the app.py group bars; add identity/forensics/twod_doc to a group (e.g., 'Identity & Signatures') so their results are visible.
- Escape ALL attacker-controlled strings (producer, creator, signer, editor_name, uploaded filename, API-returned BIC/bank names) before interpolation into unsafe_allow_html markdown — a single esc() helper applied at the file-properties block (app.py:1002-1102) closes the hostile-PDF XSS.
- Add a proper except-handler around the analysis/results block rendering the same friendly error card as the size/page gates, and wrap the initial fitz.open at app.py:848 to handle corrupted uploads.
- Implement offline 2D-Doc signature verification: bundle the ANTS/France Titres certificates (fully local, satisfies the no-external-upload constraint) and verify the parsed signature; rename the pill to 'Decoded — signature not verified' until then, because the current green 'Verified' is the single most dangerous overstatement in the UI.
- Cache extract_pdf_data, get_modification_history, and check_virustotal results in session_state keyed by the same file hash as the analysis, eliminating per-rerun OCR/API costs; delete temp files via a session-end hook or analyze from bytes (fitz supports streams) to remove temp files entirely.
- Fix the 5 failing tests to re-encode intended behavior (or fix regressions they reveal — especially the service-after-invoice anachronism and missing-company-ID checks) and add CI so spec drift can't ship; tighten test_scoring cap assertions to the real 40/−5/−2/floor-5 math.
- Add positive-path structure fixtures: fitz saveIncr() for incremental updates, a JS action, an AcroForm text widget, an opacity-0 annotation, and a ghost-object file; add tests for get_modification_history since app.py consumes it directly.
- Test the untested decision-critical code first: document_type.py classification (it gates the critical-flag caps), scoring's CONFIDENCE_MULTIPLIERS/_skip_caps_types/identity scanner-pipeline downgrade, twod_doc parsing + cross-check, external.py response handling (mock the APIs), and at least one positive paste-artifact fixture (synthetic scan with a pasted amount region, matching CLAUDE.md's own 'Test Document Ideas' list).
- Replace the '→ modified with ' magic-string contract with a structured `detected_editor` field in get_modification_history diffs; replace the 'signer-in-flag-details' convention with a first-class field on the structure ModuleResult.details.
- Either ship pyzbar (libzbar0 via packages.txt already exists as a mechanism) and add an availability-gated end-to-end QR extraction test, or delete the enable_qr plumbing — right now the tested QR suite gives false confidence about a feature that never runs.
- Per-entity attribution: extract name/address from a ±300-char context window around each SIREN occurrence instead of one global regex pass, so multi-party documents label each entity correctly; broaden legal-form patterns (SA, mixed case) and support non-13-length VAT forms.
- Make the VT pill three-state with nuance: treat suspicious-only verdicts (or <2 engines) as amber 'inconclusive', cache responses, and add tooltip text clarifying VT checks malware, not document authenticity.
- Surface the cap logic in the UI: when a critical flag caps the score, show 'score limited by a critical finding' next to the gauge — currently the gauge can show 40 while all four group bars are green, which confuses users and hides the engine's reasoning.
- Differentiate HIGH vs CRITICAL visually (distinct colors below 50) and derive gauge/bar colors from get_risk_level instead of re-hardcoding thresholds in three places in app.py.

---

### CODE AUDIT — Identity documents (MRZ/ICAO 9303), French 2D-DOC barcodes, and OCR extraction — src/modules/identity.py, src/modules/twod_doc.py, src/extractors/ocr.py

**Checks:**
- **Identity pre-detection by keywords (_has_identity_keywords)** — identity.py:94-125. Scans extracted text for 1 match among FR/EN ID keywords ('passeport', 'passport', 'carte nationale', 'machine readable zone', '<<<<<' ...). Gates whether the identity module runs on 'unknown' docs (analyzer.py:157-167). No severity — routing only.
  - _quality:_ Reasonable as a cheap gate; single-match rule is deliberately permissive. '<<<<<' can match non-ID content (git conflict markers, ASCII art). A false positive also force-overrides doc_type to 'identity' in the analyzer, changing scoring multipliers for the whole document.
- **Face-photo detection (_has_face_photo)** — identity.py:128-210. Renders first 2 pages at 72 DPI grayscale, OpenCV Haar frontal-face cascade (scaleFactor=1.3, minNeighbors=3, minSize=20x20). Only used when the PDF has no text layer; a hit classifies the doc as an identity document.
  - _quality:_ Naive but scoped: only runs on text-less scans. Haar at 72 DPI with minNeighbors=3 fires on any portrait (newspaper scan, flyer). Silent disable if cascade file missing (identity.py:163-165). The consequence is doc-type override, not a flag, so damage is indirect (wrong confidence multipliers/caps applied).
- **MRZ detection via OCR (ocr_extract_mrz)** — ocr.py:219-371. Renders bottom 20% of first 2 pages at 120 DPI, preprocess (grayscale, contrast x1.5, sharpen), Tesseract psm 6 with [A-Z0-9<] whitelist, spaces replaced by '<'. Looks for 2 consecutive 44-char lines starting with 'P' (TD3) or 3x30 lines starting with I/A/C (TD1); sliding-window trimming tolerates up to +6 junk chars; global-buffer regex fallback for concatenated lines. Returns lines + page or None.
  - _quality:_ Well-commented and pragmatic, but brittle: single y_ratio=0.80 assumes the card fills the page; no rotation/deskew handling; tolerance is asymmetric (extra chars OK, dropped chars fatal); buffer fallback can lock onto a shifted 88-char window (any 'P' one position off) yielding misaligned fields that are then shown to the user as extracted identity data.
- **Structure-only MRZ validation (_validate_mrz_structure_only) — the ONLY validation that runs in production** — identity.py:791-904. Flags: MRZ_INVALID_SEX (medium) if sex not in {M,F,<}; MRZ_DOB_IMPOSSIBLE (high) if computed age <0 or >120; MRZ_DOCUMENT_EXPIRED (medium) if expiry < today. Deliberately skips ICAO checksums because OCR errors would cause false CRITICALs.
  - _quality:_ The rationale (OCR noise) is sound, but the outcome is that the module named 'MRZ check digit validation (cryptographically reliable)' in scoring.py:34 never validates a single check digit. Age uses days//365 (leap drift) and threshold 120 vs 130 elsewhere. A single misread DOB digit still yields a -25 HIGH flag despite the anti-OCR-noise philosophy.
- **Cross-field MRZ coherence (_check_mrz_coherence)** — identity.py:911-1235. Flags (low/medium only, plus one high): MRZ_DOC_TYPE_UNEXPECTED (low; TD3 must start 'P', TD1 must start I/A); MRZ_PASSPORT_TYPE_UNUSUAL (low; 2nd char not </O/D); MRZ_ISSUER_FORMAT_INVALID (medium; issuer not 1-3 letters); MRZ_US_PASSPORT_CARD_ISSUER_MISMATCH (high; 'IP' prefix with issuer != USA); MRZ_CNI_TYPE_UNEXPECTED (low; FRA TD1 prefix must be 'IR'); MRZ_CNI_OPTIONAL2_NOT_EMPTY (low; FRA line2[18:29] must be all '<'); MRZ_US_VALIDITY_PERIOD_UNEXPECTED (low; 10y adult/5y minor ±1.5y — needs issuance_date); MRZ_NATIONALITY_FORMAT_INVALID (low); MRZ_DOC_NUMBER_FORMAT_INVALID (low); MRZ_VALIDITY_PERIOD_UNUSUAL (low; 1-20y — needs issuance_date); MRZ_NAME_FORMAT_INVALID (low; no '<<').
  - _quality:_ Good instinct (broad, OCR-tolerant checks) but two checks are permanently disabled because analyze_identity hardcodes issuance_date=None (identity.py:1428) while the function that could supply it (ocr_extract_issuance_date) is never called. The hardcoded 'IR' expectation for French CNI is factually dubious (post-2021 CNIe MRZ starts 'IDFRA'), and TD1 allowed prefixes (I/A) disagree with ocr.py's I/A/C.
- **ICAO 9303 checksum validation (validate_td3 / validate_td1) — DEAD CODE** — identity.py:472-623 and 630-784. Verifies check digits with 7-3-1 weighting: MRZ_DOC_NUMBER_CHECK_FAIL (critical), MRZ_DOB_CHECK_FAIL (critical), MRZ_EXPIRY_CHECK_FAIL (critical), MRZ_OPTIONAL_CHECK_FAIL (high, TD3), MRZ_FINAL_CHECK_FAIL (critical), plus age>130 (high) and expiry (medium). Never invoked by analyze_identity or anything else in production; text-layer extraction (extract_mrz_lines, identity.py:404-465) is also dead.
  - _quality:_ The algorithm (mrz_check_digit, identity.py:297-337) is correct, but the TD1 composite at identity.py:770 omits line2[18:29] (optional data 2), which ICAO 9303 Part 5 includes — would produce false CRITICALs on genuine documents with non-empty optional2 if ever reconnected. The strongest tamper signal in the whole area is unreachable.
- **Identity module scoring** — identity.py:1441-1449. Start at 100; -25 per high, -10 per medium, -5 per low; floor 0. Confidence 0.65 with MRZ, 0.4 without. No MRZ found => score 100, zero flags (identity.py:1401-1411).
  - _quality:_ No 'critical' branch in the deduction loop — a critical flag would deduct 0 points (latent bug, becomes live if checksums are ever reconnected). Returning a perfect 100 when nothing could be verified rewards absence of evidence.
- **Scan authenticity: ELA + noise-grid (_analyze_scan_authenticity) — DEAD CODE** — identity.py:1242-1352. Renders page 1 at 200 DPI, Error Level Analysis via forensics module (suspicious pixel ratio > 8% => 'MEDIUM'), plus 8x8 Laplacian-variance grid with coefficient of variation > 2.0 => 'LOW'. Never called; analyze_identity hardcodes photo_analysis={'ela_run': False} at identity.py:1439.
  - _quality:_ Beyond being dead, the noise metric is conceptually flawed: Laplacian variance measures edges/detail, not sensor noise, so any document mixing text blocks and blank background exceeds CV 2.0 — the threshold is close to meaningless.
- **2D-DOC DataMatrix pre-filter (_has_datamatrix_candidate)** — twod_doc.py:731-774. On a 100 DPI render: binary threshold at gray<80, external contours, keep regions 60-300px per side, aspect 0.6-1.7, dark density 0.25-0.70. Decides whether the expensive pylibdmtx decode runs.
  - _quality:_ Sensible speed optimization but a hard gate on detection: low-contrast scans (barcode gray ~120), barcodes merged with adjacent dark elements (RETR_EXTERNAL bounding box > 300px), or small barcodes (<60px) silently skip decoding entirely.
- **2D-DOC scan (scan_pdf_for_2d_doc)** — twod_doc.py:777-886. Page 1 only; only top-left and top-right quadrants; decode at 200 DPI with pylibdmtx timeout=1500ms; keeps only payloads starting with 'DC'; stops after first hit.
  - _quality:_ Position assumptions are hardcoded: the 2D-DOC spec does not mandate top-of-page-1 placement (pay slips and some invoices place it elsewhere; multi-page PDFs with a cover letter miss it entirely). Fixed 1.5s timeout makes detection nondeterministic on busy pages. Broad except at 883-885 turns decode crashes into 'no 2D-DOC found'.
- **2D-DOC header/message parsing (parse_header, parse_message, parse_twod_doc)** — twod_doc.py:401-661. Parses versions 01-04, hex-encoded dates since 2000-01-01, doc type, perimeter, country; message zone parsed per DI registry (twod_doc.py:231-359) as fixed-length or GS/RS/US-terminated fields; signature stored raw after US.
  - _quality:_ Parsing is faithful to the spec for known DIs, but the registry is incomplete: an unknown DI that is actually fixed-length (no GS terminator) desynchronizes the parser and corrupts every subsequent field. No plausibility checks on header (future emission date, signature date before emission, CA/cert ID against the known ANTS registry).
- **2D-DOC verified-data extraction and display (extract_verified_data / app.py panel)** — twod_doc.py:994-1114 + app.py:2062-2205. Extracts name (DI 10/12+13/44 regex/6G/62/60), fiscal numbers (47/49), address (22/24/25), tax fields (4V/4X/41/43), invoice fields (18/1D), identity fields (65-6O, AC, E4); computes balance and an income range from tax; displayed to the user as 'SIGNÉES - ne peuvent pas être falsifiées' / 'Source of Truth'.
  - _quality:_ DANGEROUS: the ECDSA signature is never verified, no certificate chain, no CA whitelist — yet the UI asserts the data cannot be faked. float(x) if x.isdigit() (twod_doc.py:1061-1063) silently drops negative or decimal amounts. Purely informational: produces no Flags and has zero impact on the trust score.
- **2D-DOC vs visible-PDF comparison (verify_2d_doc_against_pdf) — DEAD CODE** — twod_doc.py:1448-1855 (plus verify_pdf_with_2d_doc 1858-1906, scan_image_for_2d_doc 889-931, format_verified_data 1117-1183 — all uncalled). Compares name/postal/city/street presence, amounts 4V/4X/41/43/1D, computed balance, fiscal numbers 47/49, invoice number 18 against the full PDF text; income-vs-tax coherence using hardcoded 2024 brackets (estimate_income_from_tax, twod_doc.py:1264-1305) flagging displayed income < 0.5x estimated min or > 2x max; outputs fraud_indicator strings (no Flag objects, no severities).
  - _quality:_ This is the module's raison d'être and it is unreachable. Even if reconnected: amount search matches the value ANYWHERE in the document; number regex (twod_doc.py:1436) merges digit runs across spaces ('Page 1 2024' -> 12024); no OCR fallback so scanned documents fail every comparison; brackets drift yearly; else-clause on the for loop at 1767 gives the positive-coherence path only when no too-low income broke out, but a too-high income appends a mismatch without breaking, so a later small legit number can still trigger the too-low CRITICAL.
- **Issuance-date OCR (ocr_extract_issuance_date) — DEAD CODE** — ocr.py:374-452. Full-page 150 DPI OCR (lang=fra), searches 'DELIVR|ISSU' keyword, date pattern within following 120 chars, validates day 1-31 / month 1-12 / year 1980-2035, returns ISO date.
  - _quality:_ Never called (identity.py:1425-1428 hardcodes issuance_date=None for a 3-4s time budget), which permanently disables the two validity-period checks that depend on it. Hardcoded year ceiling 2035 is a time bomb. Day validation (1-31) accepts Feb 31.

**Weaknesses:**
- Dead detection core #1: validate_td3 (identity.py:472), validate_td1 (identity.py:630) and extract_mrz_lines (identity.py:404) have no production callers — analyze_identity (identity.py:1359) goes straight to OCR + _validate_mrz_structure_only, so ICAO check digits (the module's strongest, checksum-grade tamper signal) are never computed, even for PDFs whose text layer contains a clean MRZ where checksums would be exact. scoring.py:34 still justifies identity's 1.5 weight with 'MRZ check digit validation (cryptographically reliable)' — stale rationale.
- Dead detection core #2: the entire 2D-DOC comparison engine — verify_2d_doc_against_pdf (twod_doc.py:1448), verify_pdf_with_2d_doc (twod_doc.py:1858), scan_image_for_2d_doc (twod_doc.py:889), format_verified_data (twod_doc.py:1117), estimate_income_from_tax's fraud use — has zero callers (grep-verified). app.py:815-916 only decodes and displays. 2D-DOC results produce no Flags and never touch the trust score.
- No cryptographic verification of the 2D-DOC signature anywhere (signature captured raw at twod_doc.py:654 and never used), while the UI hardcodes 'SIGNÉES - ne peuvent pas être falsifiées' (twod_doc.py:1148) and 'Source of Truth' (twod_doc.py:1125). CA ID / cert ID are never checked against the public ANTS/France Titres registry, header dates are never sanity-checked (future emission, signature < emission).
- identity.py:1442-1448: score deduction loop handles high/medium/low but not 'critical' — a critical flag deducts 0 points. Latent today (production paths emit max 'high') but becomes live the moment checksum validation is reconnected.
- identity.py:770: TD1 composite checksum omits line2[18:29] (optional data 2) which ICAO 9303 Part 5 includes — genuine TD1 documents with non-empty optional2 would fail MRZ_FINAL_CHECK_FAIL (critical) if this dead code is ever reconnected.
- identity.py:387-391: birth-date century heuristic picks 2000+yy whenever 2000+yy <= current year, so a centenarian born in '26' (1926) parses as 2026 — age 0, no flag, and wrong extracted data displayed to the user.
- identity.py:1059-1070: hardcoded expectation that a French CNI TD1 prefix is 'IR'. Post-2021 French CNIe MRZ starts 'IDFRA' — every genuine new-format CNI likely gets MRZ_CNI_TYPE_UNEXPECTED ('IR' appears confused with the residence-permit document code).
- Prefix whitelist inconsistency: ocr.py:332 accepts TD1 first char in {I,A,C} (C = US Green Card) but identity.py:1010 validates only {I,A} — any document that OCR accepts via 'C' is guaranteed a MRZ_DOC_TYPE_UNEXPECTED flag.
- identity.py:1425-1429: issuance_date hardcoded to None 'for time budget', permanently disabling MRZ_VALIDITY_PERIOD_UNUSUAL (identity.py:1191-1219) and MRZ_US_VALIDITY_PERIOD_UNEXPECTED (identity.py:1104-1142); the supplier function ocr_extract_issuance_date (ocr.py:374) is fully implemented and dead.
- identity.py:1242-1352: _analyze_scan_authenticity (ELA + noise grid) is dead; analyze_identity hardcodes photo_analysis={'ela_run': False} (identity.py:1439). Its noise metric is also unsound — Laplacian variance measures detail, not noise, so the 8x8-grid CV>2.0 threshold fires on essentially any document mixing text and background.
- identity.py:1402-1411: when no MRZ is found the module returns score=100 with zero flags (confidence 0.4) — a perfect score for a document on which nothing was verified.
- ocr.py:279: single y_ratio=0.80 renders only the bottom 20% of the PDF PAGE, but the MRZ is at the bottom of the CARD — a CNI photocopied on the top half of an A4 (the most common capture pattern) has its MRZ around mid-page and is never seen. No rotation/deskew handling either (portrait phone photos of landscape cards fail).
- ocr.py:41-74: _extract_mrz_candidate tolerates up to +6 extra OCR chars but zero dropped chars — Tesseract dropping one '<' from a filler run (very common) rejects the line outright; ocr.py:302's blanket space->'<' substitution can corrupt counts.
- ocr.py:352-365: global-buffer fallback regexes (P[A-Z0-9<]{43})([A-Z0-9<]{44}) can anchor one character off the true line start (any 'P' inside the buffer), producing shifted field boundaries; since checksums are never verified, garbage extracted fields (name, DOB, nationality) are stored in details and displayed as fact.
- ocr.py:222 vs 237-244: default dpi=120 contradicts the docstring ('Pourquoi 150 DPI', '300 recommandé'); ocr.py:131-134 documents from_y_ratio=0.62 while the only value used is 0.80 — comment drift that will mislead the redesign.
- twod_doc.py:819-885: scans page 1 only, top-left/top-right quadrants only, stops at first hit, decode timeout hardcoded 1500ms; doc.close() not in finally (twod_doc.py:881); broad except at 883-885 logs and returns [] so decode crashes are indistinguishable from 'no barcode'.
- twod_doc.py:753-771: pre-filter thresholds all hardcoded (gray<80, 60-300px, aspect 0.6-1.7, density 0.25-0.70) with no adaptive thresholding — the gate silently discards faint or merged barcodes before decode is even attempted.
- twod_doc.py:557-601: parse_message desynchronizes on any unknown fixed-length DI (registry twod_doc.py:231-359 is incomplete vs the spec) or on producers emitting GS after fixed fields — one unknown DI corrupts every following field.
- twod_doc.py:1061-1063: float(x) if x.isdigit() silently drops negative amounts ('-123') and decimal amounts ('1234,56') for tax fields 4V/4X/41 — values disappear from the 'verified data' panel with no warning.
- twod_doc.py:1264-1270: French tax brackets hardcoded to one year (2024/2025) with no link to DI 45 ('Année des revenus', which is right there in the registry); comment says ±20% margin, code uses 0.25 (twod_doc.py:1297-1299).
- twod_doc.py:1022: name extraction from field 44 via r'[A-Z][A-Z\s]+$' breaks on hyphenated or apostrophe names (JEAN-PIERRE, O'BRIEN) — truncated names would then fail the (dead) name comparison.
- twod_doc.py:59-60: cv2 and numpy imported unconditionally while fitz/PIL/pylibdmtx are carefully guarded — one missing opencv wheel crashes the module import and (via analyzer.py:32) the whole analyzer.
- Error handling that hides failures: identity.py:1392-1398 catches all OCR exceptions and proceeds to the score-100 'no MRZ' path; ocr.py:449-451 broad except returns None; identity.py:206-208 swallows render errors. In every case a hard failure is indistinguishable from a clean 'nothing found'.
- Minor dead weight: unused 'import io' (identity.py:75), unused 'from pathlib import Path' (ocr.py:26), scan_datamatrix_from_image always sets page=0 (twod_doc.py:721), TwoDocVerifiedData.raw_fields typed 'list = None' (twod_doc.py:991), MRZ_LINE_PATTERN's 29-45 tolerance (identity.py:270) is unreachable because extract_mrz_lines requires exact lengths (identity.py:442,455).

**False Positive Risks:**
- Genuine post-2021 French CNIe: MRZ starts 'IDFRA', but identity.py:1061 expects 'IR' for issuer FRA — every authentic new-format French ID card is flagged MRZ_CNI_TYPE_UNEXPECTED (low, -5). If the card also uses optional2 (identity.py:1072-1086 expects all '<'), a second low flag stacks.
- French CNI issued 2004-2013 with legally extended validity (10 -> 15 years by decree): the MRZ expiry date has passed but the card is legally valid — flagged MRZ_DOCUMENT_EXPIRED (medium, -10) on a perfectly acceptable document. Same for any workflow where users legitimately upload a recently-expired ID.
- US Green Card (permanent resident card): accepted by OCR via first char 'C' (ocr.py:332) then systematically flagged MRZ_DOC_TYPE_UNEXPECTED (low) by identity.py:1010 which only allows I/A — every genuine Green Card loses points.
- US or other passports with sex marker 'X' (issued since 2022): _validate_mrz_structure_only (identity.py:863-869) only accepts M/F/< — genuine X-marker passports get MRZ_INVALID_SEX (medium, -10).
- A slightly blurry but genuine passport photo where OCR misreads one DOB digit (e.g. '9'->'8' making the year 1885): age > 120 triggers MRZ_DOB_IMPOSSIBLE (high, -25) — exactly the OCR-noise false positive the structure-only design was supposed to avoid, at the highest per-flag deduction the module can give.
- Scanned brochure/newsletter/company report with a portrait photo and no text layer: _has_face_photo forces doc_type='identity' (analyzer.py:157-167), applying identity confidence multipliers (fonts x0.3, content x0.4, structure x0.5) and identity CRITICAL-cap logic to a document that is not an ID — distorting the whole document's score in both directions.
- If the dead comparison engine is reconnected as-is: a genuine but SCANNED avis d'imposition (image-only PDF) fails every field comparison because verify_pdf_with_2d_doc (twod_doc.py:1889-1898) extracts text with fitz only, no OCR fallback — 'CRITIQUE: possible fraude aux aides sociales' on an authentic document.
- If reconnected: a genuine avis d'imposition listing small amounts near income keywords (e.g. 'revenus de capitaux mobiliers : 150 €') trips the income-coherence check (twod_doc.py:1741-1756) because 150 < 0.5x estimated minimum income — false CRITICAL fraud indicator on an authentic tax notice.
- If reconnected: 2D-DOC name fields include qualité and may be RS-truncated ('M JEAN DUP...' per spec), while the printed document shows 'M. DUPONT Jean' — substring comparison after normalization (twod_doc.py:1387-1408) fails on word order alone, producing 'Nom non trouvé' fraud indicators on genuine documents.

**False Negative Gaps:**
- Forged 2D-DOC accepted as gospel: because the ECDSA signature is never verified and CA/cert IDs are never checked, an attacker can generate a DataMatrix with arbitrary data ('DC04...' + fields matching their edited PDF text) and a garbage signature. TrustyFile decodes it and the UI presents the forged values as 'signed — cannot be falsified'. This actively inverts trust: the fraud tool becomes an authentication stamp.
- Tampered visible text with intact genuine 2D-DOC — the exact fraud the 2D-DOC exists to catch (edited RFR on an avis d'imposition for benefits fraud): the comparison engine is dead code, so the mismatch is never computed, no flag is raised, and the trust score is unaffected. The user must manually eyeball the side-by-side panel.
- Edited MRZ with internally consistent format: a fraudster who changes DOB/expiry digits (without recomputing check digits) passes today's production validation, because checksums are never verified — structure-only checks only require 6 digits and a valid sex char. The classic 'change one digit' passport fraud sails through with score ~100.
- Pre-2021 French CNI (old laminated format, 2 MRZ lines x 36 chars, still massively in circulation and valid): neither ocr.py (looks only for 44 or 30-char lines) nor identity.py recognizes this format — no MRZ found, score 100, confidence 0.4. Same for TD2 documents and machine-readable visas ('V' prefix).
- CNI photocopied/photographed onto the top half of an A4 page (the most common real-world capture): the MRZ sits mid-page, outside the fixed bottom-20% OCR band (ocr.py:279) — MRZ never found, score 100. Rotated phone photos (90 degrees) fail the same way since there is no orientation detection.
- Completely fabricated ID with no MRZ at all (template-built 'CNI' image, or a real ID photo cropped above the MRZ): analyze_identity returns score=100 with zero flags (identity.py:1402-1411) — a fabricated document scores perfectly because absence of evidence is scored as evidence of absence.
- Photoshopped ID photo (swapped face, edited name field in the visual zone): the visual zone (VIZ) is never OCR'd or compared against MRZ fields, and the ELA/noise scan-authenticity check is dead (identity.py:1439) — as long as the MRZ region is untouched, arbitrary edits to the printed name/photo raise nothing from this module.
- CNIe where the back-side 2D-DOC and the MRZ disagree (e.g. doctored MRZ, genuine barcode or vice versa): both are decoded in the same session (app.py scans 2D-DOC, identity module extracts MRZ) but the two are never cross-compared — a direct, cheap, high-signal check that simply does not exist.
- 2D-DOC placed anywhere other than the top half of page 1: bottom-of-page barcodes (some pay slips/invoices), barcodes on page 2+ (cover letter first) are never scanned (twod_doc.py:822, 832-835) — document treated as having no 2D-DOC, losing both the fraud check and the positive trust signal.
- Faint/low-contrast scans of genuine barcodes: pre-filter threshold gray<80 (twod_doc.py:755) or size/density bounds silently skip pylibdmtx decode — no barcode 'found', no verification, no signal that detection was skipped rather than negative.
- Sample/specimen MRZs: reusing the ICAO 'UTOPIA' (UTO) specimen or ANTS specimen data passes all structural checks — no blocklist of known specimen documents/issuer codes exists.
- If the income-coherence check is ever reconnected: income falsified by less than ~50% (e.g. real 40k shown as 25k to hit a benefits threshold) passes, because the flag threshold is income < 0.5x estimated minimum (twod_doc.py:1742); and any searched amount is accepted if it appears ANYWHERE in the document text, so a tampered headline value passes when the true value survives in a detail table.

**Improvement Ideas:**
- Verify the 2D-DOC signature locally: the ANTS/France Titres certificate registry (TSL) is public and can be bundled with the app; C40/Base32-decode the signature and verify ECDSA with the 'cryptography' package — fully local, satisfies the never-send-the-file constraint. Until then: (a) validate ca_id/cert_id against the known ANTS list, (b) sanity-check header dates (emission not in future, signature >= emission), (c) soften the 'cannot be falsified' UI copy to 'signed data (signature not verified)'.
- Turn 2D-DOC into a real scoring module: wire verify_2d_doc_against_pdf (currently dead) into the analyzer as a ModuleResult — field mismatch = critical Flag, full match = strong positive bonus (project goal #2: confirm legitimate files). Fix its comparison first: anchor amount searches to their labels (search near 'Revenu fiscal de référence' instead of anywhere), fix the digit-run-merging regex (twod_doc.py:1436) by requiring 3-digit groups after separators, add an OCR fallback for scanned tax notices, and select tax brackets by DI 45 (income year) instead of hardcoding 2024.
- Cross-check 2D-DOC vs MRZ on CNIe: both are already decoded in the same session — compare name/DOB/document number between the signed barcode and the OCR'd MRZ; mismatch is a near-certain tamper signal at almost zero extra cost.
- Run ICAO checksums opportunistically instead of never: (1) if the PDF has a text layer with an MRZ, use extract_mrz_lines + validate_td3/td1 (checksums are exact there — e.g. France Identité exports, native-PDF fakes); (2) on OCR output, compute check digits anyway and use them asymmetrically: all-checksums-pass is a strong positive trust signal (random OCR errors rarely produce four passing digits), while failures stay low-severity 'unverifiable' rather than critical. Also fix the TD1 composite to include line2[18:29] (identity.py:770) and add the missing 'critical' branch to the score loop (identity.py:1442).
- Checksum-guided OCR repair: for failing check digits, try the standard confusion substitutions (0/O, 1/I, 5/S, 8/B, 2/Z, '<'/K) and re-test — if a single substitution makes all checksums pass, treat as genuine with a note; if no small repair works, escalate severity. This converts OCR noise from a reason to skip validation into a quantifiable signal.
- Locate the MRZ instead of assuming bottom-20%-of-page: detect the OCR-B band morphologically (long horizontal runs of monospaced glyphs) or OCR the full page at low DPI first, then re-OCR the located band at high DPI; try 0/90/180/270 rotations. This fixes the 'card photocopied on top of an A4' miss, the single biggest identity false-negative.
- Support the missing formats: old French CNI (2x36), TD2, visas ('V'); unify the TD1 prefix whitelist between ocr.py:332 and identity.py:1010; replace the hardcoded 'IR' French expectation with a small per-country document-code table (FRA CNIe='ID', FRA titre de séjour='IR', USA passport card='IP', ...).
- Never return score=100 for 'nothing verifiable': when no MRZ is found on a document routed to the identity module, return a neutral score (e.g. 50-60) with an explicit 'unverifiable' flag/state, and let the UI distinguish 'verified authentic-looking' from 'could not check anything'. Similarly distinguish 'OCR crashed' from 'no MRZ' (identity.py:1392-1398).
- Reconnect or delete the dead halves deliberately: call ocr_extract_issuance_date (one extra page OCR) to re-enable the two validity-period checks that are permanently off; either run scan-authenticity (ELA restricted to the photo region of the card — much more discriminative than whole-page) or remove _analyze_scan_authenticity and the misleading photo_analysis details key; delete scan_image_for_2d_doc / format_verified_data if the artifact panel replaces them.
- Harden 2D-DOC detection: scan all four quadrants (plus bottom strip) of the first 2-3 pages, use Otsu/adaptive thresholding in the pre-filter, retry decode at higher DPI when the pre-filter hits but decode fails, and raise the pylibdmtx timeout for candidate regions only. Log/flag 'barcode-like region found but undecodable' as its own low-severity signal instead of silence.
- Make parse_message resilient: treat unknown DIs as fatal-to-field but not fatal-to-stream (resynchronize on the next GS), tolerate GS after fixed-length fields, and record RS-truncation on fields so comparisons can use prefix-matching for truncated values. Complete the DI registry from spec section 8.
- Add a specimen/sample blocklist (UTO issuer code, published ANTS specimen document numbers, the spec's example MRZs) — reused sample data in an otherwise well-formed document is a high-confidence fraud signal.
- Fix data-quality bugs feeding the UI: century heuristic for centenarian DOBs (identity.py:391), isdigit() dropping negative/decimal tax amounts (twod_doc.py:1061-1063), hyphen/apostrophe-safe name regex (twod_doc.py:1022), and guard cv2/numpy imports in twod_doc.py like the other optional deps so a missing wheel doesn't kill the whole analyzer.

### CODE AUDIT — Image analysis (Module F, src/modules/images.py) and advanced image forensics (Module H, src/modules/forensics.py) — embedded-image checks, screenshot/scan detection, paste-artifact noise analysis, Error Level Analysis (ELA), and disabled clone detection. Both modules are wired into src/analyzer.py (lines 141, 149) with scoring weights images=0.8, forensics=0.9 (src/scoring.py:30-31).

**Checks:**
- **IMAGES_SCREENSHOT_DETECTED (check_screenshot_dimensions)** — images.py:159-193. Compares each embedded image's pixel dimensions against 10 hardcoded desktop/laptop screen resolutions (images.py:139-150), both orientations, ±20px tolerance (images.py:156). Severity HIGH (30 points) per image.
  - _quality:_ Cheap but brittle: desktop-only list (no phone resolutions, no 1600x900/2560x1600/ultrawide), exact-match so cropped/resized screenshots never match, HIGH severity is harsh for a signal with common legitimate causes. Tolerance was sensibly tightened from 50 to 20px after a documented A4-at-90PPI false positive (images.py:152-155).
- **IMAGES_RESOLUTION_MISMATCH (check_resolution_consistency)** — images.py:196-242. Computes pseudo-DPI per image as pixel_width / (page_width/72) — assumes every image spans the full page (images.py:113-115). Flags MEDIUM if max_dpi > 4x min_dpi and min_dpi > 10.
  - _quality:_ Fundamentally broken: DPI is derived from PAGE size, not the image's placed rectangle, so it effectively compares raw pixel counts; a 300px logo in a 1-inch box computes as ~36 DPI. Fires or stays silent at random relative to its stated purpose. Needs page.get_image_rects(xref). The comment at images.py:111-112 admits the shortcut.
- **IMAGES_HEAVY_COMPRESSION (check_heavy_compression)** — images.py:245-287. For DCT/JPEG-filtered images, compression_ratio = width*height*3 / stored_bytes; flags MEDIUM if ratio > 80:1 (images.py:274).
  - _quality:_ Naive: hardcodes 3 bytes/pixel so grayscale JPEG scans get a 3x-inflated ratio (real 27:1 flags as 80:1); flat graphics and text scans legitimately exceed 80:1 while a q-60 double-saved photo can stay under. JPX ignored. Should read JPEG quantization tables to estimate quality instead of using size.
- **IMAGES_EXCESSIVE_COUNT (check_image_count)** — images.py:290-328. Flags MEDIUM if images-per-page > 15. Contains a dead loop (images.py:321-326) identifying full-page images that just does `pass` with a TODO.
  - _quality:_ Arbitrary threshold, mostly harmless. get_images(full=True) counts repeated logo xrefs once per page and counts resource-listed-but-never-drawn images, inflating counts. Dead TODO loop is superseded by check_image_only_pdf and should be deleted.
- **IMAGES_NO_IMAGES (check_no_images)** — images.py:331-358. Flags LOW (5 points) if zero raster images and text_length > 500 ('unusual for branded invoices').
  - _quality:_ Weak premise: vector logos (InDesign/Illustrator/LaTeX, most utility-company PDFs) are invisible to page.get_images(), so professionally branded documents get flagged for having no images. Systematic noise pointing the wrong way — a text-only digital PDF is if anything a positive trust signal.
- **IMAGES_IMAGE_ONLY_PDF / IMAGES_MOSTLY_IMAGE_PDF (check_image_only_pdf)** — images.py:365-444. Counts 'full-page' images (width>500 AND height>700 px regardless of placement). If count >= 0.8*page_count: chars_per_page < 50 → MEDIUM IMAGE_ONLY; < 200 → MEDIUM MOSTLY_IMAGE.
  - _quality:_ Right concept, loose implementation: pixel thresholds ignore placement (large product photo counts as full-page; low-res fax scan doesn't); aggregate count doesn't require images on distinct pages (8 photos on one page of a 10-page doc triggers); and if the OCR fallback has populated text_by_page, chars_per_page exceeds 200 and true image-only scans are never flagged.
- **IMAGES_PASTE_NOISE_ANOMALY (check_paste_artifacts + find_amount_regions)** — images.py:456-655. Regex-locates text blocks containing currency amounts (AMOUNT_PATTERN, images.py:456-460), renders pages at zoom 2, computes noise = gray - GaussianBlur(5x5), skips pages with total noise variance < 1.0 (assumed digital), flags HIGH if an amount region's noise variance < 1/4 of the surrounding neighborhood variance (padding = 1x region size, neighborhood var must exceed 2.0).
  - _quality:_ The most targeted check and the right fraud scenario, but the physics is off: the Gaussian residual is dominated by text-edge energy, not sensor noise. So (a) the <1.0 digital-page gate almost never triggers (antialiased text alone exceeds it) — the claimed digital-PDF exclusion is false; (b) a pasted region containing typed text has high edge energy → high variance → never flagged; (c) variance ratios mostly measure text density. Also the neighborhood window INCLUDES the region itself (images.py:619-624), biasing the comparison, and region bboxes are whole text blocks that can span a full table row. Credit: white-level analysis was tested and honestly removed (images.py:647-652).
- **FORENSICS_ELA_MAJOR_EDIT / FORENSICS_ELA_MINOR_EDIT (compute_ela / detect_suspicious_regions / analyze_ela)** — forensics.py:63-170, 411-454. For each embedded image >= 200x200 px: re-encode JPEG q=95, amplify absdiff x20, threshold grayscale ELA at mean+3σ, keep contours >= 500 px area. Total suspicious area > 3% of image → MEDIUM (-15); > 5% → HIGH (-30). Runs on every decodable image regardless of original format.
  - _quality:_ Textbook ELA neutered by three flaws: (1) never checks base_image['ext'], so PNG/Flate/CCITT-converted images with no JPEG history get meaningless ELA; (2) cv2.multiply(diff, 20) saturates at 255 (forensics.py:95) — any diff >= 13 flattens to 255, destroying the relative-brightness signal and potentially pushing mean+3σ above 255 (empty mask); (3) the 3%/5% area gates require an edit covering a huge fraction of the image, while the module's own stated scenario (edit one amount) covers ~0.05-0.1% of a 300dpi scan — orders of magnitude below threshold.
- **Clone / copy-move detection (detect_clones / analyze_clones) — DISABLED dead code** — forensics.py:177-318. Grid-aligned 16px blocks, perceptual hash (8x8 mean-brightness bins, 16 levels), verification via MAD < 3, Manhattan distance >= 64, >= 10 verified blocks required, images > 2MP skipped, groups on <= 2 rows or <= 2 columns discarded as tables. Integration commented out at forensics.py:458-462 due to table false positives.
  - _quality:_ Dead code. Even re-enabled it cannot catch the target fraud: a copied digit spans 1-2 grid blocks (10 required), non-overlapping grid alignment misses arbitrary-offset pastes, the <=2-rows/cols filter discards small matches, and the 2MP cap excludes a standard 300dpi A4 scan (~8.7MP). Needs redesign (overlapping blocks or keypoint matching + offset-vector consistency), not re-tuning.

**Weaknesses:**
- images.py:113-115 — DPI is computed as image_pixels / page_size_inches, assuming every image covers the full page. This is wrong for any placed image (logo, signature, stamp) and makes check_resolution_consistency (images.py:196-242) compare pixel counts, not resolutions. Fix: page.get_image_rects(xref) for the placed rect.
- forensics.py:95 — cv2.multiply(diff, 20) saturates uint8 at 255; all diffs >= 13 become identical, flattening the ELA signal that detect_suspicious_regions then thresholds. With high mean the mean+3σ threshold can exceed 255, producing an empty mask (silent no-detection). Amplification should happen in float, or thresholding should be done on the raw diff.
- forensics.py:354-365 — ELA runs on whatever doc.extract_image returns without checking base_image['ext']. PNG/Flate images and CCITT/JBIG2 scans (which PyMuPDF converts to PNG) have no JPEG compression history, so ELA output is noise. SMask (soft mask) xrefs >= 200x200 are also analyzed as if they were photos.
- forensics.py:47-48 — ELA_SUSPICIOUS_RATIO (3%) and ELA_HIGHLY_SUSPICIOUS_RATIO (5%) gate on total suspicious area as a fraction of the whole image. A single edited amount on an A4 300dpi scan is ~0.05-0.1% of the image; the module's own headline fraud scenario can never reach its flag threshold. Detection statistics should be local/windowed, not global.
- images.py:589-593 — the page_noise < 1.0 'purely digital PDF' gate does not work: the Gaussian-blur residual is dominated by antialiased text-edge energy, so virtually every rendered text page passes the gate. The docstring's claim that digital PDFs are excluded (images.py:534-538) is false in practice, causing both wasted compute and digital-PDF false positives.
- images.py:615-624 — the 'neighborhood' window used for noise comparison includes the amount region itself, biasing neighborhood_var toward region_var and weakening the ratio test; region bboxes are whole text blocks (find_amount_regions, images.py:491-507), which for tables can span the entire row, diluting any local anomaly.
- images.py:456-460 — AMOUNT_PATTERN misses amounts without two decimals ('1 000 €', '1000 EUR', '25€'), does not handle US comma-thousands except via a partial match, and the bare second alternative matches ANY d.dd decimal (quantities, rates, version numbers), so 'amount regions' are both over- and under-inclusive. Fraudulent edits to dates, IBANs, names, or addresses are never examined at all.
- forensics.py:56 + 241 — CLONE_MAX_IMAGE_PIXELS = 2,000,000 skips clone detection for any image over 2MP; a standard 300dpi A4 scan is ~8.7MP, so the check (were it enabled) would skip exactly the documents it targets.
- forensics.py:458-462 — clone detection is entirely disabled (commented-out integration); ~140 lines (forensics.py:177-318) are dead code, and forensics.py:409/419's suspicious_images counter is incremented but never used.
- images.py:321-326 — dead loop in check_image_count that identifies full-page images and does `pass` with a TODO; superseded by check_image_only_pdf.
- images.py:701-704 and forensics.py:455-456 — broad `except Exception` swallows paste-detection and per-image ELA failures with only a log warning; the module still reports its normal score and confidence, so a systematically failing check silently looks like a clean result. find_amount_regions (images.py:488-489) likewise returns [] on any get_text error.
- images.py:130, forensics.py:377 — doc.close() is not in try/finally; an exception mid-loop leaks the document handle. Also images.py:104-108 silently maps extraction failure to size_bytes=0, which then silently disables the compression check for that image (images.py:265).
- Module docstring promises 'Image placement - Images placed over text (covering content)' (images.py:11) and CLAUDE.md lists it, but no such check exists anywhere — the white-rectangle-over-original-amount attack via PDF drawing operators is completely unhandled.
- images.py:270 — uncompressed size hardcodes 3 channels and ignores bpc/colorspace: grayscale JPEGs get 3x-overestimated compression ratios, CMYK underestimated.
- images.py:93 + counting — page.get_images(full=True) lists resource-referenced images even if never drawn, and the same xref repeated on N pages counts N times; no dedup by xref, inflating IMAGES_EXCESSIVE_COUNT and skewing resolution stats.
- images.py:569-575 — pixmap conversion assumes n==3 means RGB; if a pixmap ever carries 4 channels (alpha/CMYK edge cases), cv2.COLOR_RGB2BGR on a 4-channel array raises, which the outer try in analyze_images then swallows for the whole paste check.
- images.py:662-667 — SEVERITY_POINTS is a local duplicate of the scoring table in scoring.py (as is the inline -30/-15 logic in forensics.py:464-470); divergence risk if the org-wide weights change.
- Performance/architecture: the PDF is opened and images re-extracted at least three separate times (images.py:83, images.py:549, forensics.py:341) plus the main extractor pass; page rendering at zoom 2 is repeated per module. One shared extraction pass should feed both modules.
- forensics.py:474 — confidence = min(0.9, 0.5 + 0.1*n_images) rewards image COUNT, not analysis success; ten tiny decorative images yield 0.9 confidence even if every ELA call threw.

**False Positive Risks:**
- Honest paper invoice scanned by the customer (flatbed or scan app, JPEG at typical scanner quality): stacks IMAGES_IMAGE_ONLY_PDF (medium), often IMAGES_HEAVY_COMPRESSION (medium — text scans compress far beyond 80:1), and FORENSICS_ELA_MAJOR_EDIT (high — dense text edges produce strong ELA response easily exceeding 3-5% of image area on a never-JPEG or heavily-saturated ELA map). A perfectly legitimate scan can lose 60+ points from these two modules alone.
- Grayscale scanned documents specifically: the 3-bytes/pixel assumption (images.py:270) triples the computed compression ratio, so a normal-quality grayscale scan flags IMAGES_HEAVY_COMPRESSION at an effective real ratio of ~27:1.
- Digital invoice containing a noisy photographic element near the totals (product photo, scanned signature, stamp, embedded ID photo): the paste-noise check compares the clean digital amount text (near-zero residual variance) against a neighborhood window inflated by the adjacent photo's noise → HIGH IMAGES_PASTE_NOISE_ANOMALY on an untouched document, precisely because the digital-PDF gate (page_noise < 1.0) fails to exclude it.
- Legitimate personal-workflow screenshots: bank/mobile-app payment confirmations or e-tickets exported by screenshotting at exactly 1920x1080/1366x768 etc. get a HIGH severity IMAGES_SCREENSHOT_DETECTED (30 points) — a very common non-fraud behavior for individuals submitting proofs.
- Professionally produced text-only or vector-branded PDFs (LaTeX invoices, accounting-software output, utility bills with vector logos): flagged IMAGES_NO_IMAGES because vector artwork is invisible to page.get_images() — the most trustworthy class of document receives a penalty for its trustworthy trait.
- Expense reports / real-estate diagnostic reports / illustrated statements with many small receipt photos or per-line icons: IMAGES_EXCESSIVE_COUNT at >15 images/page, and repeated per-page letterhead images inflate the count further because xrefs are counted once per page.
- Documents mixing a high-res full-page scan appendix with small UI icons: the pseudo-DPI spread (really just pixel-count spread) exceeds 4x → IMAGES_RESOLUTION_MISMATCH on a normal composite document.
- Photographed (not scanned) documents re-saved once by a phone scan app: uniform JPEG artifacts plus paper texture push large contiguous areas over the mean+3σ ELA threshold; with the x20 saturated amplification the ELA map becomes near-binary and contour areas balloon past the 3-5% gates.

**False Negative Gaps:**
- The flagship scenario — a single amount changed in Paint/Photoshop on a scanned invoice — is missed by ELA because a ~100x30px edit is ~0.05-0.1% of a 300dpi A4 scan, far below the 3% ELA_SUSPICIOUS_RATIO gate (forensics.py:47). Local anomaly strength is never evaluated, only global area.
- Copy-move of a digit within the same document (the classic '1'→'4' clone): clone detection is disabled outright (forensics.py:458-462), and even enabled its parameters (min 10 matching 16px grid-aligned blocks, >2MP images skipped) could not detect a one-digit clone on a real scan.
- Whole-image re-save laundering: attacker edits the scan, then re-exports the ENTIRE image at uniform quality (or screenshots their own edit). ELA differences become uniform, the relative mean+3σ threshold adapts, and nothing flags — the dynamic threshold guarantees ~constant tail mass regardless of tampering.
- Format-switch laundering: converting the edited image to PNG/Flate before embedding removes all JPEG history; the module runs meaningless ELA anyway (no ext check at forensics.py:354-365) and reports clean with normal confidence.
- Vector overlay attack: drawing a white rectangle + new text with PDF operators OVER the original scanned image (what many PDF editors actually do). The embedded image is byte-identical so ELA sees nothing; the promised 'image placed over text' check was never implemented; the paste-noise check only fires if the overlay happens to sit on a regex-matched amount region and survives the text-edge-dominated variance test.
- Edits to anything other than currency amounts: dates, IBAN, SIREN/SIRET, names, addresses, reference numbers are outside AMOUNT_PATTERN (images.py:456-460), so the paste-noise check never even looks at them; amounts without two decimals ('1 000 €') are also invisible.
- Phone screenshots: none of the 10 SCREEN_RESOLUTIONS are phone-shaped (1170x2532, 1080x2400, 1284x2778...), and any cropped or resized screenshot defeats the exact-dimension match entirely — yet phone screenshots are the dominant modern screenshot form.
- Pasted region containing typed text: the noise-variance test expects the pasted area to be QUIETER than its surroundings, but typed replacement text adds edge energy, raising region variance above the /4 threshold → systematically missed (the most common paste actually includes new text).
- Fake text layer over an image-only scan: adding invisible OCR-style text (>200 chars/page) suppresses both IMAGE_ONLY and MOSTLY_IMAGE flags (images.py:408-442) while also feeding attacker-chosen text to the content module; similarly, if TrustyFile's own OCR fallback fills text_by_page first, genuine image-only scans stop flagging.
- Small tampered images: anything under 200x200 px (stamps, signature images, small pasted logos) is excluded from all forensics (forensics.py:39-40, 360-361), and images under 10px regions are skipped in paste detection.
- Bilevel/CCITT fax-style scans (very common for French attestations): converted to PNG on extraction, ELA meaningless, noise layer near-binary — tampering in this whole document class passes both modules.
- Noise-matched paste: an attacker who adds synthetic noise (or uses content-aware fill) to the pasted rectangle defeats the variance-ratio test; no higher-order statistic (JPEG 8x8 grid phase, noise spectrum, CFA pattern) is checked.

**Improvement Ideas:**
- Compute true placed DPI via page.get_image_rects(xref) (pixels / placed-rect-inches) and dedup images by xref; this simultaneously fixes check_resolution_consistency, makes 'full-page image' mean actual page coverage, and stops per-page double counting.
- Gate ELA on base_image['ext'] in ('jpeg','jpg'); skip SMasks; do ELA math in float without cv2.multiply saturation; estimate the original quality from the JPEG quantization tables (works fully locally) and re-compress at that estimated quality — this is standard ELA practice and dramatically improves signal.
- Replace the global 3%/5% ELA area gate with local statistics: slide a window (or use the text-block map from find_amount_regions) and flag windows whose ELA energy is an outlier vs the image's own distribution. This is what makes a 0.1%-area amount edit detectable while a uniformly re-saved image stays clean.
- Rebuild clone detection as overlapping-block or keypoint (ORB) copy-move with offset-vector clustering/RANSAC: genuine copy-move produces many matches sharing ONE consistent offset vector, while table structure produces periodic offsets — this solves the exact false-positive problem that got the feature disabled, and lift the 2MP cap by downscale-then-verify.
