- Fix the paste-noise physics: mask out text strokes (binarize and dilate) before measuring variance so you compare BACKGROUND noise only; exclude the region from its own neighborhood window; and add a JPEG-blocking-grid phase check (pasted content is rarely aligned to the host image's 8x8 DCT grid) as an independent, noise-robust paste signal.
- Broaden the sensitive-region finder beyond AMOUNT_PATTERN: dates, IBAN/BIC, SIREN/SIRET, reference numbers, and totals keywords ('Total', 'TTC', 'Net à payer'); use span-level bboxes instead of whole block bboxes so the analyzed region tightly bounds the value.
- Implement the promised occlusion check: use page.get_drawings() and image rects to detect opaque rectangles/images drawn over existing text or over the scanned image in z-order — this directly catches the white-box overlay attack that currently bypasses everything, and it is cheap and local.
- Distinguish text-layer provenance: record whether text came from a real text layer or the OCR fallback, and have check_image_only_pdf use the pre-OCR count; treat 'image-only scan' as a document-class signal (route to scan-specific checks, cap stacked scan penalties) rather than letting IMAGE_ONLY + HEAVY_COMPRESSION + ELA pile up on honest scans — this is the single biggest false-positive reducer.
- Fix compression heuristics: bytes-per-pixel adjusted for actual channel count and bpc, or better, decode the JPEG quantization tables to estimate save quality and count of re-saves (double-quantization detection is also a strong tamper signal and fully local).
- Modernize screenshot detection: add phone/tablet resolutions, use aspect-ratio families plus UI-chrome cues (status bar strip, uniform top band) instead of exact dimensions, and downgrade severity to medium — screenshots are a provenance signal, not proof of fraud (project goal: minimize false positives).
- Error handling and honesty: try/finally around doc.close(); when a check throws, lower the module's confidence and attach an 'analysis incomplete' note instead of silently reporting clean; delete dead code (images.py:321-326 TODO loop, unused suspicious_images) or move disabled clone code behind an explicit experimental flag.
- Architecture: extract images once (in pdf_extractor) and pass decoded arrays + placed rects + ext to both Module F and Module H; render each page once and share the pixmap — removes 2 of 3 redundant PDF opens and roughly halves this area's runtime.
- Add positive trust signals from this area (project goal 2): e.g., 'digital PDF with vector graphics and coherent single-pass JPEG images', 'scan with uniform noise statistics across all sensitive regions' — small confidence bonuses that help confirm legitimate files instead of only penalizing.
- Centralize SEVERITY_POINTS/score deltas in scoring.py and import them, removing the duplicated tables in images.py:662-667 and forensics.py:464-470.

---

### CODE AUDIT — Module A: PDF metadata analysis (src/modules/metadata.py) + core PDF extraction layer (src/extractors/pdf_extractor.py)

**Checks:**
- **META_IMAGE_EDITOR (check_producer, metadata.py:180-199)** — Case-insensitive substring search of Producer+Creator (concatenated) against 13 image-editor names (photoshop, gimp, canva, photopea, paint.net, pixelmator, etc.). Severity: high. Early-returns, suppressing all other producer checks.
  - _quality:_ Sound idea (image editors are anomalous producers for official docs), but naive substring matching, hardcoded in-function list rebuilt every call, and the early return means an AI-generated + image-edited doc only gets one flag. Canva appears in three lists with three severities; this one wins by check order, not by design.
- **META_IMAGE_CONVERSION (check_producer, metadata.py:203-215)** — Substring search for image-to-PDF keywords ('image conversion', 'image to pdf', 'img2pdf', 'photo to pdf'). Severity: low (scans are legitimate).
  - _quality:_ Reasonable low-severity signal, but the keyword list misses the producer strings of the most common scan apps (CamScanner, Adobe Scan, Genius Scan, Microsoft Lens, iOS Quartz), so real-world coverage is near zero.
- **META_AI_GENERATED (check_producer, metadata.py:218-231)** — Substring search against 26 AI-tool names (chatgpt, openai, claude, gemini, bard, tome, gamma.app, ...). Severity: critical (50 points). Early return.
  - _quality:_ Brittle: several entries are short common substrings ('bard' matches 'Bombardier', 'tome' matches any producer containing it, 'gemini'/'copilot' collide with unrelated products). Critical severity on a bare substring hit is a strong FP amplifier, and the list will age fast.
- **META_ONLINE_CONVERTER (check_producer, metadata.py:234-247)** — Substring search against 6 'highly suspicious' converters (ilovepdf, smallpdf, sejda, pdfcandy, pdf2go, canva). Severity: high. Early return.
  - _quality:_ Matches the spec, but CLAUDE.md says 'flag but don't over-penalize' converters; high (30 pts) plus structure-module flags can double-penalize. Canva is unreachable here (caught earlier as image editor).
- **META_SUSPICIOUS_PRODUCER (check_producer, metadata.py:250-262)** — Substring search against ~20 moderately suspicious tools (pdf24, sodapdf, nitro, pdfelement, xodo, google docs, generic 'pdf editor'...). Severity: medium. Early return.
  - _quality:_ Generic entry 'pdf editor' (metadata.py:54) matches any producer containing those words; 'google docs' at medium contradicts the minimize-false-positives goal since many small businesses legitimately invoice from Google Docs.
- **META_FUTURE_CREATION_DATE (check_dates, metadata.py:290-300)** — CreationDate > now + 1 day → critical (50 pts); 1-day tolerance for timezones. Not called by analyze_metadata — invoked only from structure.py:1776, so points land in the structure module's score.
  - _quality:_ Concept fine; implementation compares a timezone-STRIPPED PDF datetime (parse_pdf_date discards the offset) against naive local datetime.now(). A doc created in UTC+13/+14 analyzed on a UTC-8..-10 host can legitimately appear >24h in the future. Critical severity for possible clock skew is harsh.
- **META_DOCUMENT_MODIFIED (check_dates, metadata.py:306-338)** — ModDate − CreationDate > 2 seconds → critical (50 pts) with human-readable gap message; rationale: 'official documents should never be modified after creation'. Invoked from structure.py:1776.
  - _quality:_ The single biggest false-positive generator. The 2-second threshold is a wrong assumption: large report generation exceeds 2s between setting CreationDate and finalizing; server-side linearization, e-signature workflows (DocuSign/Adobe Sign), and stamping legitimately update ModDate. Because timezone offsets are stripped, creation stored as local (+02'00') and mod stored as UTC (Z) for the SAME instant reads as a 2h 'modification' → instant critical. Flat critical severity regardless of gap size (2s vs 300 days) throws away signal.
- **META_IMPOSSIBLE_DATES (check_dates, metadata.py:341-351)** — ModDate < CreationDate → high (30 pts): impossible ordering indicating corruption or forged metadata.
  - _quality:_ Good check in principle, but zero tolerance interacts badly with timezone stripping: creation stored in UTC and mod in local negative-offset time for the same instant reads as mod-before-creation → spurious high flag.
- **META_NO_METADATA (check_missing_metadata, metadata.py:375-383)** — All values in the raw PyMuPDF metadata dict empty → medium (15 pts), 'metadata possibly stripped'.
  - _quality:_ Effectively DEAD/unreachable: PyMuPDF's doc.metadata always includes a synthetic non-empty 'format' key (e.g. 'PDF 1.7'), so non_empty_fields is never 0. A fully stripped PDF only ever gets the low-severity META_NO_PRODUCER.
- **META_NO_PRODUCER (check_missing_metadata, metadata.py:384-391)** — Some metadata present but neither producer nor creator set → low (5 pts).
  - _quality:_ Works, but severity is inverted: absent producer/creator on a supposedly system-generated invoice is a stronger laundering signal than 5 points, and it simultaneously drops module confidence to 0.3 (metadata.py:443-451), muting the module in the weighted final score — the attacker is rewarded twice for stripping.
- **Module scoring + confidence (analyze_metadata, metadata.py:432-451)** — Score = 100 − Σ severity points (low 5 / medium 15 / high 30 / critical 50), floored at 0. Confidence: 1.0 if producer/creator AND creation date present, 0.7 if one, 0.3 if neither.
  - _quality:_ Simple, but SEVERITY_POINTS is duplicated per module (also in structure.py) inviting drift, and confidence is derived from fields the attacker controls: forged-complete metadata gets confidence 1.0 while stripped metadata gets 0.3 — backwards for adversarial input.
- **SHA256 file hashing (calculate_file_hash, pdf_extractor.py:87-115)** — Chunked (8KB) SHA256 of the raw file, stored in PDFData.file_hash for identification/caching.
  - _quality:_ Correct and standard. But the file is hashed, then reopened by fitz, then re-opened by ~6 downstream modules via file_path — no single-read consistency guarantee, and no size/page-count guard (PDF-bomb / huge-doc DoS risk on the Streamlit host).
- **PDF date parsing (parse_pdf_date, pdf_extractor.py:118-155)** — Strips 'D:' prefix, truncates to 14 chars, right-pads with '0', strptime('%Y%m%d%H%M%S'); timezone offset deliberately discarded ('for simplicity in MVP'); returns None + logger.warning on failure.
  - _quality:_ Multiple defects: (1) discarding the offset makes every downstream date comparison unsound; (2) zero-padding builds invalid dates for short forms — 'D:2024' (valid per PDF spec, meaning Jan 1 2024) becomes '20240000000000' → month 00 → strptime fails → silently None; (3) nonstandard producer formats (ISO-style dates) fail; (4) an unparseable date — itself a tamper signal — degrades to None with only a log line, silently disabling all three date checks AND lowering module confidence.
- **Metadata extraction (extract_metadata, pdf_extractor.py:158-188)** — Maps PyMuPDF doc.metadata (Info dictionary view) into PDFMetadata (parsed dates, producer/creator/author/title/subject/keywords) plus the raw dict.
  - _quality:_ Reads only the Info dictionary; XMP is handled separately in structure.py by reopening the file. Author/title/subject/keywords are extracted but never used by any check (the CLAUDE.md-specified author/company mismatch check is unimplemented). PyMuPDF returns '' not None for absent fields, so 'str | None' fields actually carry '' — falsy so checks work, but the type contract is wrong.
- **Text extraction (extract_text, pdf_extractor.py:191-212)** — page.get_text('text') for every page, one string per page; no per-page error handling; no OCR triggering (ocr.py is separate).
  - _quality:_ get_text includes invisible text (render mode 3) indistinguishably from visible text — a white-rectangle-over-old-amount tamper yields old and new text mixed with no visibility signal; a page-level MuPDF error aborts the whole extraction.
- **Top-level extraction (extract_pdf_data, pdf_extractor.py:215-262)** — Hash → fitz.open (context manager) → metadata + per-page text → PDFData(file_path, hash, page_count, metadata, raw_metadata, text_by_page). Docstring documents raising FileNotFoundError / fitz.FileDataError.
  - _quality:_ Violates the project's own rules ('Always use fitz.open() with error handling', 'never crash on malformed PDFs, return partial results'): no try/except here and analyzer.py:109 doesn't catch either, so a corrupt PDF surfaces as a raw exception. No encrypted-PDF handling (doc.needs_pass/is_encrypted → silently empty metadata/text, no flag), no doc.is_repaired capture (MuPDF silently repairs broken xrefs — a strong tamper signal, lost), no doc.is_pdf check (an image renamed .pdf passes analyzer's suffix-only check and opens as an image document). PDFData's images/fonts/links fields are still TODO (pdf_extractor.py:81-84), so every downstream module re-opens the file from file_path — 6+ redundant parses per analysis.

**Weaknesses:**
- Dead code — LEGITIMATE_PRODUCERS (metadata.py:108-135) is defined, documented ('we won't flag these') and never referenced anywhere in the codebase; there is consequently no positive-trust signal from known-good producers (SAP, Sage, JasperReports...), despite 'confirm legitimate files' being a primary project goal.
- Unreachable check — check_missing_metadata (metadata.py:375-383) counts non-empty values in PyMuPDF's raw metadata dict, which always contains a synthetic non-empty 'format' key ('PDF 1.7'), so META_NO_METADATA can never fire; only the 5-point META_NO_PRODUCER ever triggers for stripped metadata.
- Confused layering / misleading module boundaries — check_dates (metadata.py:267-352) is defined in metadata.py, advertised in the module docstring (metadata.py:8-9), tested in tests/test_metadata.py, but excluded from analyze_metadata (comment at metadata.py:427-428) and only called from structure.py:1776, where its META_* flags are scored with the structure module's SEVERITY_POINTS.
- Timezone stripping — parse_pdf_date (pdf_extractor.py:143-148) truncates to 14 chars and discards the UTC offset, producing naive datetimes; check_dates then compares them to naive datetime.now() (metadata.py:287). Any producer that writes CreationDate and ModDate in different conventions (local+offset vs Z) yields a phantom multi-hour 'modification' and an instant critical flag.
- Arbitrary hardcoded thresholds — 2-second mod/creation tolerance (metadata.py:310), 1-day future tolerance (metadata.py:290), all severities and SEVERITY_POINTS (metadata.py:143-148) are inline constants with no config, no per-document-type variation, and SEVERITY_POINTS is duplicated in structure.py (drift risk).
- Flat critical severity for any mod gap — metadata.py:311-338 assigns 'critical' identically for a 3-second gap and a 300-day gap; the graduated messages (seconds/minutes/hours/days) exist purely for display while the score impact is constant 50 points.
- Naive substring matching — check_producer (metadata.py:176, 185, 218, 234, 250) does `needle in combined` with no word boundaries; short needles like 'bard' (metadata.py:81), 'tome' (95), 'gemini' (80), 'copilot' (82), 'pdf editor' (54) collide with legitimate producer strings ('Bombardier...', products named Gemini, Microsoft Copilot-branded exports).
- Early returns hide co-occurring signals — check_producer returns after the first match in each tier (metadata.py:199, 231, 247, 262), so at most one producer flag is ever emitted; image-editor detection preempts AI detection, and multiple independent suspicious markers don't accumulate score impact.
- IMAGE_EDITORS and IMAGE_CONVERSION_KEYWORDS are function-local constants (metadata.py:180-184, 203) unlike the other module-level lists — inconsistent, rebuilt on every call, and easy to miss when auditing the lists.
- parse_pdf_date zero-padding bug — pdf_extractor.py:148 pads short-but-valid PDF dates ('D:2024', 'D:202401') with '0', producing month/day '00' which strptime rejects; spec-valid dates parse to None, silently disabling all date checks with only a logger.warning (pdf_extractor.py:154).
- Unparseable dates produce no flag — a malformed CreationDate/ModDate string (itself a classic tamper artifact) results in None, a log line, skipped date checks, AND reduced module confidence (metadata.py:444-451) — the failure is triply hidden.
- No error handling around fitz.open — extract_pdf_data (pdf_extractor.py:246) and analyzer.py:109 let fitz exceptions propagate, contradicting CLAUDE.md's 'never crash on malformed PDFs, return partial results with lower confidence'.
- No encrypted/repaired/non-PDF detection — extract_pdf_data never inspects doc.needs_pass, doc.is_encrypted, doc.is_repaired, or doc.is_pdf; an encrypted or silently-repaired PDF (repair = strong tamper hint) or an image renamed .pdf all flow through with empty metadata and no dedicated flag; analyzer validates only the .pdf filename suffix, not magic bytes.
- PDFMetadata type contract wrong — PyMuPDF returns '' for absent metadata fields, so fields declared 'str | None' (pdf_extractor.py:48-55) carry empty strings; downstream truthiness checks happen to work but details dicts expose '' and identity comparisons to None would fail.
- Extractor is under-built relative to its role — PDFData images/fonts/links are TODOs (pdf_extractor.py:81-84), so structure.py, fonts, images, forensics all re-open the file independently from pdf_data.file_path: 6+ full parses per analysis, no consistency with the hashed bytes, no shared caching.
- extract_text has no per-page error isolation (pdf_extractor.py:203-210) — one malformed page aborts extraction for the whole document instead of returning partial results.
- Extracted-but-unused metadata — author, title, subject, keywords (pdf_extractor.py:182-185) feed no check; the CLAUDE.md-specified 'author/company mismatch' detection for Module A is unimplemented.
- Confidence heuristic is attacker-controlled — analyze_metadata (metadata.py:443-451) grants confidence 1.0 exactly when producer+dates are present (trivially forgeable with exiftool/pikepdf) and 0.3 when absent, down-weighting the module precisely when stripping — a suspicious act — occurred.
- No file-size/page-count guard anywhere in the extraction path (pdf_extractor.py:215-262) — a PDF bomb or 5000-page upload hashes, parses, and text-extracts unboundedly on the small cloud host.

**False Positive Risks:**
- E-signed contracts and invoices (DocuSign, Adobe Sign): signing legitimately updates ModDate hours or days after CreationDate → META_DOCUMENT_MODIFIED critical (−50). Digitally signed documents — the most trustworthy category — are systematically hammered by the 2-second rule, which fires from structure.py regardless of signature status.
- Server-side post-processing by legitimate issuers: banks/utilities that generate a statement then linearize it ('Fast Web View'), stamp a duplicate mark, or merge attachments minutes later → ModDate gap > 2s → critical flag on a genuine document.
- Mixed timezone conventions from one legitimate generator: CreationDate written as local time with +02'00' and ModDate as UTC 'Z' for the same instant → phantom 2-hour 'modification' → critical, purely an artifact of parse_pdf_date discarding offsets (pdf_extractor.py:143-148).
- Long-running report generation: a JasperReports/Crystal Reports 500-page statement where CreationDate is set at job start and ModDate at finalization 10-30s later → 'modified 15 second(s) after creation - possibly tampered', critical.
- Documents from far-east timezones: an invoice created this morning in Auckland (UTC+12/+13) analyzed on a US-hosted server can appear more than a day in the future after offset stripping → META_FUTURE_CREATION_DATE critical.
- Freelancer/small-business invoices designed in Canva (millions of legitimate users) → META_IMAGE_EDITOR high; invoices written in Google Docs → META_SUSPICIOUS_PRODUCER medium — both common legitimate workflows CLAUDE.md explicitly says not to over-penalize.
- A customer legitimately merging their genuine invoice with T&C pages via iLovePDF/Smallpdf before forwarding it → META_ONLINE_CONVERTER high (30 pts).
- Producer-string substring collisions: any legitimate producer/creator containing 'bard' (e.g. 'Bombardier ERP Export'), 'gemini' (unrelated products named Gemini), 'tome', or 'copilot' (Microsoft 365 Copilot-branded exports) → META_AI_GENERATED critical (−50) from a substring accident (metadata.py:74-105, matching at :218-219).
- A user who opens a genuine PDF in mobile PDF Expert/Xodo just to read or annotate and re-saves before uploading → medium suspicious-producer flag plus a critical mod-date flag, though content is untouched.
- Legitimate letters/attestations exported from Grammarly's editor ('grammarly ai' in creator, metadata.py:92) → critical AI-generated flag.

**False Negative Gaps:**
- Full-rewrite laundering: attacker edits the invoice content and re-exports through Word, LibreOffice, Acrobat, or prints to PDF (Quartz PDFContext / Skia / Microsoft Print to PDF). Producer looks benign or is absent from every suspicious list, CreationDate == ModDate on the fresh file → zero metadata flags and confidence 1.0. The module only catches attackers naive enough to leave a converter fingerprint.
- Ghostscript/qpdf/mutool/pikepdf laundering — the classic post-edit cleanup tools that rewrite the whole file and destroy incremental-update evidence — are absent from every suspicious list; producer 'GPL Ghostscript 10.x' passes clean, when it should at least be a 're-processed document' signal for an invoice.
- Direct metadata forgery: one exiftool/pikepdf command sets Producer='SAP NetWeaver', CreationDate=ModDate=plausible past date. Every check passes and confidence becomes 1.0; there is no cross-validation in this module between Info-dict claims and structural evidence (XMP mismatch is delegated to structure.py only).
- Metadata stripping as evasion: removing the Info dictionary costs 5 points (META_NO_PRODUCER; META_NO_METADATA is unreachable due to the 'format'-key bug) and drops module confidence to 0.3, so in the confidence-weighted final score the stripped module barely counts — stripping is net-rewarded.
- ModDate normalization: copying CreationDate into ModDate after editing (exiftool one-liner; several editors do this by default) fully bypasses META_DOCUMENT_MODIFIED and META_IMPOSSIBLE_DATES.
- Unparseable/garbage date strings: a tampered PDF whose CreationDate is 'D:garbage' or ISO-format parses to None → all three date checks silently skip, no MALFORMED_DATE flag exists, and confidence drops, muting the module — malformed dates should themselves be a signal.
- Encrypted PDFs: doc.needs_pass / is_encrypted never checked; an encrypted upload yields empty metadata/text, earning only low META_NO_PRODUCER — no 'encrypted, cannot analyze' flag.
- Silently repaired PDFs: MuPDF auto-repairs broken xref tables (frequent after crude binary edits); doc.is_repaired is never read, so a strong tamper indicator is discarded at extraction time.
- Non-PDF disguises: analyzer validates only the '.pdf' filename suffix; fitz opens an image or XPS renamed to .pdf (doc.is_pdf unchecked), producing empty metadata → only low-severity flags for what is likely a screenshot-of-an-invoice fraud.
- Invisible/covered text tampering at the extraction layer: extract_text mixes render-mode-3 (invisible) text with visible text indistinguishably, so downstream modules cannot detect the overlay-new-text-hide-old-text pattern from the text layer.
- Author/title/subject fields never analyzed: an invoice claiming EDF whose Author='John Smith' or Title='invoice_edited_v3_FINAL' sails through — the CLAUDE.md 'author/company mismatch' check for Module A is unimplemented.
- AI-tool list decay: only fixed 2023-2024-era brand names are matched; any newer or self-hosted generator — or simply clearing the producer — evades META_AI_GENERATED entirely.

**Improvement Ideas:**
- Make parse_pdf_date timezone-aware: parse the full PDF date grammar (offset, 'Z', apostrophe-quoted minutes, short forms like 'D:2024' with spec defaults), return aware datetimes, compare in UTC, and expose the raw strings + a MALFORMED_DATE flag when parsing fails instead of a silent None.
- Replace the flat 2-second/critical modification rule with graduated severity informed by context: no flag under ~1 minute, low for same-session gaps, escalate with gap size, and cross-reference structure-module evidence (incremental update count, signature presence) — a mod gap that coincides with a valid digital signature should be neutral or positive, not critical.
- Switch producer matching from bare substring to word-boundary/regex token matching with per-entry match rules, report ALL matches instead of early-returning, and move IMAGE_EDITORS/keywords to module level (or better: externalize all producer lists to a versioned data file with per-entry severity, so ghostscript/qpdf/mutool/quartz/skia/stirling-pdf and future AI tools can be added without code changes).
- Fix META_NO_METADATA by excluding PyMuPDF's synthetic keys ('format', 'encryption') before counting non-empty fields, raise the severity of fully stripped metadata, and stop letting missing metadata reduce module confidence — decouple 'how much evidence we saw' from 'how suspicious the absence is' so stripping is penalized, not rewarded.
- Actually use LEGITIMATE_PRODUCERS as a positive trust signal (score bonus / higher baseline) to serve the confirm-legitimate-files goal, but only when corroborated by structural evidence (e.g. matching XMP toolkit, no incremental updates), since the Info dict alone is trivially forgeable.
- Harden extract_pdf_data per the project's own rules: wrap fitz.open with error handling returning partial PDFData + extraction flags; detect and flag doc.needs_pass/is_encrypted, doc.is_repaired (strong tamper signal currently discarded), and doc.is_pdf/magic-byte mismatches; add file-size and page-count guards before parsing.
- Enrich PDFData once instead of letting six modules re-open the file: fill the TODO images/fonts/links fields, add raw creation/mod date strings, PDF version, xref/trailer facts, and is_repaired, so all modules analyze one consistent snapshot matching the SHA256 hash.
- Implement the specified-but-missing author/company mismatch check using the already-extracted author/title/subject fields (compare against vendor identity detected by content/template modules).
- Differentiate visible vs invisible text at extraction time (get_text('dict') span flags / render mode) so downstream modules can detect overlay-tamper patterns instead of receiving them pre-mixed.
- Centralize SEVERITY_POINTS and thresholds in scoring.py or a config module (they are duplicated in metadata.py:143-148 and structure.py), and move check_dates fully into the structure module to end the split-ownership confusion between metadata.py and structure.py:1776.

---

### CODE AUDIT — Module G: External Verification (src/modules/external.py) + Document Type Classifier (src/document_type.py) — identifier verification against French/EU registries and keyword-based document classification that drives scoring confidence multipliers

**Checks:**
- **SIRET registry verification (EXTERNAL_SIRET_NOT_FOUND / EXTERNAL_SIRET_VERIFICATION_FAILED)** — For each labeled, Luhn-valid SIRET extracted by content.extract_siret, queries https://recherche-entreprises.api.gouv.fr/search?q={siret} (free-text search, 10s timeout). If the API says 'not found' -> critical flag (50 pts). Any other error (timeout, 429, HTTP 5xx, offline) -> low flag (5 pts). external.py:568-660.
  - _quality:_ Naive: uses the full-text search endpoint instead of an exact-lookup endpoint, then walks results for an exact siege/matching_etablissements SIRET match with a SIREN-prefix fallback. 'Not found' at critical severity is far too harsh for a search API that excludes non-diffusible companies and lags new registrations. Network errors penalize the document instead of lowering confidence.
- **SIREN registry verification (EXTERNAL_SIREN_NOT_FOUND / EXTERNAL_SIREN_VERIFICATION_FAILED)** — For each labeled, Luhn-valid SIREN (skipping SIRENs already covered by a verified SIRET), same API search. Not found -> critical (50 pts); other errors -> low (5 pts). external.py:662-707.
  - _quality:_ Weaker than the SIRET path: verify_siren_annuaire (external.py:252) blindly takes results[0] without checking results[0]['siren'] == siren, so the 'verified company' can be a completely different entity returned by full-text search.
- **Potential-SIREN pattern verification (unlabeled 'XXX XXX XXX' numbers)** — extract_potential_sirens (external.py:66-104) matches any \b\d{3}\s+\d{3}\s+\d{3}\b, filters by Luhn checksum (~10% of random 9-digit numbers pass), then queries the registry for each. Not-found is only logged (no flag); a hit on a closed company raises EXTERNAL_COMPANY_CLOSED high (30 pts). external.py:709-740.
  - _quality:_ Brittle and noisy: matches thousand-separated amounts ('123 456 789,00 EUR'), reference numbers, and table figures; every Luhn-passing match fires a sequential 10s-timeout HTTP request, burning the API's rate limit and leaking document numbers to an external service. The only fraud value is the closed-company flag, which inherits the results[0] blind-trust bug.
- **Company closed/inactive check (EXTERNAL_COMPANY_CLOSED)** — If the matched etablissement's etat_administratif != 'A', status is set to 'closed' and a high flag (30 pts) is raised. Applied on all three SIRET/SIREN/potential-SIREN paths. external.py:202, 636-646, 697-707, 729-740.
  - _quality:_ Conceptually good (invoices from ceased companies are a real fraud pattern) but implemented at the wrong granularity: it conflates 'this establishment closed' (company relocated -> old head-office SIRET is closed but the company is alive) with 'company ceased'. It also never compares closure date to the document date, and a missing etat_administratif field silently maps to 'closed'.
- **Company name vs registry match (EXTERNAL_COMPANY_NAME_MISMATCH)** — If extracted_company_name is supplied, compares it to the registry name via normalize_company_name (strip legal forms SA/SAS/SARL/etc., punctuation, case) + word-set Jaccard similarity >= 0.8. Mismatch -> high flag (30 pts). external.py:474-549, 648-660.
  - _quality:_ DEAD CODE in production: analyzer.py:172-176 never passes extracted_company_name. Even if wired, Jaccard-on-words at 0.8 fails on acronyms ('EDF' vs 'ELECTRICITE DE FRANCE' -> similarity 0) and on any name where one token differs out of three (2/3 = 0.67 < 0.8), so it would be extremely false-positive-prone.
- **EU VAT verification via VIES (EXTERNAL_VAT_INVALID / EXTERNAL_VAT_VERIFICATION_FAILED)** — For each checksum-valid French VAT, GETs https://ec.europa.eu/taxation_customs/vies/rest-api/ms/{CC}/vat/{num}, with one 1s-sleep retry on isValid=false (VIES false-negative mitigation). isValid=false -> high flag (30 pts); transport errors -> low flag (5 pts). external.py:290-353, 742-769.
  - _quality:_ DEAD CODE in production: verify_vat defaults to False and no caller enables it. Implementation bug: any non-200 response returns {'valid': False} with error=None (external.py:346), which the caller treats as a confirmed-invalid VAT (high severity) instead of a verification failure. Ignores VIES userError/MS_UNAVAILABLE semantics.
- **IBAN verification via anyapi.io (EXTERNAL_IBAN_INVALID / EXTERNAL_IBAN_VERIFICATION_FAILED)** — If an API key is configured (st.secrets/env IBAN_API_KEY), every IBAN extracted by content.extract_iban is sent to https://anyapi.io/api/v1/iban with the key as a query parameter. valid=false -> high flag (30 pts); errors -> low flag (5 pts). Bank name resolved from a 24-entry hardcoded BANK_CODES table via _resolve_bank_name. external.py:363-467, 771-816.
  - _quality:_ Almost entirely redundant: mod-97 checksum is already computed locally in content.validate_iban_checksum (content.py:1540), and the loop at external.py:776 ignores the local is_checksum_valid result, so even locally-invalid IBANs are shipped to a third-party commercial API (privacy-sensitive bank data + double-penalty with the content module). The only net-new information is the BIC, most of which is then discarded by the tiny, partly-wrong BANK_CODES table.
- **Module scoring + confidence (external)** — score = 100 - sum(severity points: low 5 / medium 15 / high 30 / critical 50), floored at 0. confidence = 0.1 if no verification attempted, 1.0 if all succeeded, else 0.5 + 0.5 * success_ratio. Verified companies/IBANs serialized into details for the UI. external.py:556-561, 818-854.
  - _quality:_ Confidence conflates 'the API call worked' with 'the document checked out': a lookup that conclusively proves a VAT invalid does NOT increment verifications_successful, so a genuine fraud finding lowers the module's own weight in the final average. Network failures both add flags (score penalty) and lower confidence — double-counting analyzer-side problems as document risk.
- **Document type classification (document_type.py)** — Joins all pages, lowercases, counts distinct-keyword substring presence across 8 categories (invoice, identity, certificate, attestation, tax_document, technical, scientific, legal_notice). Winner needs >= 3 hits else 'unknown'. Output drives scoring.CONFIDENCE_MULTIPLIERS (e.g. scientific: fonts x0.15, structure x0.25, content x0.20, images x0.5), the identity-module trigger, and whether flag caps apply (scoring.py:270-305). document_type.py:120-167.
  - _quality:_ Deliberately simple and explainable, but brittle: raw substring matching (no word boundaries, no whitespace normalization), tie-breaking silently resolves by dict insertion order (invoice first) contradicting the 'Tie -> unknown' comment at line 31, and the min-score comment (line 33 says 2) contradicts the code (line 164 requires 3). No confidence output, no runner-up margin requirement. Because its output down-weights tamper-detection modules, it is a security-relevant component treated as a convenience heuristic.

**Weaknesses:**
- external.py:181-184 + 191: SIREN-prefix fallback treats a SIRET as verified when only the SIREN exists — matching_etablissement falls back to the siege, so a SIRET whose establishment (NIC) does not exist at all is reported as a verified company with head-office data; the nonexistent establishment is never flagged.
- external.py:177-178: the 'keep looking for exact SIRET match' loop is broken — once the SIREN fallback sets matching_etablissement (line 183), the very next company iteration hits 'if matching_etablissement: break', so the search for an exact establishment match stops after at most one more result.
- external.py:252: verify_siren_annuaire uses results[0] from a free-text search without verifying results[0]['siren'] == siren — the 'verified' company (name, status, address shown in the UI and used for the closed-company flag) can be an unrelated entity.
- external.py:202 and 263: status = 'active' if etat_administratif == 'A' else 'closed' — a missing/None field maps to 'closed', so an API schema change or a partial record produces a high-severity EXTERNAL_COMPANY_CLOSED flag out of nothing. Also conflates establishment closure with company cessation.
- external.py:618-623 and 679-684: 'not found' in a full-text search API is treated as CRITICAL (50 pts). The recherche-entreprises API excludes non-diffusible companies (a large share of micro-entrepreneurs) and lags new registrations, so this severity is unjustifiable for a search miss.
- external.py:625-630, 686-691, 753-758, 782-787: network/timeout/429 errors add low-severity flags against the DOCUMENT — the trust score of the file drops because the analyzer's internet was flaky. Analyzer-side failure should affect confidence only, never score.
- external.py:346: verify_vat_vies returns ({'valid': False, 'reason': 'VIES lookup failed'}, None) on ANY non-200 status; the caller at 759-769 then raises EXTERNAL_VAT_INVALID (high) — VIES downtime is reported as a fraudulent VAT number. It also ignores VIES REST userError/MS_UNAVAILABLE fields.
- external.py:84: extract_potential_sirens pattern \b(\d{3})\s+(\d{3})\s+(\d{3})\b matches space-grouped amounts, reference numbers, and phone-adjacent digits; ~10% pass Luhn, each firing a sequential HTTP call (10s timeout each, no session reuse, no cache, no backoff) — slow, rate-limit-burning, and it leaks arbitrary document numbers to an external API.
- external.py:776: the IBAN loop ignores is_checksum_valid from extract_iban — locally-invalid IBANs are still sent to anyapi.io and then double-flagged (content module + EXTERNAL_IBAN_INVALID high).
- external.py:404: bic_data.get('bankCode', '').upper() crashes with AttributeError if the API returns bankCode: null (default only applies when the key is absent); _resolve_bank_name is called outside any try/except at line 798, and analyzer.py does not wrap module calls, so one odd API response can kill the whole analysis.
- external.py:371 and 376: BANK_CODES data errors — 'TRZO' is Treezor (a BaaS used by Qonto/Lydia), not Wise (Wise BICs start with TRWI); table mixes 4-char bank codes with 8-char BICs and covers ~24 banks, so bank_name is None for most real-world IBANs.
- external.py:446-449: the anyapi.io API key is sent as a URL query parameter (ends up in proxy/server logs), and the full IBAN — sensitive personal banking data extracted from the document — is sent to a third-party commercial service, the weakest point relative to the project's locality constraint; the full IBAN is also stored unmasked in details['verified_ibans'] (line 794) while flags carefully mask it.
- external.py:649-660 dead code: extracted_company_name is never passed by any caller (analyzer.py:172-176), so EXTERNAL_COMPANY_NAME_MISMATCH can never fire; external.py:742-769 dead code: verify_vat defaults to False and is never enabled; app.py:137 meanwhile hardcodes enable_external = True despite the module docstring saying it should be optional.
- external.py:512-549: company_names_match uses word-set Jaccard >= 0.8 — acronym vs full name scores 0, and dropping/adding one word in a 3-word name scores 0.67; if this check is ever wired up as-is it will misfire constantly. normalize_company_name's legal-form list (496-498) also misses SASU, EIRL, SELARL, SC, etc.
- external.py:824-830: confidence formula counts a conclusive negative result (VAT proven invalid) as an UNSUCCESSFUL verification, reducing the module's weight exactly when it found fraud; there is no notion of coverage vs outcome.
- document_type.py:151: raw substring matching without word boundaries — 'api' matches inside 'capital', 'rest' inside 'prestation'/'restant', 'avoir' is a ubiquitous French verb, 'table' matches 'comptable', 'figure' matches 'figurer' — inflating technical/scientific/invoice scores on unrelated French documents.
- document_type.py:141: full_text keeps intra-page newlines ('total\nttc'), so multi-word keywords like 'total ttc', 'carte nationale d'identité', 'je soussigné' silently fail whenever the PDF text layer breaks lines mid-phrase; keywords also use ASCII apostrophes while real French PDFs typically use U+2019, so identity/attestation keywords can systematically miss.
- document_type.py:31-33 vs 155-164: comments contradict the code — 'Tie -> unknown' is false (max() resolves ties by dict insertion order, so 'invoice' silently wins any tie), and 'Minimum score to classify: 2' is false (code requires 3).
- document_type.py:120-167: the classifier returns a bare label with no confidence/margin, yet scoring.py:60-100 uses it to multiply tamper-module confidence down to 0.15-0.5 and analyzer.py:157-166 uses it to gate the identity module and (scoring.py:305) whether flag caps apply — a low-signal heuristic silently controls how strictly tampering evidence is weighed.

**False Positive Risks:**
- Legitimate invoice from a non-diffusible micro-entrepreneur (statut de diffusion restreinte — very common for auto-entrepreneurs): the recherche-entreprises search returns no results -> EXTERNAL_SIRET_NOT_FOUND at critical severity (-50), likely pushing an authentic invoice into HIGH/CRITICAL risk.
- Invoice bearing the SIRET of a company's former head office after a relocation: that establishment's etat_administratif is no longer 'A' while the company is fully active -> EXTERNAL_COMPANY_CLOSED (high, -30). Same for a legitimate archival invoice from a company that has since ceased trading — no comparison of closure date vs invoice date.
- Invoice from a company registered days ago (registry indexing lag) or during an API outage window: 'not found' -> critical flag on a genuine document.
- Analysis run offline or behind a corporate proxy: every labeled SIRET/SIREN/IBAN collects a low VERIFICATION_FAILED flag (-5 each); a normal invoice with SIRET + SIREN + IBAN loses 15 points for the analyzer's connectivity, not for anything in the document.
- If VAT verification is ever enabled: VIES returning HTTP 500/302 or a member-state DB being unavailable is converted to {'valid': False} (external.py:346) -> EXTERNAL_VAT_INVALID high flag on a perfectly valid VAT number (VIES is notoriously flaky, which the code itself acknowledges).
- A financial table containing space-grouped figures like '552 081 317,00' that coincidentally pass Luhn and correspond to a real but closed company's SIREN -> EXTERNAL_COMPANY_CLOSED high flag via the potential-SIREN path, on a document that never claimed any relationship with that company.
- Valid IBAN from a newer neobank or a country poorly covered by anyapi.io's dataset returning valid:false -> EXTERNAL_IBAN_INVALID (high) despite the local mod-97 checksum passing.
- If company-name matching is ever wired: any legitimate invoice using a trade name or acronym ('EDF', 'Canal+', 'Free') vs the registry's legal name ('ELECTRICITE DE FRANCE', 'GROUPE CANAL+ SA') fails Jaccard 0.8 -> high mismatch flag.
- Document-type misses re-creating the FPs the multiplier system was built to prevent: a real scientific paper whose keywords are split across line breaks, or a French attestation using curly apostrophes (U+2019) in 'je soussigné'/'d'identité', classifies as 'unknown' -> no confidence multipliers -> the fonts/structure modules penalize its many fonts and revisions at full weight.
- A long business contract or report that happens to contain 3+ invoice keywords ('tva', 'iban', 'échéance') gets classified 'invoice' and subjected to full invoice-calibrated scrutiny (missing legal mentions, date logic), lowering the score of a legitimate non-invoice.

**False Negative Gaps:**
- Identifier-borrowing fraud (the dominant real-world pattern): a fake invoice using a REAL company's SIRET/SIREN/VAT passes every external check — the module verifies that identifiers EXIST, never that the document originates from that company. With all verifications successful, confidence hits 1.0 and the module contributes a perfect 100 to the weighted average, actively boosting the fraudulent document's trust score.
- BEC/IBAN-swap fraud: a genuine supplier invoice with only the IBAN replaced by the attacker's (checksum-valid, real bank) account passes IBAN verification with valid:true; there is no cross-check between IBAN country/bank and the issuing company (French company + Lithuanian EMI IBAN raises no flag), which is precisely the classic payment-redirect scenario.
- SIREN + fabricated NIC: an attacker takes a real company's SIREN and appends a fake establishment number that passes the SIRET Luhn check; the SIREN-prefix fallback (external.py:181-191) reports it as a verified company using siege data instead of flagging the nonexistent establishment.
- Company name spoofing: the document loudly claims 'EDF' but carries the SIRET of an unrelated shell company that exists in the registry — no flag, because EXTERNAL_COMPANY_NAME_MISMATCH is dead code (extracted_company_name never passed) and VIES name/address comparison is a TODO comment (external.py:762).
- Keyword-stuffing to disarm tamper detection: since document_type counts substrings in document-controlled text, an attacker adds hidden/white 4pt text with 'abstract methodology references doi: proceedings peer-reviewed' -> classified 'scientific' -> scoring multiplies fonts x0.15, structure x0.25, content x0.20, images x0.5 — the exact modules that would catch text edits, incremental updates, and pasted images are nearly silenced. Same trick with identity keywords also changes flag-cap behavior (scoring.py:305).
- Fraud on non-French/EU documents: extract_french_vat/extract_siret only cover French formats, so a fraudulent German, Belgian, or UK invoice triggers zero external verification; the module quietly self-neutralizes (confidence 0.1) with no flag such as 'invoice claims EU origin but no verifiable identifier found'.
- Scanned/flattened fraud: rasterizing a tampered invoice removes the text layer, so no identifiers are extracted, no external checks run (confidence 0.1), and the classifier returns 'unknown' — the whole module is evaded by the simplest laundering step.
- Checksum-valid but unregistered VAT numbers are never caught in production because verify_vat is permanently disabled (the FR key formula over any Luhn-valid SIREN produces checksum-valid VATs the content module accepts).
- Companies in liquidation/redressement judiciaire: etat_administratif stays 'A' during proceedings, so an invoice from a company in bankruptcy proceedings — a known fraud vector — passes the closed-company check.
- Rate-limit blindness on the potential-SIREN path: non-'not found' errors (429s from the API's ~7 req/s limit) are silently swallowed (external.py:720-723), so on documents with many number patterns the closed-company check silently stops running with no signal that coverage collapsed.
- No temporal cross-check: company creation_date is fetched (external.py:204) but never compared to the document date — an invoice dated before the issuing company legally existed (a strong, cheap fraud signal) raises nothing.

**Improvement Ideas:**
- Replace free-text search with exact lookups and verify identity of the response: require result['siren'] == queried SIREN on every path (fixes external.py:252 results[0] blind trust), keep the exact-establishment walk but fix the premature break at external.py:177-178, and explicitly flag 'SIREN exists but establishment NIC does not' as its own medium/high signal instead of silently falling back to the siege.
- Recalibrate severities around API reality: downgrade SIRET/SIREN 'not found' from critical to medium (non-diffusible companies, indexing lag), stop flagging network errors entirely (lower confidence instead — zero score impact), and split EXTERNAL_COMPANY_CLOSED into 'establishment closed, company active' (low/info) vs 'legal unit ceased before document date' (high) by comparing date_cessation/closure to the document date.
- Add temporal consistency checks with data already fetched: flag documents dated before company creation_date or after cessation — cheap, high-precision fraud signals currently discarded.
- Make the external module produce explicit positive-trust signals (project goal #2): an INFO-level 'company verified: name matches, active at document date, address matches document footer' flag, feeding a trust bonus — and make confidence reflect coverage (how many identifiers could be checked) separately from outcome, so a conclusive fraud finding never reduces the module's weight (fixes external.py:824-830).
- Drop the anyapi.io IBAN call: mod-97 is already local (content.py:1540); ship the Banque de France CIB/bank-code file (public data) for local BIC/bank-name resolution, and add the actually-valuable check — IBAN country/bank vs issuing-company country mismatch (BEC redirect detection). This removes the PII leak, the query-string API key, and the None-crash at external.py:404 in one move.
- Wire up the dead checks: pass a company name extracted from the document header/template module into analyze_external, replace Jaccard with rapidfuzz token_set_ratio plus an acronym/initialism handler, compare against both nom_complet and nom_raison_sociale; enable verify_vat (VIES is free) after fixing external.py:346 to treat non-200/MS_UNAVAILABLE as verification-failure, and compare the VIES-returned name/address to the document.
- Constrain extract_potential_sirens: require a legal-context anchor within ±60 chars (RCS, SIREN, SIRET, TVA, 'capital', 'immatriculée') and reject matches followed by decimal separators or currency symbols; add an on-disk cache keyed by identifier, a shared requests.Session, a per-document cap on external calls, and backoff on 429.
- document_type: normalize before matching (collapse whitespace including newlines, map U+2019 to ASCII apostrophe, strip soft hyphens) and match keywords with word-boundary regexes — this alone fixes 'api'-in-'capital', 'rest'-in-'prestation', 'avoir'-as-verb, and the broken multi-word keywords.
- document_type: weight keywords by distinctiveness ('facture'/'avis d'imposition' worth 3, 'table'/'introduction' worth 0.5), require the winner to beat the runner-up by a margin (else 'unknown'), return (type, confidence, scores) instead of a bare label, and make the comments match the code (tie behavior, threshold).
- Treat classification as adversary-controlled: never let document text reduce the confidence of structure-level tamper evidence — incremental updates, ghost objects, and signature breaks are format facts independent of genre, so remove 'structure' from CONFIDENCE_MULTIPLIERS (scoring.py:60-100) or floor its multiplier near 1.0; alternatively, cross-check that classification keywords are actually visible when rendered (font size/color) before applying any multiplier, so hidden-text keyword stuffing cannot disarm scrutiny.
- Add an explicit 'no verifiable identifiers' signal for documents that claim to be French invoices (classifier says invoice, content finds amounts/TVA mentions) but expose no SIRET/SIREN at all — legally required on French invoices and currently a silent confidence-0.1 no-op.
- Harden response parsing: validate the annuaire JSON shape (siren present, etat_administratif in {'A','C',...}), map missing status to 'unknown' rather than 'closed' (external.py:202), and wrap the whole per-identifier verification in try/except so one malformed API response degrades to a verification-failure instead of crashing the analysis.

---

### CODE AUDIT — Signal aggregation: scoring.py (weighted average, confidence multipliers, flag caps), summary.py (verdict/bullets/positive signals), models.py (data contracts), analyzer.py (module orchestration + document-type override)

**Checks:**
- **Confidence-weighted average (calculate_final_score)** — final = Σ(score_i × weight_i × confidence_i × type_multiplier_i) / Σ(weight_i × confidence_i × type_multiplier_i), clamped 0-100 (scoring.py:137-183). Module weights hardcoded: metadata 1.0, content 1.2, visual 0.8, fonts 0.9, images 0.8, forensics 0.9, structure 1.3, external 1.5, identity 1.5; unknown modules default 1.0 (scoring.py:25-38). Empty module list or zero total weight returns 100 ('assume innocent').
  - _quality:_ Sound arithmetic but structurally naive: a pure average lets 8 clean modules bury one module that found real fraud (structure score 0, weight 1.3, among ~9.5 total weight → final ≈87 = LOW). Fails open (returns 100) when nothing could be analyzed. Weight values have no calibration basis; 'twod_doc' is absent from the table because its results never reach scoring at all.
- **Document-type confidence multipliers (CONFIDENCE_MULTIPLIERS)** — Per-type per-module weight reducers to suppress invoice-calibrated false positives: technical {structure 0.20, content 0.30, fonts 0.40}, scientific {fonts 0.15, structure 0.25, content 0.20, images 0.50}, legal_notice {fonts/structure/content 0.30}, identity {fonts 0.3, content 0.4, structure 0.5}; invoice/certificate/attestation/tax_document/unknown get none (scoring.py:60-100). Feeds calculate_final_score as a third weight factor.
  - _quality:_ Reasonable FP mitigation idea, but symmetric: it silences true positives just as much as false positives (a mid-line font edit in a 'scientific' doc is weighted at 0.15). Magic numbers with no calibration data. Whole-module silencing instead of per-flag-code suppression is too coarse. Depends entirely on a trivially gameable keyword classifier (document_type.py).
- **Risk level thresholds (get_risk_level)** — score ≥80 → LOW, 50-79 → MEDIUM, 20-49 → HIGH, <20 → CRITICAL (scoring.py:107-130). Hardcoded bands.
  - _quality:_ Simple and fine in isolation, but not the single source of truth: create_analysis_result later mutates trust_score below the band matching the assigned risk_level, and app.py:1191 adds its own separate 'TRUSTED' label at score ≥95, creating three inconsistent taxonomies.
- **Single-critical hard cap** — If any critical flag exists (and caps apply for this doc type): risk elevated LOW/MEDIUM → HIGH, trust_score capped at 40, then reduced −5 per high flag and −2 per medium flag, floored at 5 (scoring.py:307-318).
  - _quality:_ Good instinct (prevents averaging from burying a critical), but binary and severe: a single ubiquitous critical like META_DOCUMENT_MODIFIED (fired for ANY ModDate > CreationDate + 2s, metadata.py:305-340 called from structure.py:1776) drives every touched-but-legitimate invoice to ≤40/HIGH. Can also produce score 5 with risk 'HIGH' (score band says CRITICAL) — score/risk contradiction shown side by side in the UI.
- **Double-critical cap** — ≥2 critical flags → risk forced to CRITICAL and trust_score capped at 19 (scoring.py:320-323). No dedup: two flags with the same code, or the same underlying cause found by two modules, both count.
  - _quality:_ Brittle: counts raw flags, not independent evidence. Combined with the flag_counts-before-downgrade bug (see weaknesses), a benign scanner-pipeline flag that was explicitly deemed a false positive still counts toward the ≥2 escalation.
- **Cap-skip for secondary document types** — For document types in {scientific, legal_notice, technical}, hard caps are skipped entirely — critical flags cannot cap the score; only the (already reduced) weighted average applies (scoring.py:263-265, 304-305). unknown and all primary types keep caps.
  - _quality:_ Dangerous design: the strongest defensive mechanism (caps) is disabled based on a keyword-count classifier over attacker-controlled text. Combined with the multipliers this is a double weakening on exactly the doc types an attacker can steer classification toward.
- **Identity scanner-pipeline exemption + severity downgrade** — For doc_type=identity only: a critical flag with code META_DOCUMENT_MODIFIED and details.time_difference_hours < 0.5 (30 min) is treated as a benign scanner-then-viewer workflow — excluded from 'genuine criticals' (caps apply only if ≥1 genuine remains) and mutated in place from severity 'critical' to 'low' (scoring.py:270-303). Missing details defaults hours to 999 (treated as genuine).
  - _quality:_ Thoughtful FP fix but implemented with side effects: mutates the caller's Flag objects (non-idempotent if create_analysis_result runs twice on the same results); helper takes an unused ModuleResult param; float(hours) can raise on malformed details; the 30-minute threshold is arbitrary and, since metadata dates are attacker-controlled, the exemption is forgeable.
- **Flag collection / sorting / counting** — collect_all_flags flattens all module flags and sorts critical>high>medium>low (scoring.py:186-207); count_flags_by_severity tallies per severity (scoring.py:210-226). Counts feed the caps.
  - _quality:_ Works, but the comment 'Add module name to flag for context' (scoring.py:200) does nothing — module attribution is lost downstream, so the summary/UI cannot say which module found what. No deduplication of semantically identical flags before counting for caps.
- **Document type classification (detect_document_type)** — Lowercased full text scanned for hardcoded keyword lists per type; +1 per distinct keyword present; max wins; winner needs ≥3 hits else 'unknown'; no-text → 'unknown' (document_type.py:120-167). Result drives multipliers, cap-skipping, and identity-specific handling.
  - _quality:_ Fast and explainable, but adversarially trivial: 'technical' keywords include 'api', 'json', 'request', 'response', 'schema', 'authentication', 'payload' (document_type.py:81-93) — seven generic words reachable via invisible text. Docstring/comment says 'Tie → unknown' (document_type.py:31) but max() silently returns the first dict key on ties — behavior contradicts documentation. Real invoices with CGV footers ('article 1', 'article 2', 'conformément', 'en vigueur') can drift toward legal_notice.
- **Identity module trigger + doc_type override (analyzer)** — Identity/MRZ module runs if classifier said 'identity', OR doc is 'unknown' AND is_likely_identity_doc (keywords or face photo at 100 DPI) passes; in the latter case doc_type is overridden to 'identity' so identity multipliers and the scanner exemption apply (analyzer.py:151-167).
  - _quality:_ Pragmatic fix for text-less scans, but the override widens the attack surface: any image-only PDF containing a face photo inherits reduced content/structure/fonts weight plus the 30-min META_DOCUMENT_MODIFIED exemption.
- **Module execution & confidence protocol** — Analyzer always runs metadata, content, visual, fonts, images, structure, forensics; identity conditionally; external only if enabled (analyzer.py:116-176). Modules self-report confidence 0.1-1.0 based mostly on data availability (e.g. content 0.3 when no dates found, images 0.5 with no images, structure fixed 0.95). Failed analysis → score 100 with low confidence.
  - _quality:_ Confidence conflates 'how much data existed' with 'how reliable the verdict is'. Modules that fail or find nothing return score=100 + low confidence, which still nudges the average toward trust rather than abstaining. structure's fixed 0.95 confidence is asserted, not measured.
- **Verdict selection (summary)** — Flags grouped into themes via FLAG_THEME_MAP (summary.py:20-110); dominant theme = highest severity-weighted sum among {origin, tampering, dates, identity, security} only (summary.py:685-714); verdict picked from per-risk-level template tables EN/FR (summary.py:574-624, 717-735).
  - _quality:_ Clean template design, but the theme map is incomplete: every MRZ_* code (identity module) and FORENSICS_ELA_* code falls into 'other', which is excluded from dominant-theme selection — a document with cryptographically failed MRZ check digits gets a generic verdict instead of the identity-fraud one.
- **Bullet generation (_build_bullets)** — One pass over themes in fixed order [tampering, dates, identity, origin, security, visual, images, fonts, other]; skips low-severity flags; dedupes by flag code; max 2 bullets per theme via an O(n²) membership expression (summary.py:763-820). Falls back to flag.message when no template (summary.py:738-760).
  - _quality:_ Mostly works, but theme 'signature' is absent from theme_order, so STRUCT_SIGNATURE_INVALID (high, structure.py:1301-1306) and STRUCT_SIGNATURE_NOT_TRUSTED never surface as bullets; the 'skip signature' branch at summary.py:799-800 is dead code. Unmapped codes (MRZ_*, FORENSICS_*) appear last and untranslated in French mode. The 2-per-theme limiter (summary.py:817) is a convoluted list-comprehension recount.
- **Positive trust signals (_get_positive_signals + POSITIVE_SIGNALS)** — Intended to emit up to 2 'clean module' sentences (module score ≥90 and zero flags) plus a special sentence for an EU-trusted signature (summary.py:633-656, 823-879).
  - _quality:_ DEAD CODE — generate_rich_summary (summary.py:886-920) never calls it, and app.py imports only generate_rich_summary/get_flag_sentence (app.py:812). Positive trust signals therefore do not exist anywhere in the user-facing output OR the score. Even if wired up: 'score ≥90 and no flags' would praise a module that analyzed nothing (content returns 100/conf 0.3 when no dates found → 'All dates and references are consistent'); the has_negative_flags test (857-859) contains a tautological 'theme in grouped' clause; POSITIVE_SIGNALS lacks forensics/identity entries.
- **Signature/2D-Doc handling relative to scoring** — STRUCT_SIGNATURE_TRUSTED excuses exactly 1 incremental update inside the structure module (structure.py:1308-1309); 2D-Doc barcodes are scanned separately in app.py and rendered as a display panel only (app.py:906-916, 2062+), never passed to create_analysis_result.
  - _quality:_ The two strongest cryptographic signals in the system have near-zero influence on the aggregate: a valid EU signature grants no score bonus; a 2D-Doc whose signed payload contradicts the printed document does not lower the trust score at all.
- **Legacy wrappers (generate_summary, quick_analyze)** — generate_summary concatenates verdict + bullets into one English-only string (scoring.py:339-355); quick_analyze returns {score, risk_level, flag_count incl. low flags} (analyzer.py:212-239).
  - _quality:_ Functional; generate_summary ignores language and duplicates generate_rich_summary; harmless but drifting API surface.

**Weaknesses:**
- Stale flag_counts vs downgrade ordering (scoring.py:267-268 vs 300-323): flag_counts is computed BEFORE the identity scanner-pipeline downgrade, so a critical explicitly deemed a false positive still counts in flag_counts['critical']. An identity doc with 1 genuine critical + 1 exempted scanner-pipeline critical hits the '≥2 criticals' branch (scoring.py:320) → forced CRITICAL/≤19, directly contradicting the code comment 'The score cap already ignores them'.
- Fail-open on undecidable input (scoring.py:160-161, 179-180): empty module list or zero effective weight returns 100/LOW — an unanalyzable document is presented as fully trustworthy instead of 'could not analyze'.
- In-place mutation of input Flag objects (scoring.py:300-303): create_analysis_result rewrites f.severity='critical'→'low' on the caller's ModuleResults; non-idempotent (a second scoring pass sees different inputs), and any code holding references (tests, UI caching in st.session_state) observes silently changed data. Helper _is_scanner_pipeline_critical takes an unused parameter r (scoring.py:280) and float(hours) at scoring.py:287 can raise ValueError on a non-numeric details value, crashing the whole analysis.
- Score/risk inconsistency (scoring.py:307-318): after a single critical, trust_score is decremented (−5/high, −2/medium, floor 5) but risk_level stays 'HIGH'; a displayed score of 5 belongs to the CRITICAL band per get_risk_level. app.py:1191 adds a third, unrelated 'TRUSTED' threshold at ≥95.
- High/medium flags never cap anything on their own (scoring.py:307): the deductions only execute inside the 'critical ≥1' branch. Six high-severity flags spread across modules produce zero cap — only the diluted average applies.
- Cap-skip is keyed on an adversarially controllable classifier (scoring.py:265 + document_type.py:81-113): 'technical' matches on generic words ('api', 'json', 'request', 'response', 'schema', 'payload', 'authentication'); 3 keyword hits suffice; max() tie-break contradicts the documented 'tie → unknown' behavior (document_type.py:31 vs 155).
- Dead code — positive signals never emitted (summary.py:823-879): _get_positive_signals is called by nothing (verified via grep of app.py and src/); AnalysisSummary's docstring (models.py:129-130) claims bullets include positive signals — false. The project's stated goal 2 (confirm legitimate files) is unimplemented at the aggregation layer.
- Signature theme unreachable in bullets (summary.py:785-800): 'signature' is missing from theme_order, so an INVALID digital signature (high severity) never appears in the summary; the skip-signature branch is dead code.
- Incomplete FLAG_THEME_MAP (summary.py:20-110): all MRZ_* codes (≈15 codes, identity.py:533-1030) and FORENSICS_ELA_* codes map to 'other' → excluded from dominant-theme verdict selection (summary.py:701), ordered last in bullets, and shown untranslated in French mode via the flag.message fallback.
- collect_all_flags loses module attribution (scoring.py:198-201): comment says 'Add module name to flag for context' but the loop just appends the flag unchanged; downstream consumers cannot tell which module produced a flag.
- Duplicated severity-point tables: SEVERITY_POINTS is redefined per module (structure.py:1734-1739, metadata.py, etc.) and again as severity_weight in summary.py:698 — drift risk when tuning.
- UI group bars diverge from the global formula (app.py:1236): group scores use weight × confidence but omit the document-type multipliers, so per-category bars can scream red while the global score, with the same modules silenced, stays green (or vice versa).
- Cross-concern coupling: check_dates lives in metadata.py but is invoked only by the structure module (structure.py:40, 1776), so the identity-type 'structure: 0.5' multiplier also halves the date-tampering signal's weight — a doc-type tweak aimed at PDF-structure noise silently discounts metadata-date evidence.
- analyzer.py:104-105 rejects any non-.pdf suffix despite the project claiming image support; suffix check is extension-based, not content-based (a renamed .pdf passes to fitz).
- Hardcoded, uncalibrated constants throughout: MODULE_WEIGHTS (scoring.py:25-35), CONFIDENCE_MULTIPLIERS (scoring.py:60-100), risk bands (scoring.py:123-130), cap values 40/19/5 and per-flag deductions (scoring.py:311-323), 30-minute scanner window (scoring.py:287) — none configurable, none justified by measured FP/FN rates on the samples/ corpus.

**False Positive Risks:**
- Any legitimate invoice whose ModDate is >2 seconds after CreationDate — ERP generates the PDF, then an e-invoicing platform, archiving system, DocuSign stamp, or the sender simply opening and re-saving in Acrobat updates ModDate. structure (via metadata.check_dates, metadata.py:305-340) emits a CRITICAL META_DOCUMENT_MODIFIED; the single-critical cap (scoring.py:307-312) forces the score to ≤40/HIGH regardless of 8 other clean modules. The 30-minute exemption exists ONLY for identity docs — invoices get zero tolerance.
- A legitimate scanned ID processed in a batch workflow where OCR/indexing software touches the file 35+ minutes after scanning: exceeds the 30-minute scanner-pipeline window (scoring.py:287) → genuine critical → capped, and if a second benign critical exists (e.g. far-future card expiry date parsed as content), the stale flag_counts bug escalates to CRITICAL/≤19.
- A real attestation or insurance certificate stating a validity period ending years ahead ('valable jusqu'au 01/2031'): CONTENT_FAR_FUTURE_DATE is critical-severity in content.py → hard cap to ≤40 even though future validity dates are the norm for this document class (and 'attestation'/'certificate' types get no confidence multipliers — scoring.py:97-98).
- A small-business invoice genuinely made with Canva or an AI-assisted invoice generator: META_AI_GENERATED / online-converter flags are critical/high with early-return (metadata.py:218-247); one critical → capped at 40 → 'likely fraudulent' verdict for a real, if informal, invoice. CLAUDE.md explicitly says 'flag but don't over-penalize' online converters — the cap does the opposite.
- A legitimate invoice whose CGV footer contains 'article 1', 'article 2', 'conformément', 'en vigueur' can out-score the invoice keyword set and classify as legal_notice — not itself a flagging FP, but its structure/content/fonts findings get silenced (0.30) and any real flags become unexplainable in a doc the user knows is an invoice; conversely a sparse scanned invoice classifies as 'unknown' and keeps full caps, so IMAGES_IMAGE_ONLY_PDF + a metadata critical push authentic phone-scanned invoices into HIGH.
- A fully clean, EU-signature-trusted document with two incidental medium flags (e.g. system fonts + no producer) never gets a positive counterweight: the trusted signature adds nothing to the score (only excuses one incremental update inside structure) and _get_positive_signals is dead code — the doc lands mid-MEDIUM with a 'needs verification' verdict.
- MEDIUM-risk documents whose only flags are low-severity: _build_bullets skips low flags (summary.py:809-810), so the UI shows a verdict claiming 'issues that need verification' with an empty bullet list — the user is alarmed with no stated reason.

**False Negative Gaps:**
- Whole-file rewrite laundering: an attacker edits an invoice then re-prints it through a clean generator (headless Chrome print-to-PDF, LibreOffice export, qpdf). Result: single revision, ModDate==CreationDate, embedded fonts, clean structure. Every module returns ~100 and the weighted average (scoring.py:166-183) yields LOW. Nothing in the aggregation layer models 'absence of history is not proof of authenticity' — a pristine container earns maximal trust with no corroborating positive evidence required.
- Classifier gaming to disable caps: injecting invisible/white 1pt text with ~4 generic technical keywords ('api', 'json', 'request', 'response', 'schema'...) reclassifies the doc as 'technical' → hard caps skipped entirely (scoring.py:265) AND structure weight ×0.20, content ×0.30, fonts ×0.40 (scoring.py:61-65). A tampered invoice carrying two critical tampering flags can then average out to MEDIUM or even LOW instead of forced CRITICAL/≤19.
- Metadata forgery defeats the identity exemption: metadata dates are attacker-controlled (exiftool one-liner). Setting CreationDate = ModDate − 10min on a tampered ID scan makes _is_scanner_pipeline_critical true (scoring.py:280-287) → the tampering critical is downgraded to 'low' and excluded from caps. Combined with the face-photo doc_type override (analyzer.py:157-167), any image-only forgery containing a face self-selects into the most lenient scoring regime.
- Evidence concentration dilution: fraud whose traces land in one module without a critical flag — e.g. fonts finds FONTS_MIDLINE_CHANGE (high) + FONTS_MIXED_SUBSETS (high), fonts score ≈40 — is averaged against ~8 clean modules (fonts weight only 0.9) → final ≈93, LOW. High flags trigger no cap (scoring.py:307), so a classic Paint-edited-amount attack detectable by the fonts module alone still reads 'appears legitimate'.
- Cross-module corroboration is invisible: three independent modules each finding one medium flag (metadata converter + structure deleted objects + images paste anomaly) is much stronger evidence than any one alone, but the average treats them as three mild dips (~85 each) → final ~MEDIUM-high. No mechanism raises severity when independent weak signals agree.
- 2D-Doc contradiction ignored by the score: a forged French tax document whose government-signed 2D-Doc payload disagrees with the printed amounts/name produces high-confidence mismatch flags in twod_doc (twod_doc.py:1546-1685) — but scan_pdf_for_2d_doc results are display-only in app.py (906-916, 2062+) and never enter create_analysis_result; trust_score and risk_level are unaffected by cryptographic proof of forgery.
- External verification fails open: with enable_external off (the default, analyzer.py:57) or API failure, EXTERNAL_* checks contribute nothing (confidence 0.1, external.py:826); a fabricated company with a checksum-valid but unregistered SIRET passes content's checksum test and is never penalized at aggregation time.
- Misclassified forged secondary docs are nearly unfloggable: a forged diploma/transcript that classifies as 'scientific' has fonts ×0.15, content ×0.20, structure ×0.25 AND no caps — even multiple critical findings mathematically cannot push the weighted average into HIGH unless visual/images/metadata simultaneously collapse.
- Sub-threshold severity flooding: an attacker keeping every individual signal at 'medium' (producer swap to a merely 'suspicious' tool, ≤10 deleted objects, moderate font count) stays under both cap triggers; per-module scores of ~70-85 average to ~80 → LOW, with each module's own SEVERITY_POINTS floor preventing accumulation across modules.

**Improvement Ideas:**
- Replace the pure weighted average with a two-tier aggregate: keep the average as the 'baseline', but bound it by the worst corroborated evidence, e.g. final = min(weighted_avg, worst_module_score + margin) or log-opinion pooling, so one module with strong findings cannot be outvoted by eight silent ones. Add cap triggers for accumulations of high/medium flags (e.g. ≥2 high → cap 55, ≥4 medium across ≥3 modules → cap 65), not just criticals.
- Fix the flag-cap pipeline ordering bug: perform the identity scanner-pipeline classification FIRST, produce a derived (not mutated) flag list with downgrades applied, THEN compute flag_counts and caps from that derived list (scoring.py:267-323). Make create_analysis_result pure — never mutate input Flag objects; attach a 'suppressed_reason' annotation instead so the UI can explain the downgrade.
- Fail closed on undecidable input: when module_results is empty or weight_total ≈ 0 (scoring.py:160-161, 179-180), return a distinct 'UNANALYZABLE' outcome (or trust_score=None) rather than 100/LOW; similarly make modules that found no data return an explicit 'abstain' marker instead of score=100 with low confidence, and exclude abstentions from the average.
- Apply document-type adjustments per flag CODE, not per module: maintain a table of FP-prone codes per type (e.g. suppress FONTS_HIGH_DIVERSITY for scientific, STRUCT_INCREMENTAL_UPDATES for legal) while leaving universally suspicious codes (FONTS_MIDLINE_CHANGE, IMAGES_PASTE_NOISE_ANOMALY, MRZ_* failures) at full weight for every type. Never skip hard caps based on the classifier — replace _skip_caps_types with per-code suppression plus a 'manual review' banner.
- Harden detect_document_type against gaming: require structural anchors per type (MRZ pattern for identity, amount+VAT+SIRET co-occurrence for invoice), score only text that is actually rendered visible (filter white/invisible/tiny text via spans' color and size from PyMuPDF), weight keyword hits by TF and page position, implement the documented tie→unknown behavior (document_type.py:155), and expose the classification + evidence in the result so users can contest it.
- Make cryptographic signals first-class in scoring: feed 2D-Doc scan results into create_analysis_result as a ModuleResult with weight ≥1.5 — signed-data mismatch → critical flag + cap; verified match → strong positive. Give STRUCT_SIGNATURE_TRUSTED an explicit bounded score bonus and targeted suppression of structural flags, instead of the current display-only treatment (app.py:906-916).
- Implement real positive trust signals in the SCORE, not just prose (project goal 2): a small additive bonus pool (capped, e.g. +10 total) for verified positives — valid MRZ check digits, checksum-valid + registry-confirmed SIRET, trusted signature, single-revision PDF from a known-good producer, fonts fully embedded. Then either wire _get_positive_signals into generate_rich_summary with a stricter cleanliness test (require confidence ≥0.7 so 'nothing analyzed' isn't praised) or delete the dead code (summary.py:823-879).
- Rework META_DOCUMENT_MODIFIED handling at aggregation: graduate severity by time gap and producer/creator pair for ALL document types (2s→low, hours→medium, days+editor-producer→high/critical), extend the scanner-pipeline reasoning beyond identity docs, and require corroboration (incremental update count >0 from structure) before letting a metadata-only date gap trigger the critical cap — metadata dates alone are forgeable in both directions.
- Restore consistency across surfaces: derive risk_level from the final post-cap trust_score in exactly one place; unify the UI 'TRUSTED ≥95' label (app.py:1191) with get_risk_level bands; include document-type multipliers in the app.py:1236 group-bar computation; centralize SEVERITY_POINTS in one module imported everywhere.
- Complete the summary layer: add all MRZ_* and FORENSICS_ELA_* codes to FLAG_THEME_MAP (map MRZ failures to 'identity', ELA to 'tampering') with EN/FR templates; add 'signature' to theme_order so invalid signatures produce bullets; actually attach module names in collect_all_flags (scoring.py:200) for drill-down; let _get_dominant_theme consider a mapped-in 'forensics' theme.
- Deduplicate before counting for caps: collapse flags with identical (code, key-details) emitted by multiple modules or repeated within a module, so the ≥2-critical escalation reflects independent evidence, not double-reporting.
- Calibrate everything against the samples/ corpus: move MODULE_WEIGHTS, CONFIDENCE_MULTIPLIERS, cap values, and risk bands into a config file; build a small evaluation harness (labelled legitimate vs tampered PDFs from the CLAUDE.md test-document list) that reports per-flag FP/FN rates and per-module score distributions, and tune thresholds from measurements instead of intuition.

---

### CODE AUDIT — src/modules/content.py — Module B: Content Analysis (date extraction/coherence, invoice reference consistency, French legal-mention validation; called from src/analyzer.py:125; extractors reused by src/modules/external.py and app.py)

**Checks:**
- **French date extraction (find_french_dates / parse_french_date, lines 59-128)** — Regex-extracts dates like '15 janvier 2024', '1er fév. 2024' using a hand-built FRENCH_MONTHS map. Extraction only — feeds all date checks. No severity itself.
  - _quality:_ Reasonable for French month-name dates. Matches on text.lower() then slices the original text by position, which assumes lower() preserves string length (true for French, not for all Unicode). Requires 'day month year' order; no 'month year' ('janvier 2024') or ISO support.
- **Numeric date extraction (find_numeric_dates, lines 131-193)** — Extracts DD/MM/YYYY, DD-MM-YYYY (with optional H:MM time) and DD/MM/YY. Two-digit years are always mapped to 2000+YY (line 181), so '99' becomes 2099. Extraction only.
  - _quality:_ Hard-assumes French day-first convention — US MM/DD documents are silently misparsed or dropped. The 2000+YY pivot turns any 1990s short date (e.g. an unlabeled birth date '12/05/85') into a far-future 2085 date that then trips a CRITICAL flag downstream. No ISO (YYYY-MM-DD), no dotted (15.01.2024), no month/year ('12/2024') support.
- **Abbreviated month-year extraction (find_abbreviated_month_dates, lines 196-235)** — Extracts 'Mar 23', 'Avr. 24' style dates (consumption graphs), defaulting day to 1. Extraction only.
  - _quality:_ Naive: pattern \b([A-Za-zéûô]{3})\.?\s+(\d{2})\b matches ANY 3-letter token followed by 2 digits — 'Mai 68' becomes 2068-05-01, 'NOV 85' becomes 2085-11-01 — and these synthetic dates flow into the far-future/very-old checks.
- **Date type classification (identify_date_type + DATE_CONTEXT_KEYWORDS, lines 260-454)** — Classifies each date as invoice/service/due/creation/document/reference_date/order by longest substring keyword found in the context (previous line + current line up to the date). Drives which coherence checks apply.
  - _quality:_ Substring matching with no word boundaries: 'du' (service) and ', le' (document) match nearly all French prose, 'dated' matches inside 'updated'. 'date d'émission' appears in BOTH invoice and creation lists (lines 267, 331) — tie broken by dict insertion order. The special-case city regex at line 436 ([A-ZÀ-Ü][a-zà-ÿ]+,\s*le\s*$) can NEVER fire because the context string built at lines 507-508 includes the date itself, so the context never ends with ', le' — dead branch (masked only because the generic ', le' keyword still classifies it).
- **CONTENT_FAR_FUTURE_DATE (check_impossible_dates, lines 588-606)** — Any extracted date > now + 365 days → CRITICAL (−50 pts). Skipped for reference_date-typed dates and if context contains a future-ok keyword (valable, expire, jusqu'au...).
  - _quality:_ Threshold hardcoded; the keyword escape depends on the label being within the 2-line/120-char context window, which fails in tabular layouts. Combined with the 2-digit-year pivot and the 3-letter-month over-matching, this critical flag fires on garbage extractions.
- **CONTENT_VERY_OLD_DATE (check_impossible_dates, lines 610-636)** — Any date < 2000-01-01 → MEDIUM (−15 pts). Skips reference_date type, birth keywords (né le, naissance...), and legal-reference keywords (loi, article, décret...).
