  - _quality:_ The 2000 cutoff is arbitrary; guard lists are decent but the keyword lists are hardcoded substrings ('l. ', 'r. ', 'code ') that both over- and under-match. Historic but legitimate dates (lease start on a rent receipt, marriage date) get flagged.
- **CONTENT_EXPIRED_DOCUMENT (check_impossible_dates, lines 639-653)** — Any date whose context contains a strict expiry keyword (valable jusqu, expire le, valid until...) and is in the past → MEDIUM per occurrence.
  - _quality:_ Flags once per matching date, so a driving licence with several category expiry rows yields multiple stacked MEDIUM flags. Conflates document freshness (expired but genuine) with tampering — arguably not a fraud signal at all.
- **CONTENT_ANACHRONISM_DUE (check_date_logic, lines 683-695)** — If an invoice-typed date exists, any due-typed date earlier than invoice_date − 1 day → HIGH (−30 pts).
  - _quality:_ Uses invoice_dates[0], where ordering is extraction-method order (French dates before numeric), NOT document order — the 'primary' invoice date is arbitrary. Compares ALL due dates in the document against that one date, which breaks on statements/dunning letters listing several invoices.
- **CONTENT_ANACHRONISM_ORDER (check_date_logic, lines 699-711)** — Any order-typed date later than invoice_date + 1 day → HIGH.
  - _quality:_ Same invoice_dates[0] arbitrariness. The 1-day tolerance is hardcoded. Note: the module docstring promises a service-date-after-invoice-date check (line 6) but no service anachronism check exists — check_date_logic only covers due and order.
- **CONTENT_FUTURE_DOCUMENT_DATE (check_future_document_date, lines 716-746)** — First-PAGE dates typed invoice/document/creation that are > now + 1 day → CRITICAL (−50 pts). Called with first-page dates only (analyze_content line 1786).
  - _quality:_ 1-day tolerance is too tight: subscription/insurance invoices legitimately dated a few days ahead get a critical flag. First-page-only scoping means a future date on page 2+ is never checked.
- **CONTENT_DATE_CREATION_MISMATCH (check_date_vs_creation, lines 749-820)** — Compares the FIRST 'document'-typed and FIRST 'invoice'-typed date on page 1 against PDF metadata CreationDate: abs gap > 7d → MEDIUM, > 30d → HIGH, > 180d → CRITICAL. Skipped silently when CreationDate is missing.
  - _quality:_ The single biggest false-positive engine: uses abs(), so 'PDF regenerated months after invoice date' (every on-demand re-download from Amazon/EDF/Free, every scan of an old letter) scores identically to the genuinely suspicious 'PDF created long before its stated date'. Thresholds hardcoded; also inherits the arbitrary first-date selection and the over-broad ', le' classifier.
- **check_duplicate_amounts (lines 889-900) — gutted** — Returns [] unconditionally; the repeated-amount check was removed for FP reasons. Its helper extract_amounts (lines 827-886) is now fully dead code with its own buggy regexes.
  - _quality:_ Dead. Leaves the module with ZERO amount-based checks — no HT+TVA=TTC arithmetic, no line-item sum, nothing.
- **CONTENT_REFERENCE_DATE_MISMATCH (check_reference_date_match + extract_date_from_reference, lines 1014-1144)** — If the first extracted invoice reference embeds a date (leading digits starting with '20' parsed as YYYYMMDD/YYYYMM/YYYY), compare with invoice date: year mismatch → HIGH, month → MEDIUM, day → LOW.
  - _quality:_ Assumes any ref whose digits start with '20' encodes a calendar date — fiscal-year numbering, carried-over sequences, and coincidental '20…' prefixes all produce HIGH flags. Reference selection (refs[0]) is keyword-list order, not document order, so the 'invoice reference' may actually be an order number.
- **CONTENT_INCONSISTENT_REFERENCES (check_reference_consistency + extract_all_invoice_references, lines 929-1189)** — Collects references behind keywords (facture n°, invoice #, votre/notre référence, document n°, commande n°, order #), with exclusion patterns (client n°, contrat n°, cedex, pdl...). If more than one UNIQUE value is found → CRITICAL (−50 pts).
  - _quality:_ Fundamentally broken pooling: invoice number, order number and customer references are DIFFERENT numbers by design, but they are collected into one pool and required to be identical. Any Amazon-style invoice showing both 'Facture n° INV-123' and 'Commande n° 405-999…' earns a CRITICAL flag. Ref regex ([A-Z]{0,5}[-]?\d{4,}[-]?\d*[A-Z]?, line 977) also misses short-grouped refs like 'F-24-0032'.
- **CONTENT_INVALID_SIRET / CONTENT_INVALID_SIREN (extract_siret/extract_siren + Luhn, lines 1199-1398, flags at 1666-1689)** — Extracts labeled SIRET (14 digits, 'SIRET:' variants) and SIREN (labeled or 'NNN NNN NNN RCS City'), validates Luhn checksum; each invalid occurrence → HIGH (−30 pts).
  - _quality:_ Checksum math is correct (verified conventions: SIRET doubles even positions, SIREN doubles odd). But extraction is label-dependent — an unlabeled SIRET in a footer is never validated, so removing the label defeats the check entirely. Micro-entrepreneur documents legitimately using formats the regexes miss are skipped rather than flagged.
- **CONTENT_INVALID_VAT (extract_french_vat + validate_french_vat, lines 1263-1441, flag at 1692-1702)** — Extracts FR VAT numbers (labeled or bare FRxx+9 digits), validates key = (12 + 3*(SIREN % 97)) % 97 plus SIREN Luhn; invalid → HIGH.
  - _quality:_ Formula is correct for standard SIRENs (note: 'Monaco/La Poste' special SIREN cases aside). Only FRENCH VAT is validated — DE/BE/NL/etc. numbers on foreign invoices are never checked. Unlabeled VAT with full French spacing 'FR 03 552 081 317' escapes pattern 3.
- **CONTENT_SIREN_VAT_MISMATCH (check_legal_mentions, lines 1704-1730)** — If valid VAT(s) and valid SIRET/SIREN(s) coexist, the SIREN embedded in each VAT must intersect the document SIRENs; otherwise → CRITICAL (−50 pts).
  - _quality:_ Good idea, but no multi-company awareness: marketplace invoices (platform SIRET + seller VAT), invoices listing both supplier and customer legal IDs, or factoring mentions produce guaranteed critical false positives.
- **IBAN extraction + mod-97 validation (extract_iban / validate_iban_checksum, lines 1540-1637) — extraction only** — Extracts labeled and unlabeled IBANs, validates ISO 13616 mod-97. Correct algorithm.
  - _quality:_ NEVER produces a flag in this module — check_legal_mentions does not call it; results are only consumed by external.py and the app UI. An invalid IBAN (the classic payment-diversion fraud) yields zero score impact here. Unlabeled pattern is digits-only so alphanumeric-BBAN IBANs (NL, GB…) without a label are missed.
- **RCS and capital social extraction (extract_rcs / extract_capital_social, lines 1444-1537)** — Extracts RCS mentions (3 pattern orders) and share-capital amounts.
  - _quality:_ Pure extraction, never flagged, never cross-checked (e.g. RCS number vs SIREN would be a free consistency check — they are the same 9 digits). Display-only value.
- **Scoring + confidence (analyze_content, lines 1748-1825)** — score = 100 − Σ severity points (low 5 / med 15 / high 30 / crit 50), floored at 0. Confidence: ≥2 classified dates → 0.9; ≥2 dates → 0.7; 1 date → 0.5; none → 0.3; empty text → score 100, confidence 0.1.
  - _quality:_ SEVERITY_POINTS (line 1740) duplicates the scoring config in CLAUDE.md/scoring.py — drift risk. Confidence is inflated because the generic 'du'/', le' keywords classify almost every French date, so nearly every French document reports 0.9. Empty-text path returns a neutral 100 with no OCR fallback (ocr.py exists but is not wired into pdf_extractor or analyzer), making the module blind to scanned/flattened documents. No positive trust signals despite valid SIRET+VAT+IBAN being strong legitimacy evidence.

**Weaknesses:**
- content.py:22 — 'import datefinder' is unused (the fallback at lines 535-549 is commented out), yet datefinder remains a hard dependency in requirements.txt. Net effect: no English, ISO (YYYY-MM-DD), or long-English-form date parsing at all.
- content.py:181 — two-digit year pivot 'year = 2000 + year_short' maps '85'→2085 and '99'→2099; any legitimate 1990s short date becomes a far-future date and can trigger a CRITICAL CONTENT_FAR_FUTURE_DATE.
- content.py:218 — abbreviated-month pattern matches ANY 3-letter word + 2 digits ('Mai 68', 'NOV 85', product codes, table headers), fabricating dates that feed the impossible-date checks.
- content.py:151 — numeric date regex hard-assumes DD/MM order; US-format documents are misparsed ('03/04/2024' read as 3 April); no support for '15.01.2024', 'YYYY-MM-DD', or month/year '12/2024' periods.
- content.py:436 — the city+', le' special-case regex requires the context to END with ', le', but the context built at lines 507-508 always includes the date text itself, so this branch is unreachable dead code; classification silently falls through to the generic ', le' keyword.
- content.py:289 and content.py:356 — keywords 'du' (service) and ', le' (document) are substrings present in almost all French prose; with no word-boundary or proximity constraint (line 445: 'keyword in context_lower'), nearly every date gets classified, corrupting both the coherence checks and the confidence heuristic. 'dated' (line 275) matches inside 'updated'/'outdated'.
- content.py:267 vs content.py:331 — 'date d'émission' is listed under BOTH 'invoice' and 'creation'; the tie is resolved by dict insertion order (line 443-452), which is fragile and undocumented.
- content.py:487 — context lookup uses text.lower().find(source_text.lower()), which returns the FIRST occurrence of that date string in the document, not the position the regex actually matched; a repeated date string gets the wrong context and therefore potentially the wrong type.
- content.py:478-481 — dedup key (date, source_text) means the same date string appearing under two different labels ('Date de facture: 15/01/2024' and 'Échéance: 15/01/2024') is recorded once with only the first label's type, silently disabling anachronism checks between them.
- content.py:684 and content.py:1099 — 'invoice_dates[0]' is extraction-method order (French-name dates always precede numeric dates), not reading order; the 'primary invoice date' and 'primary reference' are effectively arbitrary.
- content.py:790 — check_date_vs_creation uses abs(gap): it cannot distinguish 'PDF regenerated after the stated date' (benign, extremely common) from 'PDF created before the stated date' (physically suspicious). Thresholds 7/30/180 days (lines 792-800) are hardcoded with no document-type awareness.
- content.py:770-771 — when metadata CreationDate is absent, check_date_vs_creation returns [] silently; stripping metadata disables the check with no penalty from this module.
- content.py:889-900 — check_duplicate_amounts is a stub returning []; extract_amounts (lines 827-886) is fully dead code (no other caller in the repo), and its regexes were broken anyway (pattern '[\d\s,]+[.,]\d{2}' would over-capture spaces/newlines). Module docstring (lines 3-9) still advertises amount checks and service-date anachronisms that do not exist.
- content.py:948-960 — extract_all_invoice_references pools invoice numbers, order numbers, 'votre/notre référence' and 'document n°' into ONE namespace; check_reference_consistency (line 1174) then demands they all be identical and emits CRITICAL otherwise.
- content.py:977 — reference regex '([A-Z]{0,5}[-]?\d{4,}[-]?\d*[A-Z]?)' requires ≥4 consecutive digits, missing short-grouped refs like 'F-24-0032' or 'FAC/2024/17'; slash- and dot-separated refs are not captured.
- content.py:1036-1060 — extract_date_from_reference treats ANY reference whose digits start with '20' as encoding a calendar date; fiscal-year and sequence numbering produce HIGH mismatch flags.
- content.py:1318-1323 — SIRET extraction is label-gated ('siret' must appear); unlabeled 14-digit SIRETs in footers are never checksum-validated, so omitting the label defeats the check.
- content.py:1640-1732 — check_legal_mentions never flags ABSENCE of legal mentions (despite its docstring), never flags invalid IBANs (extract_iban is not called there), and never cross-checks the RCS number against SIREN even though both are extracted.
- content.py:1416-1418 — unlabeled VAT pattern '\b(fr\s?\d{2}\s?\d{9})\b' cannot match the common fully-spaced print format 'FR 03 552 081 317'.
- content.py:639-653 — the expired-document loop emits one MEDIUM flag per matching date with no dedup/cap, stacking penalties on documents that list several validity rows.
- content.py:1740-1745 — SEVERITY_POINTS is duplicated per-module instead of imported from scoring; the two tables can drift.
- content.py:1780-1789 — future-date and date-vs-creation checks run on page 1 only; later pages are unchecked. Line 1763 joins pages with a single \n which can also glue the last line of a page to the next page's header, creating spurious contexts.
- content.py:120-123 — matching on text.lower() and slicing the original by the same offsets assumes casefolding never changes string length; false for a few Unicode characters (edge case, but a latent misalignment bug).

**False Positive Risks:**
- Re-downloaded/regenerated invoice: Amazon, EDF, Free, OVH generate the PDF at download time, so CreationDate = download date, months after the printed invoice date → CONTENT_DATE_CREATION_MISMATCH CRITICAL (−50) on a perfectly genuine document. Same for a customer who scans or prints-to-PDF an old paper letter.
- Any invoice showing both an invoice number and an order number ('Facture n° INV-2024-118' + 'Commande n° 405-9983…') → CONTENT_INCONSISTENT_REFERENCES CRITICAL, because different-purpose references are pooled and required to be identical. This hits most e-commerce invoices.
- ID/benefits document with an unlabeled birth date in a table cell ('12/05/85' with the 'Né(e) le' label too far away for the 2-line context window) → parsed as 2085 → CONTENT_FAR_FUTURE_DATE CRITICAL.
- Insurance attestation or subscription invoice with coverage 'du 01/01/2026 au 01/07/2027' where the 'valable' keyword sits in a different table column/line than the date → far-future CRITICAL despite being a normal validity range.
- Dunning letter / account statement listing several past invoices: due dates of older invoices precede the arbitrarily-chosen 'first' invoice date → CONTENT_ANACHRONISM_DUE HIGH.
- Marketplace or dual-party invoice carrying the platform's SIRET plus the seller's VAT number (or supplier + customer legal IDs) → CONTENT_SIREN_VAT_MISMATCH CRITICAL, because the check assumes exactly one company per document.
- Company using fiscal-year or carried-over numbering ('2024-1205' issued in January 2025) → CONTENT_REFERENCE_DATE_MISMATCH HIGH.
- Invoice legitimately pre-dated by a few days (subscription pre-billing, insurance premium notices sent ahead of the effective date) → CONTENT_FUTURE_DOCUMENT_DATE CRITICAL with only a 1-day tolerance.
- Genuine but expired ID or attestation (naturally expired, still authentic) → one or several stacked CONTENT_EXPIRED_DOCUMENT MEDIUM flags; expiry is a freshness issue, not evidence of tampering.
- Documents containing incidental tokens like 'Mai 68', 'NOV 85', or 3-letter codes followed by 2 digits → fabricated far-future/very-old dates via find_abbreviated_month_dates.
- Rent receipt or contract quoting a legitimate old date ('bail du 15 mars 1998') where the context lacks the whitelisted legal keywords → CONTENT_VERY_OLD_DATE MEDIUM.
- French prose containing ', le' near a date ('Veuillez trouver, le cas échéant, … le 15/03/2023') misclassifies the date as the document's establishment date, feeding the creation-date mismatch cascade.

**False Negative Gaps:**
- Amount tampering — the flagship invoice fraud (changing 1 025 € to 2 025 €) — is completely invisible to this module: check_duplicate_amounts returns [] and there is no HT + TVA = TTC arithmetic check, no line-items-sum-to-total check, no VAT-rate plausibility check (5.5/10/20%), no cross-page total consistency. A whole-PDF rewrite that keeps dates/references clean sails through.
- Scanned or flattened-to-image PDFs: no text layer → analyze_content returns score 100 (confidence 0.1) and the OCR fallback (src/extractors/ocr.py) is not wired into pdf_extractor/analyzer, so a screenshot-of-an-edited-invoice PDF gets a near-neutral content score.
- Non-French/ISO-dated documents: 'January 15, 2024' and '2024-01-15' are never extracted (datefinder disabled, custom parsers French-only), so ALL date checks silently no-op on English or machine-formatted documents — an attacker can dodge every date check by using ISO dates.
- Invalid or swapped IBAN (payment-diversion fraud): extract_iban validates the checksum but check_legal_mentions never uses it, so a typo'd or fabricated IBAN produces zero flags from this module.
- A French 'invoice' with NO legal mentions at all (no SIRET, no VAT, no RCS) — legally impossible for a real French company — receives no flag; absence is never penalized, only present-but-invalid values are.
- Unlabeled SIRET: printing '552 081 317 66522' without the word 'SIRET' bypasses checksum validation entirely (label-gated regexes, lines 1318-1323).
- Fabricated-but-Luhn-valid SIREN/SIRET: the checksum is trivially satisfiable; with no local registry cross-check (external INSEE module is optional/post-MVP), an invented valid-checksum identifier passes.
- Service date after invoice date — explicitly promised by the module docstring — is never checked (check_date_logic only covers due and order), and the 'service' type is diluted by the generic 'du' keyword anyway.
- Future or inconsistent dates on pages 2+ escape check_future_document_date and check_date_vs_creation, which only receive first-page dates (analyze_content lines 1780-1789).
- Metadata-stripped PDFs: CreationDate=None silently disables the strongest content check (date-vs-creation); the tamperer who removes metadata loses nothing here.
- Same date string reused under different labels: the (date, source) dedup at lines 478-481 keeps only the first label's type, so an anachronism expressed with identical date strings is invisible.
- Non-French EU VAT numbers (DE/BE/NL/IT…) are extracted by no pattern and validated by nothing — foreign or cross-border invoice fraud is out of scope by accident, not by design.
- Reference tampering that keeps ONE consistent value: since the consistency check only compares occurrences within the document, an attacker who edits all occurrences (or a document with a single occurrence) is safe; no format-plausibility or sequence check exists.
- Coherent-but-fraudulent documents: an attacker who fabricates an invoice from scratch with plausible dates, one reference, and a real company's valid SIRET/VAT gets a perfect 100 from this module — there are no positive-verification signals (template/registry match) to distinguish 'consistent' from 'genuine'.

**Improvement Ideas:**
- Reinstate amount analysis with arithmetic instead of repetition-counting: verify Total TTC = Total HT + TVA within rounding tolerance, line items sum to subtotal, VAT amount / HT ratio matches a legal French rate (2.1/5.5/10/20%), and the same 'total due' value is identical everywhere it appears. These are high-signal, low-FP checks and directly catch digit-edit fraud.
- Split reference extraction into namespaces (invoice_ref, order_ref, client_ref, document_ref) keyed by the keyword that matched, and only apply the consistency CRITICAL within the same namespace; keep cross-namespace values as informational context.
- Make check_date_vs_creation direction-aware: creation_date << document date (PDF created before its own stated date) stays HIGH/CRITICAL; creation_date >> document date should be downgraded to LOW/info and suppressed when metadata Producer indicates on-demand generation or scanning (coordinate with the metadata module), since re-downloads and scans are the dominant legitimate case.
- Wire the OCR fallback into pdf_extractor so image-only PDFs get analyzed text; when text comes from OCR, run checks with relaxed checksum handling (OCR digit confusion) and reflect OCR provenance in confidence rather than returning a neutral 100/0.1.
- Replace the disabled datefinder path with locale-aware parsing (e.g. the 'dateparser' library with languages=['fr','en']) and add explicit ISO YYYY-MM-DD, dotted DD.MM.YYYY, and MM/YYYY period patterns; detect document language first and choose day-first vs month-first accordingly.
- Fix the 2-digit-year pivot: map YY to 19YY when 2000+YY would exceed current_year + small_margin (standard sliding window), eliminating the 2085-birth-date class of critical false positives.
- Fix or remove the unreachable city+', le' regex at line 436 by matching the context minus the date substring; then delete the over-generic keywords ('du', ', le', 'dated') or require word boundaries and a max distance (e.g. keyword within 25 chars before the date).
- Track the actual regex match span through extraction instead of re-finding via text.find(), keep one ExtractedDate per OCCURRENCE (not per unique string), and let a date carry multiple candidate types so relabeled duplicates still participate in anachronism checks.
- Add the missing legal-mention checks: flag French invoices (doc-type aware, using the analyzer's detect_document_type) that have NO SIRET/SIREN/VAT at all; flag invalid IBANs; scan for unlabeled 14-digit Luhn-valid SIRET candidates; cross-check RCS number == SIREN when both present.
- Introduce positive trust signals per project goal 2: a coherent set of valid SIRET + matching VAT + matching RCS + valid IBAN should add a bonus (or raise confidence), not be score-neutral — this also helps separate 'fabricated but internally consistent' from 'verified'.
- Make the SIREN/VAT mismatch multi-company-aware: pair each VAT with the nearest SIRET/SIREN in the text (proximity or same page block) before declaring a mismatch, and downgrade to MEDIUM when 2+ distinct companies are legitimately present.
- Centralize thresholds and severity points in scoring.py/config (creation-gap 7/30/180, future tolerance 1 day, far-future 365 days, very-old year 2000) so they can be tuned per document type instead of being hardcoded per function; cap repeated flags of the same code per document.
- Extend the future-date and creation-date checks to all pages (or at least last page, where totals/signatures live), and add the promised service-vs-invoice anachronism check once the 'service' classifier is cleaned of the 'du' keyword.
- Delete dead code (datefinder import, extract_amounts, the check_duplicate_amounts stub) and update the module docstring so the advertised checks match reality — the drift is already misleading for anyone tuning the engine.

---

### CODE AUDIT — src/modules/structure.py — Module E: PDF internal structure analysis (incremental updates / revision counting, version-diff modification history, XMP toolkit editor detection, digital signature presence + EU DSS verification, JavaScript / annotations / embedded files / AcroForm / deleted-object checks). Entry point analyze_structure() called from src/analyzer.py:145 with defaults; get_modification_history() also called directly by app.py:970 for the UI history view. Scoring: flat 100 minus SEVERITY_POINTS per flag (5/15/30/50), hardcoded confidence 0.95, module weight 1.3 in scoring.py (highest of all modules).

**Checks:**
- **STRUCT_INCREMENTAL_UPDATES (check_incremental_updates, lines 1227-1376)** — Counts raw b'%%EOF' occurrences in the file bytes (count_incremental_updates, line 1219); edits = eof_count - 1. If a signature is present AND EU DSS says it is 'trusted', 1 edit is excused (effective_edits = edits - 1). Severity ramp: 1 effective edit = medium (15 pts), 2 = high (30), >=3 = critical (50).
  - _quality:_ Naive: raw byte counting can't distinguish real incremental updates from linearized PDFs (which legitimately contain 2 %%EOF markers), embedded PDFs, or %%EOF bytes inside streams. The severity ramp is aggressive and undiscriminating — it never looks at WHAT changed, even though compare_versions_by_objects() exists in the same file and could tell it.
- **STRUCT_HIDDEN_MODIFICATION (lines 1349-1374)** — If incremental updates exist but raw creationDate string == raw modDate string, flags 'editor hid its traces'. Severity high (30 pts).
  - _quality:_ Good idea, but compares raw metadata strings and fires on linearized PDFs (counted as 1 edit) whose ModDate legitimately equals CreationDate, and on updates appended by tools that never touch /Info (DSS/LTV stamping, viewer-state saves).
- **Signature status flags: STRUCT_SIGNATURE_TRUSTED (low), STRUCT_SIGNATURE_TRUSTED_EXPIRED (low), STRUCT_SIGNATURE_NOT_TRUSTED (medium), STRUCT_SIGNATURE_UNVERIFIABLE (medium), STRUCT_SIGNATURE_INVALID (high) (lines 1256-1306)** — has_digital_signature() (lines 999-1045) scans widgets for Signature fields and all xref object strings for '/Type /Sig', '/SubFilter /adbe.pkcs7', '/SubFilter /ETSI'. If found, verify_signature_dss() (lines 1048-1197) base64-encodes THE ENTIRE PDF and POSTs it to the EU DSS demo REST API at ec.europa.eu, then parses SimpleReport: is_trusted = 'QC' or 'QES' substring in the signature-level string; is_valid = ValidSignaturesCount>0 OR has_qc.
  - _quality:_ Fundamentally broken against the project's hard constraint (never send the document to an external service) — and it's the default path since analyzer.py calls analyze_structure(pdf_data) with verify_signatures=True. Also unsound: is_valid/is_trusted are True whenever the level string contains 'QC' even if DSS says the signature FAILED. Signature flags are only ever emitted when eof_count > 1, so a signed single-revision PDF gets no positive signal at all; and because 'trusted signature' is a low-severity Flag, it SUBTRACTS 5 points — a positive signal lowers the score.
- **STRUCT_JAVASCRIPT_DETECTED (check_javascript, lines 1379-1455)** — Substring search for '/JavaScript' or '/JS' in every xref object string, plus 'javascript' (case-insensitive) anywhere in str(annot.info) of every annotation. Severity critical (50 pts) — one hit halves the module score.
  - _quality:_ Very brittle: '/JS' is a substring match, not a token match, so any name beginning with /JS matches; an empty /Names /JavaScript tree left by a generator matches; an annotation whose free-text comment merely contains the word 'javascript' matches. Meanwhile PDF-name hex escaping (/J#53) trivially evades it. It never extracts or inspects actual JS payloads.
- **STRUCT_HIDDEN_ANNOTATIONS (check_hidden_annotations, lines 1458-1516)** — Flags annotations with opacity == 0 as hidden. Severity high (30 pts).
  - _quality:_ Only checks /CA opacity; the docstring promises 'invisible flag' checking but the /F Hidden/NoView flags (the standard way to hide annotations) are never inspected. Also never reads annotation content, so it can't say what was hidden.
- **STRUCT_SUSPICIOUS_ANNOTATIONS (lines 1497-1527)** — Flags annotation types FileAttachment, Sound, Movie, Screen, RichMedia. Severity high (30 pts).
  - _quality:_ Reasonable list, but severity is flat and no payload inspection is done. Link annotations with /Launch or /URI actions — a real phishing vector — are not checked.
- **STRUCT_EMBEDDED_FILES (check_embedded_files, lines 1535-1598)** — Any embedded file (doc.embfile_count() > 0) is flagged: critical if a filename ends in .exe/.js/.vbs/.bat/.cmd/.ps1/.dll, otherwise high (30 pts).
  - _quality:_ Extension-only classification is trivially evaded (rename), and flagging ALL embedded files as high directly punishes Factur-X / ZUGFeRD / XRechnung hybrid e-invoices — the most standards-compliant legitimate invoices in this tool's French/EU target domain.
- **STRUCT_ACROFORM_DETECTED (check_acroform, lines 1601-1666)** — Counts page widgets excluding Signature fields; any non-signature form field yields one medium flag (15 pts) listing up to 10 fields.
  - _quality:_ Correctly excludes signature fields; but interactive forms are the NORM for French CERFA / tax / attestation documents that this product explicitly targets. Mitigated only partially by scoring.py doc-type multipliers (0.20 for forms).
- **STRUCT_DELETED_OBJECTS (check_object_streams, lines 1669-1727)** — Regex rb'\d{10}\s+\d{5}\s+f' over the raw file counts classic xref-table free entries; threshold 10 (unsigned) or 15 (trusted signature); over threshold = medium (15 pts).
  - _quality:_ Only works on classic xref tables. PDF 1.5+ cross-reference streams (the majority of modern PDFs) encode free entries in compressed binary — the regex sees nothing. It also counts the mandatory object-0 free entry of every xref section, and random matches inside binary streams. obj_count/endobj_count (lines 1692-1693) are computed and never used — dead code.
- **Date-gap checks via metadata.check_dates() (called at structure.py lines 1776-1779; implementation metadata.py:267-350)** — META_FUTURE_CREATION_DATE: creation > now+1 day = critical. META_DOCUMENT_MODIFIED: ModDate - CreationDate > 2 SECONDS = critical (50 pts). META_IMPOSSIBLE_DATES: ModDate < CreationDate = high.
  - _quality:_ The 2-second tolerance is far too tight — any generator that stamps ModDate at save-completion of a large render, any OCR/archival post-processing, any legitimate re-save instantly costs 50 points inside the highest-weighted module. Note this is NOT double-counted with the metadata module (analyze_metadata explicitly delegates date checks here), but emitting META_* codes from the 'structure' module confuses flag attribution in the UI.
- **STRUCT_XMP_EDITOR_MISMATCH (check_xmp_toolkit_mismatch, lines 873-992)** — Finds the first xref object containing '/Subtype /XML', extracts x:xmptk="...", maps it to an editor via XMP_TOOLKIT_SIGNATURES (adobe/microsoft/nitro/foxit/pdflib/itext); if that editor's first keyword doesn't appear in Producer/Creator, flags high (30 pts) — or medium if Producer/Creator matches a SCANNER_SIGNATURES entry (lines 851-870).
  - _quality:_ The core assumption is wrong: Adobe's XMP SDK ('Adobe XMP Core') is open source and embedded by countless non-Adobe generators (and LaTeX pdfx/hyperxmp packages hardcode literal Adobe toolkit strings into their XMP templates), so 'Adobe XMP Core' does NOT prove Acrobat touched the file. Also grabs the FIRST /Subtype /XML stream, which may be an image's object-level XMP, not the document catalog's /Metadata.
- **Modification history extraction (get_modification_history lines 726-829, compare_versions_by_objects 359-565, extract_text_per_version 568-629, diff_versions 632-723, _detect_tool 290-356)** — UI-facing (app.py:970), produces no flags. Splits the file at each %%EOF, opens each truncation with fitz, diffs page text (whitespace-normalized SequenceMatcher) between consecutive versions, inventories changed xref objects with a heuristic type classification, and labels each version with Producer/xmptk-derived tool. Approach B (object-level) preferred, falls back to Approach A (per-version text extraction). Skips 0-page truncations as linearization stubs.
  - _quality:_ The most sophisticated part of the module, with sensible fallbacks and linearization-stub handling — but its output is never fed back into scoring, and object comparison keyed by xref number produces garbage when an editor renumbers objects. Object type classification (lines 527-541) is crude: anything page-mapped with '/Length' is 'content_stream', '/Font' matches resource dicts.

**Weaknesses:**
- HARD CONSTRAINT VIOLATION: verify_signature_dss() (structure.py:1048-1101) base64-encodes the WHOLE document and POSTs it to https://ec.europa.eu/.../validateSignature. analyze_structure() defaults verify_signatures=True and analyzer.py:145 calls it with defaults, so every signed PDF is uploaded to an external service in the default production path — the project rule says the document must NEVER leave the machine. It is also the DSS *demo* webapp (no SLA), with a 30s timeout that stalls analysis when unreachable.
- Duplicate signature verification per analysis: analyze_structure() calls has_digital_signature() + verify_signature_dss() at structure.py:1762-1764, then check_incremental_updates() repeats BOTH at 1252-1258 — the file is uploaded twice and all xrefs are scanned twice; results are not shared or cached.
- Fail-open error handling everywhere: every check (e.g. structure.py:989-991, 1452-1454, 1529-1531, 1595-1597, 1663-1665, 1724-1726) catches bare Exception, logs, and returns zero flags; count_incremental_updates returns 1 ('assume clean') on read failure (1222-1224); analyze_structure then reports score 100 with hardcoded confidence 0.95 (1794-1801). An adversarially malformed PDF that breaks parsing gets a PERFECT structure score at the highest module weight.
- count_incremental_updates (structure.py:1219) counts raw b'%%EOF' bytes: linearized PDFs (2 markers by design), PDFs embedded as attachments, and %%EOF sequences inside uncompressed streams all inflate the count. The version-diff code knows about linearization stubs (427-441, 604-607) but the flag-producing counter does not.
- verify_signature_dss trust logic is unsound (structure.py:1143-1155): is_trusted = 'QC'/'QES' substring in the level string and is_valid = is_fully_valid OR has_qc — a signature DSS reports as FAILED is still declared valid+trusted if the level string mentions QC, which then excuses an incremental update in check_incremental_updates (1308-1314).
- Positive signals subtract points: STRUCT_SIGNATURE_TRUSTED is severity 'low' and the scorer at 1789-1792 subtracts 5 points for it — a QES-signed document scores LOWER than the same document unsigned. There is no bonus/informational severity in the scoring model.
- Signature flags only emitted when eof_count > 1 (check_incremental_updates returns at 1246-1247 before any signature logic): a signed single-revision PDF — the strongest possible legitimacy signal — produces no flag, no trust bonus, and analyze_structure's own signature probe result (1762-1764) is used only to tune the deleted-objects threshold.
- Dead code: extract_text_from_content_stream (structure.py:104-181) and _decode_pdf_string (184-240) — a hand-rolled content-stream text parser — are never called by any live function (compare_versions_by_objects uses get_text() instead, contradicting its own docstring at 383-388). Also obj_count/endobj_count in check_object_streams (1692-1693) are computed and never used, and the hand parser itself is broken anyway (no hex strings <...> Tj, no balanced-unescaped-paren strings, non-greedy [.*?] fails on nested arrays).
- Brittle whitespace-sensitive substring matching against serialized objects: '/Type /Sig' and '/SubFilter /adbe.pkcs7' (1028-1035), '/Subtype /XML' (903, 320), '/Type /Page' (537) all require exactly one space; PDFs commonly serialize '/Type/Sig' with no space — false negatives depend on PyMuPDF's re-serialization behavior. '/JS' (1417) is an unanchored substring, and PDF name hex-escaping (/J#53, /Java#53cript) evades all of these string checks.
- check_object_streams free-object regex (structure.py:1697) only understands classic xref tables; PDF 1.5+ cross-reference streams (most modern PDFs) hide all free entries from it, making the ghost-data check a no-op exactly where it matters; thresholds 10/15 (1702-1707) are arbitrary and every classic xref section contributes its mandatory object-0 free entry.
- check_xmp_toolkit_mismatch reads the FIRST '/Subtype /XML' stream in xref order (structure.py:900-909), which can be an image's XMP packet rather than the document /Metadata; _detect_tool (317-327) has the same bug plus an unconditional break after the first XML object even if it had no xmptk.
- The 2-second ModDate tolerance in the delegated check_dates (metadata.py:310) assigns CRITICAL (50 pts) inside the weight-1.3 structure module to any PDF whose ModDate trails CreationDate by more than 2 seconds — a threshold real-world generators violate routinely.
- compare_versions_by_objects compares objects by xref NUMBER across versions (structure.py:514-547): editors that renumber objects on save make every object 'changed'; the resulting object_changes inventory is then meaningless, and its type classification (530-541) misclassifies (any page-mapped object containing '/Length' = content_stream, '/Font' matches page resource dictionaries).
- Resource handling: fitz documents are opened without try/finally or context managers in every check (e.g. 1398, 1477, 1553, 1622); an exception mid-loop leaks the handle. The document is also re-opened from disk ~8 separate times per analysis, and has_digital_signature/check_javascript/_detect_tool each do full O(xref_length) scans calling xref_object — repeated 3-4 times over.
- The rich modification-history diff (get_modification_history) is UI-only: none of its findings (what text changed, which tool made each revision) influence flags or score, while the scoring path relies on the crude EOF count — the module's best evidence is disconnected from its verdict.
- check_javascript annotation heuristic (structure.py:1434) matches the word 'javascript' inside str(annot.info) — annotation titles/contents are user text, so a comment mentioning JavaScript triggers a CRITICAL flag.
- Two divergent implementations of version counting exist (find_eof_positions at 49-101 with newline-skipping vs content.count(b'%%EOF') at 1219); they can disagree and neither is validated against a startxref/trailer structure.
- Promised checks never implemented: the module/CLAUDE.md docstring lists 'layer count' (OCG) analysis and deleted-object *content* recovery; neither exists — no OCG/optional-content check, no /OpenAction or /Launch action check either.

**False Positive Risks:**
- Linearized ('Fast Web View') PDFs — the default output of Adobe tooling and many bank/insurer download portals — contain 2 %%EOF markers by construction: flagged STRUCT_INCREMENTAL_UPDATES medium, and since their ModDate usually equals CreationDate, ALSO STRUCT_HIDDEN_MODIFICATION high (-45 pts total) despite zero edits.
- Factur-X / ZUGFeRD / XRechnung hybrid e-invoices (standard in France/Germany, mandated for French B2B e-invoicing) embed an XML attachment: STRUCT_EMBEDDED_FILES high (30 pts) — the most compliant legitimate invoices are punished precisely in this product's core market.
- Digitally signed documents analyzed offline or when the DSS demo API is down/times out: signature_trusted stays False, so a payslip or notarial attestation with a signature revision plus an LTV/DSS timestamp update (2-3 %%EOFs — normal PAdES-LTA practice) is flagged high or CRITICAL.
- French government fillable forms (CERFA, tax attestations, URSSAF forms): AcroForm fields trigger STRUCT_ACROFORM_DETECTED medium, and the field-format/validation JavaScript these forms legitimately contain triggers STRUCT_JAVASCRIPT_DETECTED critical (-65 pts combined, only partially rescued by scoring.py's form-type multiplier).
- LaTeX-generated invoices/reports using pdfx or hyperxmp: those packages embed XMP templates containing literal 'Adobe XMP Core' toolkit strings, so Producer='pdfTeX' + xmptk='Adobe XMP Core' fires STRUCT_XMP_EDITOR_MISMATCH high ('modified with Adobe Acrobat') on a never-edited file. Same for any generator built on Adobe's open-source XMP SDK.
- Any PDF whose generator stamps ModDate more than 2 seconds after CreationDate (large statement renders, batch invoicing systems that create-then-stamp, OCR/archival pipelines): META_DOCUMENT_MODIFIED critical, 50 points, inside the highest-weighted module.
- A PDF reviewed with commenting: a sticky-note whose text mentions 'javascript' (e.g. developer invoice line items or a comment) triggers the critical JS flag via str(annot.info) matching at structure.py:1434; a generator that leaves an empty /Names /JavaScript dictionary triggers it via substring at 1417.
- E-signature platform outputs (DocuSign/Adobe Sign style): invisible placement/tab annotations with opacity 0 fire STRUCT_HIDDEN_ANNOTATIONS high; their multiple certification/timestamp revisions exceed the single excused update and fire the incremental-update ramp.
- A customer who legitimately re-saves their own bank statement in Acrobat (e.g. after rotating a page) accumulates incremental updates fast: 3 saves = critical (50 pts), same weight as actual detected tampering, with no examination of what changed.
- Scanned documents OCR'd in Acrobat get STRUCT_XMP_EDITOR_MISMATCH medium even after the scanner allowlist — but the allowlist itself is substring-based ('scan', 'canon', 'hp scan' in SCANNER_SIGNATURES at 851-870) so unusual scanner producers not in the list are flagged high instead.

**False Negative Gaps:**
- Full-rewrite tampering — the most common real fraud path — is invisible: edit the PDF in Word/Illustrator/LibreOffice and re-export, 'Save As' (full save) in Acrobat, print-to-PDF, or run qpdf/Ghostscript after editing. The result has exactly one %%EOF, fresh coherent metadata, no free objects, no XMP mismatch — the module's entire evidence base (append-only history) is destroyed, and the file scores clean at weight 1.3.
- Metadata laundering: exiftool/qpdf can strip the XMP packet (no xmptk left, check_xmp_toolkit_mismatch returns at 913-914), set ModDate == CreationDate, and rewrite Producer — with a single-version file, STRUCT_HIDDEN_MODIFICATION never fires because it is only checked when eof_count > 1 (structure.py:1246-1247).
- Ghost-data detection is blind on modern PDFs: files using cross-reference streams (PDF 1.5+, most tooling since ~2007) encode free/deleted entries in compressed binary, so the rb'\d{10}\s+\d{5}\s+f' regex (1697) finds nothing; an incremental edit leaving recoverable old content in such a file is never flagged, and even on classic files up to 10 free objects pass silently.
- JavaScript obfuscated with PDF name hex escapes (/J#53 for /JS, /Java#53cript) or delivered via /OpenAction, /AA (additional actions), or /Launch actions is missed — only the literal substrings '/JavaScript' and '/JS' are searched (1417) and no action dictionaries are ever resolved.
- Shadow-attack / optional-content fraud: OCG layers that show different content than was signed/generated, or content hidden under a white rectangle in a later revision, are not detected — no layer-count check exists despite being in the module's stated scope, and the version diff results never affect the score.
- A tampered QES-signed document can retain trust: if DSS reports the signature FAILED but the signature-level string still contains 'QC', the code declares it valid AND trusted (1143-1155), excusing one incremental update — the exact scenario (signed original, appended fraudulent revision) the signature check exists to catch.
- Annotations hidden via the /F flag (Hidden/NoView bits) — the standard mechanism for concealing an annotation, e.g. a redaction-style white square placed over the real amount — are not detected; only opacity==0 is checked (1489).
- Malicious embedded files with benign names: the dangerous-extension list (1569) is filename-only, so 'invoice_backup.pdf' containing an exploit, a .docm macro document, or a renamed executable rates only 'high' or evades the critical classification entirely.
- Text edited within a single incremental update that also updates ModDate honestly (many touch-up tools do): flag ramp says just 'medium' (15 pts) with no inspection of the diff — even though compare_versions_by_objects could show the amount line changed, that evidence never reaches scoring.
- Signature spoofing by presence: has_digital_signature only checks for signature *structure* ('/Type /Sig' substrings); with verify_signatures=False (documented offline mode), an attacker can embed a garbage signature dictionary — nothing validates ByteRange coverage or whether later revisions fall outside the signed range (no local crypto validation exists at all).

**Improvement Ideas:**
- Replace the EU DSS upload with LOCAL signature validation using pyHanko: it validates PAdES/CMS signatures offline (or with only CRL/OCSP fetches — no document bytes leave the machine), reports ByteRange coverage, and — critically for this module — tells you whether incremental revisions were added AFTER the signed revision, which is the actual fraud signal. This resolves the hard-constraint violation and the double-upload at structure.py:1762-1764 / 1252-1258 in one move.
- Count revisions structurally, not by byte-grepping %%EOF: use PyMuPDF's Document facilities (xref/trailer chain, is_fast_webaccess to detect linearization) or pikepdf/qpdf revision parsing. Explicitly whitelist the linearization %%EOF and signature/DSS/LTV revisions so linearized and properly signed files stop being the top false positives.
- Make severity depend on WHAT an update changed, not how many there are: the module already computes per-revision text diffs and object inventories (compare_versions_by_objects); feed that back — metadata/viewer-state-only revision = low or none, annotation-only = low, content-stream text change = high, and a change touching amount/date/IBAN patterns (regex against the diff's added/removed lines) = critical with the diff attached as evidence. This converts the module's best code from UI decoration into its core detector.
- Fix fail-open behavior: wrap each check to record exceptions, surface a STRUCT_ANALYSIS_INCOMPLETE flag, and derive confidence from checks-succeeded/checks-run instead of the hardcoded 0.95 (1795) — per the project's own convention of 'partial results with lower confidence'. Never let a parse failure yield score 100.
- Query PDF structure through the object model instead of serialized-string substrings: resolve the catalog's /Names→/JavaScript tree, /OpenAction, /AA, and /Launch entries via xref traversal (or pikepdf), normalize name hex-escapes, and only flag NON-EMPTY JS payloads (attach a snippet). Same for signatures ('/Type/Sig' spacing) and the catalog /Metadata reference (fetch the real document XMP instead of the first /Subtype /XML stream, fixing 900-909 and 317-327).
- Whitelist standards-compliant embedded files: Factur-X/ZUGFeRD/XRechnung XML (factur-x.xml, zugferd-invoice.xml, xrechnung.xml, matching /AFRelationship) should be a POSITIVE trust signal for invoices, not a high flag; keep critical for executable content but sniff magic bytes, not just extensions (1569).
- Introduce a real positive-signal mechanism in scoring: informational/bonus flags with zero or negative cost so STRUCT_SIGNATURE_TRUSTED stops subtracting 5 points, and emit signature status for ALL signed documents, not only those with eof_count > 1 (move signature reporting out of check_incremental_updates).
- Rework the ghost-data check: parse xref sections and xref streams properly (pikepdf exposes this) to count truly orphaned/freed objects per revision, subtract the mandatory object-0 entries, and — the actual forensic win — attempt to extract the freed objects' prior content to show WHAT was deleted.
- Deduplicate and soften the date check: the 2-second ModDate tolerance (metadata.py:310, invoked from structure.py:1776) should scale by document type and gap size (seconds = ignore, minutes-hours = medium, cross-day with content changes = high), and only escalate to critical when corroborated by an actual content diff from the version history.
- Add the missing structural checks the module promises: OCG/layer enumeration (flag content in non-default-visible layers), /F Hidden-flag annotations, white-rectangle-over-text detection per revision, and annotation flattening detection; add /Launch and /URI action review to the annotation pass.
- Consolidate document access: open the fitz Document once in analyze_structure (context manager), pass it to every check, and build a single xref-object index reused by has_digital_signature, check_javascript, _detect_tool and check_xmp_toolkit_mismatch — removes the ~8 re-opens, 3-4 full xref scans, and the handle leaks on exception paths.
- Delete or finish the dead code: extract_text_from_content_stream/_decode_pdf_string (104-240) are never called and are broken (no hex strings, no balanced-paren literals); either remove them or repurpose them for freed-object content recovery where get_text() cannot help. Remove unused obj_count/endobj_count (1692-1693).
- Match version diffs on content rather than xref numbers in compare_versions_by_objects (514-547): hash decompressed stream bytes and compare sets, so editors that renumber objects don't render the object inventory meaningless.

---

### CODE AUDIT — src/modules/visual.py (Module C: watermarks + QR codes) and src/modules/fonts.py (Module D: font consistency) — audited with integration context from src/analyzer.py and src/extractors/pdf_extractor.py

**Checks:**
- **VISUAL_QR_URL_SHORTENER (visual.py:154-210)** — Decodes QR codes from rendered pages (zoom=2 / 144dpi via pyzbar), extracts domain, flags severity=high if domain contains any of 10 hardcoded shorteners (bit.ly, tinyurl.com, t.co, goo.gl, ow.ly, is.gd, buff.ly, rebrand.ly, cutt.ly, shorturl.at). Only checks data starting with http:// or https://.
  - _quality:_ DEAD CODE in practice: PYZBAR_AVAILABLE is hardcoded False at visual.py:33, so extract_qr_codes_from_pdf always returns []. Matching is substring ('bit.ly' in domain), so 'abit.ly' would also flag. List is short and static.
- **VISUAL_QR_SUSPICIOUS_TLD (visual.py:184-226)** — Flags severity=medium if QR domain ends with one of 11 TLDs (.xyz, .top, .club, .work, .click, .link, .tk, .ml, .ga, .cf, .gq).
  - _quality:_ Dead (pyzbar disabled). Implementation is sound (endswith), but the list is dated and .link/.click host legitimate services. No `continue` after this check, so the same QR can also be flagged for domain mismatch — double-penalizing one QR.
- **VISUAL_QR_DOMAIN_MISMATCH (visual.py:228-245)** — Flags severity=critical (−50 points) if QR domain doesn't exactly match or isn't a subdomain of expected_domains. Expected domains come from the caller, or are auto-extracted from document text (emails + URLs) at visual.py:443-445.
  - _quality:_ Dead (pyzbar disabled), and conceptually flawed when re-enabled: expected domains are auto-derived from the document's own text — attacker-controlled — and include the recipient's personal email domain. Critical severity on a heuristic match is very aggressive.
- **check_watermarks — SPECIMEN/COPY/DRAFT/VOID/etc. (visual.py:255-307)** — 10 regex patterns over full text (SPECIMEN high, VOID high, CANCELLED high, NOT VALID high, COPY/DRAFT/DUPLICATE/SAMPLE medium, TEST/CONFIDENTIAL low). Word-boundary matches, FR+EN variants.
  - _quality:_ DEAD CODE: intentionally never called from analyze_visual (comment at visual.py:431-434 cites false positives like 'Duplicata' on driving licenses). Disabling entirely instead of using layout signals (font size, rotation) throws away a real fraud signal. Also redundant: text.upper() then re.IGNORECASE (visual.py:294-297).
- **VISUAL_CONVERTER_WATERMARK (visual.py:270-353)** — 6 regex patterns over lowercased text: generic 'created/converted/generated with/by <word>' (medium), online converter names ilovepdf|smallpdf|sejda|pdf24|sodapdf (high), 'trial version' (medium), 'unregistered' (medium), 'evaluation copy' (medium), 'watermark by' (medium). This is the ONLY active check in the visual module.
  - _quality:_ The generic pattern is far too broad (matches legitimate 'Generated by Stripe' footers). A single 'converted by ilovepdf' string matches BOTH pattern 1 and pattern 2 → two flags, −45 points for one watermark. No word boundaries on converter names. text.lower() + re.IGNORECASE redundant (visual.py:326-329). Converter list misses common ones (CamScanner, pdfFiller, DocHub, pdfescape, Foxit trial).
- **extract_sender_domains helper (visual.py:360-393)** — Extracts candidate sender domains from emails and URLs in text; skip-list of 4 social domains (substring match) applied only to URLs, not emails.
  - _quality:_ Naive: recipient/customer email domains (gmail.com etc.) become 'expected' domains; URL regex `[\w.-]+` can capture trailing periods ('edf.fr.'); substring skip-list. Feeds the critical-severity QR mismatch check, so its noise would become critical flags when QR is re-enabled.
- **Visual module scoring (visual.py:400-475)** — score = 100 − Σ severity points (low 5 / medium 15 / high 30 / critical 50), floored at 0. Confidence = 0.9 if check_qr param is True, else 0.7.
  - _quality:_ Confidence is dishonest: check_qr=True yields 0.9 'because we could check QR codes' while QR scanning is actually disabled and status-watermark checks are disabled — the module returns 100/0.9 for nearly every document, inflating the global trust score.
- **FONTS_EXCESSIVE_DIVERSITY / FONTS_HIGH_DIVERSITY (fonts.py:191-239)** — Counts unique font 'families' (base name split on [-,], lowercased) across page.get_fonts(full=True) results. >10 families → high; >7 → medium. Comment says thresholds were raised from the spec's 5 to cut false positives.
  - _quality:_ Family normalization is too weak: 'ArialMT' vs 'Arial-BoldMT' count as 2 families ('arialmt' vs 'arial') because the alias table exists only inside the mid-line check. Counts fonts listed in page resource dictionaries, not fonts actually used in rendered text, so merged/template PDFs inflate counts.
- **FONTS_SYSTEM_FONTS (fonts.py:242-284)** — Substring match of base font name against 16 system fonts (arial, calibri, times, comic sans, segoe, ...); exempted if it also substring-matches a 12-entry PROFESSIONAL_FONTS list. Single low-severity flag (−5).
  - _quality:_ Reasonable severity, but conceptually weak: enormous numbers of legitimate invoices (Word/Excel-generated) use Calibri/Arial, so this fires on most small-business documents; it's near-constant noise rather than signal. Substring matching is brittle.
- **FONTS_NOT_EMBEDDED (fonts.py:287-325)** — Flags low if any font has is_embedded=False and isn't one of the base-14 standard fonts (substring match on helvetica/times/courier/symbol/zapfdingbats).
  - _quality:_ EFFECTIVELY DEAD due to a bug at fonts.py:128: is_embedded = bool(ext) or is_subset, but PyMuPDF's get_fonts() returns ext='n/a' (a truthy non-empty string) for non-embedded fonts, so is_embedded is essentially always True and the check almost never fires.
- **FONTS_MIXED_SUBSETS (fonts.py:328-367)** — Groups fonts by lowercased base name; flags low if the same base name appears both with a subset prefix (ABCDEF+Arial) and without (Arial) — indicates multiple tools touched the file.
  - _quality:_ Grouping uses the full base name including style suffix, so 'ABCDEF+Arial-BoldMT' vs 'ArialMT' never group and the common real-world case is missed. Low severity is appropriate given many legit workflows (Ghostscript, merges) produce this.
- **FONTS_MIDLINE_CHANGE (fonts.py:370-509)** — Via page.get_text('dict'), examines spans within each PyMuPDF line; extracts family (subset prefix stripped, split on [-,], 6-entry alias map, CIDFont+ names excluded); flags if >1 distinct family on a line. Severity medium for 1-2 suspicious lines, high for ≥3. Reports first 5 lines in details.
  - _quality:_ The most valuable check in either module, but naive: no whitelist for symbol/bullet/icon fonts (Wingdings, ZapfDingbats, Symbol), no metric-clone aliases (NimbusSans↔Helvetica, LiberationSans↔Arial), no comparison of size/color/flags, and the count-based severity is inverted relative to risk (a single edited amount = medium; a designed label-font/value-font layout = high). FAMILY_ALIASES + get_family are redefined inside the innermost loop (fonts.py:422-442).
- **Fonts module scoring (fonts.py:516-578)** — Same severity-point subtraction from 100. Confidence 0.9 if ≥3 fonts, 0.7 if 1-2, and a hardcoded 100/0.3 result if no fonts found (image-only PDF).
  - _quality:_ Score 100 for image-only or unopenable PDFs means the most evasion-friendly documents (flattened scans) get a perfect font score. Confidence keyed to font count, not to whether checks actually ran successfully.

**Weaknesses:**
- visual.py:33 — PYZBAR_AVAILABLE hardcoded to False with _pyzbar=None: the entire QR pipeline (visual.py:58-247, ~190 lines, three flag codes including the module's only 'critical') is dead code, yet analyze_visual's check_qr parameter and the analyzer's enable_qr_scan=True default (analyzer.py:58) pretend it runs.
- visual.py:465-468 — confidence set to 0.9 'if check_qr' even though QR scanning never executes and status-watermark checks are disabled; the module effectively runs one regex family and reports high confidence, inflating the weighted trust score for tampered docs.
- visual.py:255-307 — check_watermarks and WATERMARK_PATTERNS are dead code (never called; see comment at visual.py:431-434). The FP problem was solved by deleting the signal instead of qualifying it (e.g., by span size/rotation).
- visual.py:271 + 272 — a single string like 'converted by iLovePDF' matches both the generic converter pattern and the online_converter pattern, producing two flags (−15 and −30) for one watermark; there is no per-code deduplication anywhere in scoring.
- visual.py:271 — generic regex (?:created|converted|generated)\s+(?:with|by)\s+(\w+) has no allowlist and captures only one word; matches legitimate billing-platform footers ('Generated by Stripe', 'Créé par ...').
- visual.py:213-226 — no `continue` after the suspicious-TLD flag, so one QR code can be flagged for both TLD (medium) and domain mismatch (critical) = −65 from a single artifact.
- visual.py:443-445 — expected_domains auto-detected from the document's own text: attacker-controlled input becomes the trust anchor for a critical-severity check; extract_sender_domains (visual.py:378-383) also harvests the recipient's email domain.
- visual.py:386 — URL regex domain class [\w.-]+ can capture a trailing sentence period ('edf.fr.'), which would then never match an expected domain.
- visual.py:199 — URL-shortener detection uses substring `shortener in domain` instead of exact/suffix match.
- visual.py:123 and fonts.py:145,481 — doc.close() not in try/finally; a fitz exception mid-iteration leaks the handle.
- fonts.py:128 — is_embedded = bool(ext) or is_subset: PyMuPDF get_fonts() returns ext='n/a' (truthy) for non-embedded fonts, so is_embedded is ~always True and check_font_embedding (fonts.py:287-325) is effectively dead.
- fonts.py:405-407 — page.get_text('dict') and the span loop are outside any try/except, and analyze_fonts (fonts.py:528-557) has no error wrapper: a malformed page raising inside PyMuPDF crashes the entire analyzer, violating the project rule 'never crash on malformed PDFs, return partial results with lower confidence'.
- fonts.py:104-108 and fonts.py:397-401 — on fitz.open failure both functions return [] and only log; analyze_fonts then returns score 100 / confidence 0.3 (fonts.py:544-550): a corrupt PDF gets a perfect font score and the failure is invisible to the caller.
- fonts.py:115-143 — fonts are enumerated from page resource dictionaries (get_fonts), not from rendered spans; unused resource fonts (common after merges or in template-based generators) inflate diversity counts. usage_count (fonts.py:143) counts resource listings, not glyph usage, and is never consumed by any check — dead field, as is pages_used.
- fonts.py:210 — diversity family extraction is only re.split(r'[-,]')[0]: 'ArialMT' and 'Arial-BoldMT' count as two families; the FAMILY_ALIASES normalization exists only inside check_midline_font_changes (fonts.py:422-429) and is not shared with check_font_diversity.
- fonts.py:218,228 — diversity thresholds (>10 high, >7 medium) are hardcoded magic numbers, already once bumped ad hoc from the spec's 5; no calibration data, no per-document-type adjustment despite analyzer computing doc_type.
- fonts.py:422-442 — FAMILY_ALIASES dict and get_family() are redefined inside the per-line loop (re-created thousands of times on long documents); alias table has only 6 entries and misses standard metric clones (NimbusSans/NimbusSanL↔Helvetica, LiberationSans↔Arial, DejaVu, TeX Gyre).
- fonts.py:485-488 — mid-line severity scales with count of affected lines (≥3 → high): a fraudster editing one amount gets medium, while a legitimate two-font layout repeated on every row gets high — the gradient is backwards relative to actual risk.
- fonts.py:444-456 — mid-line comparison uses only font family; span size, color, flags (bold/italic bits), and baseline offset in the same dict are ignored, so same-family edits with wrong size/weight pass.
- fonts.py:352-356 — mixed-subset grouping keys on full base_name (including -BoldMT style), so 'ABCDEF+Arial-BoldMT' vs 'ArialMT' never groups; the check only fires on exact-name collisions.
- fonts.py:51 — pages_used: list[int] = None: incorrect type hint patched by __post_init__; should be field(default_factory=list).
- visual.py:400-405 and fonts.py:516-521 — SEVERITY_POINTS duplicated in each module (and presumably scoring.py); drift risk, single source of truth missing.
- visual.py:157 — check_qr_codes takes a document_text parameter that is never used in the function body (dead parameter).
- visual.py:294-297 and visual.py:326-329 — text case-normalized AND re.IGNORECASE passed; harmless but signals copy-paste regex hygiene.
- Cross-cutting — pdf_extractor.py:208 uses only page.get_text('text') with no OCR fallback and get_text default flags exclude annotation text, so both modules are blind to scanned pages and to FreeText-annotation overlays; neither module compensates by lowering confidence when a page has no text layer.

**False Positive Risks:**
- Any Word/LibreOffice-generated invoice with bulleted lists: bullets are rendered in Wingdings/Symbol/OpenSymbol while body text is Calibri — three or more bulleted lines trigger FONTS_MIDLINE_CHANGE at severity HIGH (−30), plus FONTS_SYSTEM_FONTS (−5); a completely ordinary small-business invoice loses 35 points in this module alone.
- Invoices with a designed label-font/value-font layout (very common in ERP output: brand font for labels, OCR-B or a mono font for reference numbers and amounts on the same line): every data row is a 'mid-line font change', ≥3 lines → high severity on a pristine document.
- Legitimate SaaS/billing invoices whose footer says 'Generated by Chargebee', 'Invoice generated by QuickBooks', 'Créé par Sage' — matches the generic converter regex (visual.py:271) → VISUAL_CONVERTER_WATERMARK medium (−15).
- A legitimate PDF a company round-tripped through Ghostscript or a print pipeline: Helvetica gets substituted with NimbusSans on some spans; NimbusSans vs Helvetica are metric clones of the same face, absent from FAMILY_ALIASES → mid-line flags. Similarly LiberationSans vs Arial in LibreOffice re-saves.
- LaTeX/pdfTeX documents (attestations, academic invoices): dozens of Computer Modern variants (CMR10, CMBX12, CMSY10...) each parsed as a distinct 'family' by the split-on-hyphen logic → FONTS_EXCESSIVE_DIVERSITY high (−30), plus math/symbol fonts inline → mid-line high (−30).
- A legitimate multi-document PDF (cover letter + invoice merged, or a bank's yearly statement pack): resource-level font enumeration across sources easily exceeds 7-10 'families' → diversity flags on untampered files.
- Text mentioning euro/degree/checkbox glyphs: generators frequently emit '€' or '✓' from a fallback/symbol font in the middle of a Helvetica line → mid-line change flag on standard French invoices.
- If/when QR scanning is re-enabled: a legitimate EDF/Orange invoice whose QR points to a payment-processor domain (payline.com, lyf.eu) or a campaign domain (edf-facture.fr) that differs from the text domains → VISUAL_QR_DOMAIN_MISMATCH critical (−50); worse, TLD + mismatch can stack to −65 on one legitimate QR.
- Emphasis typography: an amount set in 'Arial Black' next to 'Arial' text — split-on-hyphen keeps 'arial black' ≠ 'arial'... conversely 'Arial-BoldMT' vs 'ArialMT' become different families in the diversity count — both directions mis-normalize on ordinary bold emphasis.

**False Negative Gaps:**
- QR-code fraud passes entirely: a fake invoice with a QR pointing to a phishing payment page is never examined (PYZBAR_AVAILABLE=False), yet the module reports confidence 0.9 — the exact attack class the module was designed for is invisible.
- SPECIMEN/COPY/VOID documents pass: check_watermarks is never called, so a bank 'SPECIMEN' RIB or a 'DUPLICATA' attestation presented as an original gets zero flags from this module.
- Graphical watermarks are invisible: real-world watermarks (rotated diagonal vector text, image stamps, trial-tool overlays drawn as graphics) don't appear in get_text output; there is no page-render + OCR path (pytesseract is in the stack but unused here), so only watermarks that exist as selectable text are detectable.
- Scanned/flattened documents: extractor has no OCR fallback (pdf_extractor.py:208), so a screenshot-of-invoice PDF — the cheapest tamper-laundering technique — yields no text for the visual module (score 100, conf 0.9) and no fonts for the fonts module (score 100, conf 0.3).
- Whole-file regeneration laundering: attacker edits the PDF then re-exports via macOS Preview / Chrome print-to-PDF / a watermark-free converter — fonts become one consistent regenerated set, no converter text, no mid-line anomaly → both modules return ~100 and actively boost the document's trust score.
- Same-family edits: a tamper tool that re-embeds text in the same family (GHIJKL+ArialMT next to ABCDEF+ArialMT, or matching family with wrong size/weight/color) passes the mid-line check because only the normalized family is compared (fonts.py:444-467); size/color/flags in the span dict are never used.
- Bitmap patch-over: pasting a screenshot of a modified amount over the original text produces zero font anomalies — nothing in these modules examines overlap of images with text (delegated to images module, but fonts still reports a clean 100/0.9 that dilutes the images module's penalty in the weighted average).
- FreeText-annotation overlays: text added as an annotation (a one-click Acrobat/Preview edit) isn't in the default get_text output nor in page content fonts, so mid-line and diversity checks never see it.
- Non-embedded-font tampering is undetectable due to the ext='n/a' truthiness bug (fonts.py:128) — an edit that references a local system font without embedding (classic amateur-editor artifact) never triggers FONTS_NOT_EMBEDDED.
- CamScanner and similar scan-app watermarks ('Scanned with CamScanner' — extremely common on re-photographed forged documents) are absent from CONVERTER_WATERMARKS.
- When QR returns: attacker prints their phishing domain in the invoice footer text; extract_sender_domains adopts it as 'expected', so the QR pointing to the same phishing domain passes the mismatch check — the trust anchor is attacker-controlled. Punycode/homoglyph domains (xn--) are also never normalized or flagged.
- CID-font evasion: the mid-line check discards all CIDFont+F* names (fonts.py:439-440, 463-466); a generator/editor that names everything CIDFont+F1..Fn (common in some print drivers and some tamper tools) is fully exempt from mid-line detection.

**Improvement Ideas:**
- Re-enable QR scanning without the libzbar system dependency by using OpenCV's cv2.QRCodeDetector (opencv-python is already a project dependency and runs fully locally) on page pixmaps; keep pyzbar as an optional backend. Raise render zoom adaptively (2x→4x retry) when detection fails on pages containing small images.
- Fix the embedding check: treat ext == 'n/a' (and empty) as not embedded, or verify via doc.extract_font(xref); then keep FONTS_NOT_EMBEDDED as a real low-severity signal and add a positive trust signal when all non-base-14 fonts are properly embedded subsets from a single generator.
- Rebuild mid-line detection on span attributes, not just family: compare (family, size rounded, color, flags) per span; whitelist symbol/dingbat/bullet fonts and single-glyph spans (€, •, ✓); add a metric-clone alias table (Nimbus↔Helvetica, Liberation↔Arial/Times/Courier, TeX Gyre, DejaVu) shared between diversity and mid-line checks; hoist FAMILY_ALIASES/get_family to module level.
- Invert mid-line severity logic: weight by WHAT changed, not how many lines — a family/size change inside a span sequence containing an amount, date, IBAN, or reference number (reuse content.py regexes) should be high even for a single line; document-wide repeated two-font patterns should be recognized as layout style and suppressed (if >N% of lines show the same font pair, treat it as design, not tampering).
- Restore status-watermark detection with layout qualification instead of deletion: use get_text('dict') span data — a SPECIMEN/COPY string in a span with very large font size, non-black/transparent color, or on a rotated line (dir != (1,0)) is a watermark; the same word at body-text size is prose. This recovers the signal that was disabled at visual.py:431-435.
- Add a rendered-page OCR fallback (pytesseract already in stack) for pages with empty text layers, so watermark and converter checks work on scanned/flattened PDFs, and lower module confidence when a page has no text layer instead of reporting 0.9/100.
- Deduplicate flags before scoring: collapse multiple converter-pattern matches on the same matched text into one flag; add `continue` after the suspicious-TLD QR flag; cap total penalty per flag code so one artifact can't be charged twice.
- Stop auto-trusting document-derived domains for the critical QR mismatch: exclude free-mail providers, require the domain to appear in a sender context (near SIRET/TVA/company header), cross-check against templates/index.json domain lists, and downgrade auto-detected mismatches from critical to medium (reserve critical for template-confirmed senders).
- Count font diversity from actual rendered spans (get_text('dict')) rather than page resource dictionaries, apply the shared family normalization, and make thresholds document-type aware (the analyzer already computes doc_type) instead of the hardcoded 7/10.
- Add error containment per check: wrap each check in try/except inside analyze_fonts/analyze_visual, put doc.close() in finally, and on extraction failure return a degraded-confidence result that records an ANALYSIS_INCOMPLETE flag instead of a silent perfect 100 — a PDF that PyMuPDF cannot parse should never score better than one it can.
- Make confidence honest: visual confidence should reflect which sub-checks actually ran (QR disabled → cap at ~0.5; no text layer → lower further); this directly serves the project's goal of confirming legitimate files, because high confidence should mean 'we genuinely looked'.
- Extend CONVERTER_WATERMARKS (camscanner, pdffiller, dochub, pdfescape, 'scanned by', foxit trial banners) with word boundaries, and add an allowlist of legitimate billing-platform phrases ('generated by stripe/chargebee/quickbooks/sage/odoo') that either suppress or downgrade the generic 'generated by' pattern to low.
- Include annotation text in analysis (get_text with TEXTFLAGS including annotations, or iterate page.annots()) so FreeText overlay edits — a one-click tamper vector — become visible to both watermark and font checks.
- Centralize SEVERITY_POINTS in scoring.py / models.py and import it, eliminating the per-module copies; while there, remove the dead document_text parameter (visual.py:157) and the unused FontInfo.usage_count/pages_used fields or start using them (e.g., 'font used on exactly one page of a multi-page doc' is itself a useful edit signal).

---


## Part 2 — Local-technique research

### RESEARCH — Completeness critique: forgery techniques and legitimacy signals missing from TrustyFile's candidate detection list (local-only, CPU)

**Key insights:**
- The single biggest hole in the candidate list is synthetic-document detection: every listed image-forensics technique (ELA, JPEG ghost, copy-move, noise, resampling) assumes a REAL base image with a LOCAL edit. The 2025-2026 attack wave — GPT-4o-generated receipts (March 2025) and OnlyFake-style template/AI ID images — produces documents with no edit seam at all, and commercial detectors were already shown failing on them (26.8% detection reported). At minimum, add the AI-image detector + glyph-consistency + C2PA trio.
- Identity documents are almost entirely uncovered by the candidate list despite being a declared TrustyFile document class with an existing (audited, gap-ridden) identity module: ICAO 9303 MRZ check digits, MRZ↔visual-zone↔2D-Doc cross-comparison, specimen blocklists, and portrait-region forensics are all missing. The MRZ checksum + cross-zone checks are the highest signal-per-effort items in this entire report (small effort, near-deterministic).
- Two laundering paths destroy the evidence base of ~80% of the listed techniques: full PDF rewrite (kills all metadata/structure/font forensics) and print-and-rescan or screenshot (kills all digital history). The counters are not more tamper detectors but capture-channel authentication (is this really a scan?) plus an aggregation rule the list only implies: a document whose entire history has been laundered must be scored as 'unverifiable', never as 'clean' — absence of evidence is the attacker's cheapest product.
- The list has known-GOOD databases (producers, templates) but no known-BAD counterpart: fake receipt/invoice generator sites and OnlyFake-style template kits produce high-volume, layout-identical fraud that a small local pHash/producer-string blocklist catches at near-zero FP.
- Several cheap, audit-confirmed signals need no research at all and are absent from the list: fitz is_repaired/is_encrypted/is_pdf flags, annotation-overlay enumeration (FreeText over amounts, hidden /F flags, render-with/without-annots diff), and per-page provenance outliers for spliced multi-page documents. These are days, not weeks, of work.
- One near-miss rather than a gap: graphical SPECIMEN/DUPLICATA/CamScanner watermarks (invisible to text extraction) don't need a new technique — the already-listed render+OCR cross-validation pass covers them if its OCR output is also matched against the watermark keyword lists, which the implementation should make explicit.
- For C2PA and the AI-image detector alike, the scoring must stay asymmetric to protect the false-positive goal: presence of AI-origin evidence or broken cryptographic bindings is decisive, but absence of C2PA/EXIF/history must remain neutral — millions of legitimate documents have none.

**Techniques:**
- **AI-generated whole-document-image detection (diffusion/GAN synthesis detector)** (effort: large)
  - _how:_ The dominant new attack (March 2025 onward) is generating the entire receipt/invoice/ID as an image with GPT-4o-class models or services like OnlyFake — no editing traces exist because nothing was edited. Detection runs a synthetic-image classifier over rasterized pages and embedded images: frequency-domain residual analysis (Synthbuster-style cross-difference filter for periodic diffusion artifacts), patch-level classifiers with limited receptive fields (PatchForensics/AIDE-style low-level inconsistency scoring), and bit-plane/entropy features (LOTA, MLEP). For documents specifically, combine with document-domain tells: AI images have no genuine JPEG quantization history coherent with a camera/scanner, no EXIF, and text rendered as 'texture' rather than consistent glyphs.
  - _signal:_ Catches the fastest-growing fraud class (fake receipts for expense/reimbursement, fake attestations, fake ID scans) that every listed technique misses — ELA/copy-move/noise checks all assume a real base image with a local edit. Signal strength is moderate: generalization to unseen generators is the known weakness, so treat as one weak-to-medium signal fused with glyph-consistency (below). FP risk moderate on heavily recompressed or filtered legitimate photos; keep severity at medium unless corroborated.
  - _local:_ Feasible. Frequency/residual heuristics are pure numpy+OpenCV (opencv-python, numpy, scipy). A small patch classifier exported to INT8 ONNX runs on onnxruntime CPU in <1s/page with ~200-500MB RAM — fits a small cloud box. No external calls; model weights shipped with the app.
  - _sources:_ https://techcrunch.com/2025/03/31/chatgpts-new-image-generator-is-really-good-at-faking-receipts/, https://resistant.ai/blog/chatgpt-document-fraud, https://arxiv.org/pdf/2402.01123 (A Single Simple Patch is All You Need for AI-generated Image Detection), https://arxiv.org/pdf/2504.13726 (MLEP: Multi-granularity Local Entropy Patterns)
- **Glyph raster self-consistency check (same-character clustering)** (effort: medium)
  - _how:_ OCR the rasterized page with per-character bounding boxes (pytesseract TSV output), crop every instance of the same character at similar point size, normalize scale/binarize, and measure pairwise similarity (NCC/SSIM) within each character cluster. Digitally-born documents render identical glyphs pixel-identically; genuine scans show one consistent glyph shape plus uniform scanner noise; AI-generated images render each instance of 'e' or '7' slightly differently (shape variance across the cluster); a spliced/retyped amount shows up as an outlier instance whose shape or anti-aliasing profile deviates from its siblings.
  - _signal:_ Dual-purpose: (a) whole-cluster high variance = AI-generated image (complements the synthesis detector with a document-specific, generator-agnostic signal); (b) single-outlier instance = character substitution in a scan — exactly the Find it!/DocTamper attack class. Strong signal on receipts and typed invoices; FP risk from OCR box misalignment and very low-resolution scans, so gate on DPI and OCR confidence.
  - _local:_ Fully local: pytesseract (tesseract-ocr + tesseract-ocr-fra already in packages.txt scope), opencv-python, numpy, scikit-learn for clustering. CPU cost is one OCR pass per page (~2-5s, already budgeted for the OCR fallback) plus milliseconds of correlation. Low RAM.
  - _sources:_ https://l3i-share.univ-lr.fr/2023Finditagain/index.html (Find it again! receipt forgery dataset, ICDAR 2023), https://arxiv.org/pdf/2009.05158 (OCR Graph Features for Manipulation Detection in Documents), http://iwcdf2017.univ-lr.fr/wp-content/uploads/2017/11/IWCDF2017_Artaud_1.pdf (Receipt Dataset for Fraud Detection)
- **C2PA / Content Credentials manifest verification** (effort: small)
  - _how:_ Parse and cryptographically verify C2PA manifests embedded in uploaded images and PDFs using the c2pa-python library (binding to the Rust c2pa-rs SDK), with a locally cached trust list of signer certificates. Three outcomes matter: (1) a manifest asserting an AI generator (OpenAI signs GPT-4o images with C2PA metadata) is a near-decisive fraud signal for a document presented as a real receipt/scan; (2) a valid manifest from a camera/scanner/Adobe chain whose hard-binding hash still matches is a strong positive provenance signal; (3) a present-but-broken hash binding proves post-signing modification.
  - _signal:_ Asymmetric: presence of an AI-origin assertion or a broken binding is high-severity and near-zero-FP (cryptographic); absence is neutral (metadata is trivially stripped by screenshotting/re-encoding) and must NOT be penalized. This is the cheapest possible check against lazy GPT-4o receipt fraud and a genuine positive-trust signal the candidate list lacks entirely.
  - _local:_ pip install c2pa-python (prebuilt wheels, Rust core, no system deps). Verification is offline once trust-anchor certificates are cached locally; only content integrity + signature chain math, no network. Negligible CPU/RAM.
